# Arxiv Papers in cs.CV on 2026-11-25
### EgoCARBS: Egocentric Video Meets Active Acoustics for Kitchen Action Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33565v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.HC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33565v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 00:26:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 00:26:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Saif Mahmud, Vineet Parikh, Bharath Hariharan, Cheng Zhang
- **Comment**: 36 pages (12 main paper and references, 24 supplementary material). Project page: https://saif-mahmud.github.io/projects/ego-carbs
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Egocentric video records what a person looks at, but often misses what their body does: the hands leave the frame, and arm motion goes unseen. An exocentric camera recovers this context, but it must be installed wherever the activity happens. Active acoustic sensing can recover it from the wearables themselves: a device's speaker emits inaudible chirps, and its microphone senses the motion of the hands, arms, and nearby objects from their echoes. Yet no public dataset pairs it with egocentric video. We present EgoCARBS, with two deliverables. The first is a multimodal dataset and benchmark: 15 participants cook unscripted meals in their own kitchens wearing camera glasses and an off-the-shelf smartwatch that both sense with active acoustics, synchronized with egocentric and exocentric video and the watch's audio and IMU, with 4,019 verb-noun action annotations in 11.4 hours. The second is a study of what the wearables add to egocentric video. On verb recognition, they add at least as much as an exocentric camera for participants seen in training, and even more for new participants, for whom the exocentric camera does not help. Active acoustics alone provides most of this gain, and helps most where the camera does not see the hands. Trimmed action recognition, detection, and anticipation remain open. We will release the data, our data collection and annotation tools, and pre-trained models.



### ProPolar: Progressive Polar Decomposition for Implicit Neural Representations
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33567v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33567v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 00:30:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 00:30:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Pureum Kim, Younggeon Ryu, Dongyoon Lee, Hae Beom Lee, Kyong Hwan Jin
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Implicit neural representations (INRs) capture global low-frequency structure during the early stage of training and then refine localized high-frequency details, afterwards. However, standard optimizers are agnostic to the coarse-to-fine learning behavior of INRs. Such optimizers accumulate a gradient matrix in momentum buffer and retain components misaligned with the dominant low-frequency directions in the early stage. We propose a progressively growing rank scheduler and a rank-aware learning-rate scheduler for enhancing a subspace-based momentum optimization, where a momentum matrix is updated within a low-rank subspace. The rank schedule expands the subspace during training, and the scheduler holds the peak learning rate through rank growth so that the added subspace contributes to fine-detail recovery. Experiments on image fitting, single-image super-resolution (SISR), and neural radiance field optimization show that the proposed method improves convergence and peak reconstruction quality over competitive optimizers. The largest gains appear in the high-frequency refinement stage, mirroring the coarse-to-fine progression that the rank schedule targets. A significant study on hyperparameter optimization (HPO) on NeRF further confirms that these gains persist under matched search budgets.



### DoseSteer: Steering diffusion-based dose prediction models towards clinical targets via Dual-Response Guidance
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33577v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33577v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 00:42:24+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 00:42:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Shupeng Cheng, Jing Jia, Jincheng Wu, Zhangyu Wang, Gao Wang, Maohe Jiang, Chi Xu, Feiyang Wang, Zhilei Liu Shen, Guanyang Wang, Ruishan Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: AI dose-prediction models generate patient-specific 3D dose distributions from patient anatomy. Yet these predictions can still miss structure-level clinical dose targets. Once deployed, most existing predictors offer no direct mechanism to correct such deviations, deferring target enforcement to downstream plan optimization. We introduce DoseSteer, an inference-time framework that steers frozen diffusion-based dose prediction models towards specified clinical targets without retraining. To meet clinical targets while preserving overall dose prediction quality, we propose Dual-Response Guidance (DRG), a structure-centric algorithm that probes the model's responses to perturbations in each structure's noisy dose and context and jointly solves for per-structure steering strengths. Across five dataset-model configurations spanning three diffusion architectures and three datasets, DRG outperforms 12 training-free baselines under identical reference-derived target proxies, removing at least 97% of the target error, reducing the official dose error by 7.8% on average, and improving dose fidelity outside the steered structures. DoseSteer reconciles learned anatomical dose priors with explicit structure-level targets at test time, establishing a principled, training-free mechanism to refine AI dose predictions prior to plan optimization. Code is available at https://github.com/csp2002/DoseSteer.



### LLMVC: Large Language Model-Guided Generative Video Compression
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33580v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33580v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 00:46:44+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 00:46:44+00:00
- **Authors**: Lebin Zhou, Zhenghao Chen, Nam Ling, Wei Wang, Wei Jiang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent generative video methods achieve strong perceptual compression by encoding videos into compact latent representations and reconstructing them with powerful generative decoders. However, visual encoders must compress both high-level semantics and dense visual details into a unified latent space, which complicates entropy coding and decoding and may cause semantic drift under aggressive compression. In this work, we propose Large Language Model-Guided Video Compression (LLMVC), which leverages the summarization capability of frozen video large language models (Video-LLMs) to generate compact semantic tokens aligned with temporal latent slices for guiding video compression. Specifically, Hybrid Bidirectional Bimodal Attention (HBBA) enables bidirectional interactions between visual and semantic features during encoding and decoding. Semantic-Conditioned Cross-Attention (SCCA) incorporates semantic features into probability modeling during entropy coding. These semantic features provide high-level guidance throughout the pipeline, improving representation quality and encouraging semantic consistency through a transmitted text side channel. Experiments on UVG, MCL-JCV, and HEVC show that LLMVC consistently improves compression performance over traditional and recent neural video compression methods in terms of LPIPS and DISTS, while also achieving better fidelity than recent perceptual codecs.



### SEEL: Towards End-to-End 3D Sign Generation with Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33588v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33588v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 00:58:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 00:58:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Ronglai Zuo, Fei Ni, Junpeng Jing, Rolandos Alexandros Potamias, Jiankang Deng, Stefanos Zafeiriou
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D sign language generation aims to produce signing motion from written text. Motivated by the linguistic nature of sign language, recent methods combine a motion tokenizer with a language model to predict motion tokens from text. However, both components are optimized through a two-stage pipeline with mismatched objectives: the tokenizer minimizes reconstruction error in isolation, while the generator learns from rigid one-hot reference tokens. A straightforward end-to-end extension bridges discrete token selection with straight-through estimators (STE), suffering from biased gradients and codebook collapse. To address these limitations, we propose SEEL, an end-to-end framework that aligns tokenization and generation with motion quality through two complementary mechanisms. First, to overcome rigid one-hot supervision, reconstruction-aware generation decodes candidate tokens in context and converts their 3D motion errors into soft targets, guiding the generator with physical motion quality. Second, to propagate generation feedback without STE, generation-aware tokenization passes continuous codebook expectations through the frozen generator, routing generated-motion gradients to the tokenizer to prevent codebook collapse. Combining both mechanisms enables the tokenizer and generator to co-evolve, achieving state-of-the-art performance on CSL-Daily, How2Sign, and Phoenix-2014T. Code and models will be released to facilitate future research.



### VivaWorld: Long-Horizon Video World Model With Compositional 3D Priors
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33591v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33591v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 01:00:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 01:00:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Ying Jiang, Donglai Xiang, Anita Hu, Masha Shugrina
- **Comment**: 17 figures, 10 tables, 26 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long-horizon video generation is key to interactive world modeling, physical intelligence, and spatial reasoning, yet maintaining geometric consistency and accurate camera control remains challenging without 3D grounding. We introduce \textbf{VivaWorld}, a framework for generating geometry- and view-consistent long videos from an image. We first construct a compositional hybrid 3D representation that combines reconstructed point clouds, which preserve observed scene geometry and appearance, with generated textured meshes, which provide complete object geometry across $360^\circ$ novel views. We also introduce a connected-region filter removing inconsistent floating components for robust cross-view reconstruction and propose a hybrid camera embedding that combines global and object-anchored Plücker rays to capture both scene-level camera motion and camera-object relationships, and inject it together with the visual guidance into a video generation model via a control adapter. Extensive experiments show VivaWorld achieves state-of-the-art visual quality and camera control, while supporting controllable novel-view synthesis, long-horizon scene exploration, and physical AI. Project page is available at: https://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/vivaworld/



### Learning from Natural Images Untangles Number Sense
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33615v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CG, cs.CV, cs.LG, q-bio.NC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33615v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 01:41:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 01:41:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Yechan Cho, Hyeonsu Lee, Emma Deneuville, Jaeson Jang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Numerical cognition is a simple yet fundamental ability that remains challenging for modern vision-language models. Meanwhile, number sense is already present in numerically naïve animals, suggesting that it can emerge without explicit numerical learning. Deep neural networks provide a related observation, with numerosity-selective units appearing after training on unrelated natural-image tasks and even before any training. However, these findings leave unresolved whether visual learning contributes beyond the spontaneous emergence of numerical selectivity. Human development provides a clue, as children become better at making judgments in which numerosity and proportion conflict. Here, we introduce a geometric framework to trace how representations of these two quantities change across learning. Although untrained and pretrained networks exhibited similar unit-level numerosity selectivity and basic comparison performance, natural-image pretraining increased the angle between numerosity and proportion subspaces while reducing their overlap, supporting more robust readout under conflict. Direct manipulation of these geometric properties further demonstrated their functional contributions to decoding accuracy and functional independence. Importantly, conventional supervised pretraining was not essential for quantity separation, which also emerged through self-supervised learning without category labels and synthetic-image training without natural-image exposure. Together, our findings suggest that structured visual learning can refine spontaneously emerging number sense into robust and functionally independent quantity representations without explicit numerical training.



### Greedy Decoding Hides Good Plans: Sample-and-Verify Planning for Vision-Language Driving Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33621v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33621v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 01:52:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 01:52:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhipeng Bao, Wenjie Zhao, Yiping Li, Haohua Que, Qianwen Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly adopted for end-to-end driving planning, yet their performance is typically evaluated based on a single greedily decoded trajectory. However, greedy decoding may overlook better trajectories within the model's output distribution. Experiments on WOD-E2E and NAVSIM reveal a consistent performance gap between greedily decoded trajectories and the best sampled candidates, showing that planning errors can arise from trajectory selection, rather than trajectory generation alone. On WOD-E2E, when the ego vehicle should start moving, the model often assigns probability to different possible start times, causing greedy decoding to repeatedly favor waiting and produce a stationary plan even though moving trajectories are available. Motivated by these findings, we propose Sample-and-Verify (SaV), which keeps the VLM planner frozen and samples multiple candidate trajectories. A lightweight verifier is trained from ground-truth futures to estimate the relative quality of these candidates. It uses trajectory kinematics, candidate-set agreement and likelihood, and ego-motion features to select the final trajectory. On WOD-E2E, SaV reduces ADE and FDE by approximately 15% across three VLM planners on the validation set. Changes in RFS are smaller: two planners show slight decreases on val-456, while Alpamayo-1.5 improves from 7.74 to 7.81 RFS on the official test set as ADE@5s decreases by 11%. On NAVSIM, SaV reduces ADE@4s by 10-24% across three VLM planners, while PDMS changes are less consistent. These results show that trajectory selection can substantially improve displacement accuracy without additional planner training. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/hzxbzp/sample-and-verify.



### Interpreting Speckle Effects Through Neuron Interventions
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33632v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33632v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:01:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:01:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Shubham K. Singh, Hare Krishna
- **Comment**: 17 Pages 7 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Speckle, the multiplicative noise inherent to synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imaging, reduces the performance of pretrained SAR models. We use interpretability methods to examine its effects inside pretrained vision models. We identify neurons that respond selectively to speckle and test whether correcting their activations improves reconstruction. We study SARMAE, an Earth observation model pretrained on a million-scale SAR dataset. Synthetic scenes with known reflectivity allow us to measure recovery precisely. The model contains 38 speckle-selective neurons that track speckle's value distribution. Restoring these neurons to their clean values does not improve reconstruction. Activation patching across layers shows that the first MLP has the largest restoration and injection effects. Within this layer, a different set of 64 neurons recovers 44.1\% of the correlation loss on held-out scenes. When the speckle-selective neurons are held at their noisy values, 96.5\% of this gain remains, while holding the values of three decoder heads removes 37.4\%. In an ImageNet-pretrained MAE, restoration is most effective in the third MLP block. On manipulated Sentinel-1 and Capella images, the same 64 neurons also improve reconstruction, whereas for land-cover classification, only restoring the entire first MLP yields a clear gain. The neurons that respond most to speckle are therefore not the ones the model uses to recover the scene.



### What Is Redundant at High Resolution? Efficient Attention and Feed-Forward Design for All-in-One Image Restoration
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33636v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33636v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:04:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:04:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Dongheon Lee, DongJae Lee, Sewhan Chun, Chanseul Cho, Youngwook Kim, Youngmin Ro, Yonghyun Jeong
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multi-scale architectures are widely adopted in all-in-one image restoration (AIO-IR) to capture larger-scale context at low-resolution stages and refine details at high-resolution (HR) stages. However, processing spatially large feature maps at HR stages incurs substantial computational costs and memory traffic, making efficient HR processing challenging. To address this challenge, we propose High-resolution Global-Local Restoration (HiGLR), an efficient multi-scale AIO-IR network grounded in an analysis of spatial redundancy in self-attention and convolutional feed-forward networks (ConvFFNs). Specifically, this analysis identifies three key redundancies: per-query readout at the finest-scale stage, key routing across neighboring queries at intermediate stages, and spatial mixing in the expanded feature spaces of ConvFFNs. Accordingly, HiGLR replaces costly self-attention at the finest-scale stage with a learned input-independent kernel. At intermediate stages, we propose a Block-Routed Hybrid Attention, which combines coarse-scale dense attention with fine-scale block-sparse attention over value residuals relative to coarse summaries, and shares routing indices across neighboring queries. We further introduce a Dual-Space FFN that performs spatial mixing in a compact space and pointwise nonlinear transformation in an expanded space, enabling efficient kernel fusion. Combining these designs, HiGLR achieves state-of-the-art performance while delivering up to a $4.24\times$ inference speedup over existing methods.



### CariesBench: A Hierarchical Multi-view Benchmark for Dental Caries Screening and Diagnostic Reasoning from 3D Intraoral Scans
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33643v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33643v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:11:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:11:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Xuefen Liu,  Yanxiong, Xinquan Yang, Fangqian Zhang, Qinyi Lu, Yuning Zhang, Xianxu Hou, Shinan Zhang, Wen Ma, Linlin Shen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Dental caries assessment is a structured clinical process requiring lesion localization, identification of the affected tooth and surface, and severity grading rather than simple image-level recognition. 3D intraoral scans (IOS) preserve detailed dental geometry and surface morphology and support standardized rendering from complementary viewpoints, enabling fine-grained visual assessment while introducing a distinct need for anatomically grounded multi-view reasoning. Despite recent advances in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) and dental vision-language benchmarks, existing evaluations do not jointly assess hierarchical arch-to-tooth-to-surface reasoning, cross-view evidence integration, and surface-level caries assessment. To address this gap, we introduce CariesBench, a hierarchical benchmark constructed from 5,932 3D IOS, comprising 85,455 standardized multi-view renderings and 133,994 clinically grounded visual question answering instances with expert quality control. CariesBench comprises three complementary task families: Arch-level Clinical Screening (ACS), Tooth-level Diagnostic Reasoning (TDR), and Structured Clinical Decision-Making (SCD). Using the CariesBench training set, we further fine-tune Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct to obtain IntraoralGPT. Extensive evaluation of 24 representative baseline MLLMs reveals a substantial benchmark-level performance disparity between arch-level screening and tooth-level diagnostic reasoning, with higher accuracy on ACS than on TDR. CariesBench-specific supervision substantially improves the Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct baseline, increasing the 19-task macro-average clinical accuracy from 52.7% to 69.5% and TDR Overall from 34.4% to 44.7%.



### Structure-Aware Multimodal Retrieval Under Data Scarcity, Privacy Constraints and Out-of-Domain Content
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33649v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.IR**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33649v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:16:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:16:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Argyrios Zafeiriou, Dionysios Koulouris, Ilias Maglogiannis
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Purpose: Figure retrieval from reference atlases in expert domains like forensic pathology, dentistry and geology must work without labels or training pairs, on content that often cannot leave local hardware, and on images outside the training data of image-text encoders. This work proposes a structure-aware pipeline that satisfies all three, retrieving figures through the page structure recovered by layout geometry and a local open-weight 8B VLM. Methods: A geometry-based pairing method is introduced, in which the model receives a schematic of the detected boxes and selects each picture's caption without access to pixels or text. Caption retrieval is evaluated on three private corpora (29 books, 10,421 figures, 218 queries) with blind human grading, against a lexical baseline, generated descriptions and five image-text encoders. Results: Geometric pairing was correct for 96%, 92% and 77% of pictures in the three domains, against 76%, 45% and 53% for the better of two document parsers. A hosted model given the schematic was equally accurate, while coordinates as text lost 22 points. Caption retrieval obtained an nDCG@5 of 0.85, 0.87 and 0.76, against 0.54, 0.51 and 0.27 for the best cross-modal encoder and 0.41, 0.28 and 0.30 for generated descriptions. Pairing errors carried over to retrieval. Conclusion: The proposed pipeline achieves accurate figure retrieval with small local models and no in-domain training, where general and biomedical image-text encoders fall far behind. Its geometry-only pairing is the most accurate of five methods and exposes no content, even to a hosted model. Code and prompts are released.



### Linear-Time Online Visual Geometry Transformer with Hybrid Architecture
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33650v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33650v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:17:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:17:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Haoyu Kang, Pingzhi Li, Ruichen Zhang, Tianlong Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Transformer-based 3D reconstruction models such as VGGT and $π^3$ predict cameras, depth and point maps directly from many input views. Causal attention models such as StreamVGGT extend them to online video but cache all past tokens, so their total compute grows quadratically with the stream length. Recurrent models such as CUT3R and TTT3R keep a fixed-size state at constant cost, but they progressively forget early geometry. We present LinearVGGT, a hybrid model distilled from StreamVGGT, which uses Mamba2 layers for efficient sequence modeling in three of every four global layers. The remaining layers use register-guided sparse attention to retrieve a few relevant past frames, compensating for the limited exact long-range recall of Mamba2. Our distillation needs only 9\% of the GPU hours and 31\% of the training frames that StreamVGGT uses to learn from VGGT. LinearVGGT keeps an almost constant latency of 27 ms per frame over 1,024 frames and is on average $12 \times$ faster than StreamVGGT. It outperforms its teacher and is competitive with the state-of-the-art streaming model LingBot-Map, while running $2.5\times$ faster and using $2.7\times$ less memory.



### Fine-Grained Ego-Vehicle Action Recognition from Driving Video
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33654v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33654v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:20:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:20:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhipeng Bao, Wenjie Zhao, Qianwen Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Fine-grained ego-vehicle action recognition turns large-scale driving video into structured, searchable driving scenarios, providing a foundation for discovering critical maneuvers and systematically developing and evaluating autonomous driving models. However, large-scale driving videos often lack explicit ego-action labels, and existing datasets vary in action definitions, annotation granularity, and available supervision. We introduce EgoManeuver, a unified benchmark of 16 lateral and 6 longitudinal actions constructed from seven public driving datasets. Our multi-source labeling system leverages existing annotations and complementary evidence available in each source dataset, including vehicle signals, ego poses, HD-map topology, and captions, to assign labels under a unified fine-grained action taxonomy. We further standardize temporal action boundaries across datasets and validate the resulting labels through kinematic consistency checks and targeted human review. The resulting benchmark contains 86.6K segments spanning 190.8 h across 26 countries and provides a long-tail-aware evaluation protocol. We also introduce DenseEgo, a video-based baseline that combines spatially aware representations with dense per-step action supervision. On the EgoManeuver, DenseEgo achieves 82.7 lateral and 83.5 longitudinal mAP, outperforming the strongest existing video model by 1.5 and 5.0 points, respectively. Finally, we demonstrate one way to integrate DenseEgo with a language-model agent for retrieving driving videos that match user-specified actions and scenarios. All code, datasets, and model weights will be publicly released.



### Recall4D: Forgetting, Not Misalignment, Limits Long-Horizon Feed-Forward 4D Reconstruction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33655v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33655v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:22:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:22:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Tianle Lyu
- **Comment**: 20 pages, 10 figures, 15 tables. Project page: https://kincin.github.io/Recall4D/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Feed-forward 4D trackers recover camera motion, depth, and point trajectories from short video clips, but chaining windows over long videos degrades their accuracy. We find that replacing inter-window alignment with ground-truth transforms leaves tracking error essentially unchanged. Static-point error instead grows with time since last observation: repeatedly predicting a static point from windows that no longer observe it corrupts previously recovered geometry. We call this failure forgetting. Merely retaining the last observed positions of unseen points reduces error by 26% on PointOdyssey without modifying the tracker. We introduce Recall4D, a training-free framework that augments a frozen tracker with a persistent world state and sparse motion anchors. Static points retain their world-space positions outside the camera view and are refined by averaging observations decoded at sparse keyframes while in view. For dynamic objects, the tracker decodes sparse representative anchors, while geometric skinning propagates their displacements to dense queries. Across two long-horizon benchmarks, Recall4D improves the accuracy of its backbone while running 7.6-9.0$\times$ faster, and its accuracy gains carry over to five further backbones and three other long-sequence tasks, demonstrating its generality.



### Look at What Matters: Learning Lesion Prototypes from Severity Labels for Domain-Generalized Diabetic Retinopathy Grading
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33671v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33671v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:33:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:33:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziyang Zhang, Juming Xiong, Yanfan Zhu, Yuechen Yang, Junchao Zhu, Zhengyi Lu, Chongyu Qu, Yiming Zhong, Yalin Zheng, Yuankai Huo, He Zhao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Deep learning models for diabetic retinopathy (DR) grading often generalize poorly to unseen imaging domains, in part because predictions may exploit correlations specific to source datasets rather than retinal pathology. We address this challenge by constructing grading decisions from lesion evidence learned using only image severity labels. Clinical implications of DR grades provide multiple instance constraints that guide competitive learning of lesion and background prototypes at multiple scales. An additive ordinal scoring model then predicts severity exclusively from individual lesion prototype responses, explicitly connecting learned evidence with grading decisions without a global feature classification pathway. To improve the reliability of this evidence, we introduce teacher guided preservation under image degradations and spatial regularization against persistent coordinate preferences. Across six public DR datasets under a leave one domain out protocol, our method attains a mean quadratic weighted kappa of 0.761, outperforming the strongest competing method by 0.036. Localization and prototype removal analyses show that the learned responses align with retinal lesions and contribute to grading decisions. Experiments across five other prototype architectures and under image degradations further demonstrate the generality and robustness of the approach.



### MOVR-SAR: A Multi-resolution SAR Registration Dataset under Orbit-crossing and View-variant Imaging Differences
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33673v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33673v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:37:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:37:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuchi Pu, Bosong Qi, Pengcheng Shi, Jiayuan Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) image registration under varying acquisition geometries is challenging because orbit direction, look side, and incidence angle affect backscatter, terrain-induced distortion, and feature visibility. However, large-scale geometry-aware benchmarks remain scarce. This paper presents MOVR-SAR, a multi-resolution dataset of 450,845 registered and quality-screened tile pairs derived from 2774 scene pairs from Capella, Umbra, Sentinel-1, and NISAR. It covers X, C, and L bands, ground sampling distances of 0.25-20 m, and diverse regions and land-cover types. A unified framework defines four imaging-geometry pair categories from orbit direction and look side, plus viewing-angle-separation labels for Capella and Umbra, enabling stratified evaluation under same- and opposite-direction illumination. Systematic evaluation of representative matching methods shows that official and cross-modal pretrained weights perform poorly on high-resolution X-band imagery. Fine-tuning on MOVR-SAR improves accuracy and reduces disparities across platforms, imaging-geometry pair categories, and viewing-angle separations. For example, fine-tuning increases FlexGlue AUC@5 from 3.06% to 89.79% on Capella and from 1.79% to 89.03% on Umbra, reaching 93.41% and 94.77% on Sentinel-1 and NISAR, respectively. Fine-tuning substantially reduces the official-weight same-orbit, same-look-side advantage and reverses the three-method average category ranking on Capella, suggesting that this bias reflects training-data coverage rather than an invariant limitation of cross-geometry matching. Land-cover effects remain platform- and method-dependent. MOVR-SAR provides a training resource and geometry-aware benchmark for robust SAR-SAR registration. The dataset and model weights will be released at https://github.com/fdhzhz5870/MOVR-SAR.



### WorldUAV: Scaling UAV Data Synthesis via World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33682v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33682v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:47:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:47:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Xuewen Liu, Mengjuan Chen, Jing Zhang, Mengzhe Shen, Zekun Xu, Zibo Chen, Zuoxiu Zhang, Yue Dong, Zhikai Li, Qingyi Gu
- **Comment**: Project Page: https://bienluky.github.io/WorldUAV/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: UAV detection and tracking remain constrained by the scarcity of high-quality training data. Existing game-engine-based synthetic UAV data suffer from a substantial synthetic-to-real gap, while the high cost of manually collected real-world UAV data limits dataset scale. Therefore, we introduce WorldUAV, a high-quality UAV dataset constructed through an efficient framework powered by world models. Specifically, WorldUAV directly integrates UAVs into readily available real-world videos, naturally preserving realistic backgrounds, illumination, and scene dynamics. Leveraging the spatiotemporal priors of world models, it generates UAV videos with plausible motion, pose variations, and cross-frame consistency, effectively narrowing the gap between synthetic and real data. We further develop an agentic pipeline orchestrated by a multimodal large language model that acquires sources, plans UAV trajectories, invokes synthesis, and verifies generated videos, enabling fully automated dataset construction. WorldUAV comprises 6,878 UAV videos and 205,778 UAV images. Experiments show that WorldUAV significantly improves the performance of UAV detectors. Moreover, it substantially reduces dataset construction costs, requiring only 15 seconds and USD 0.0005 API cost per high-quality UAV image. Our work offers both a high-quality dataset and a scalable data generation paradigm for UAV detection and tracking.



### Read Before You Use: Improving Visual Memory Utilization in Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33687v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33687v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:53:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:53:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Junseok Kim, Kyungmin Min, Kyomin Jung
- **Comment**: 26 pages, 6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal memory enables long-horizon vision-language agents to preserve and retrieve past experiences. While prior research has focused on memory construction and retrieval, effectively using the provided memory remains a challenge for vision-language models (VLMs). We find that VLMs often fail to answer downstream questions even when sufficient multimodal evidence is provided. To investigate why VLMs struggle, we replace memory images with reference captions and observe substantial performance gains, suggesting difficulties in reading visual evidence directly. However, model-generated captions may omit critical information, limiting this approach in practice. These limitations motivate a closer examination of memory utilization, revealing failures in both evidence reading and downstream question answering. Building on this distinction, we propose \textbf{MemRU}, a two-stage training framework that improves evidence reading through supervised fine-tuning (Read SFT) and downstream evidence utilization through Group Relative Policy Optimization (Use GRPO). Experimental results demonstrate that our 8B model generalizes across three unseen multimodal memory systems, outperforming Qwen3-VL-32B and approaching GPT-5-mini.



### GeoTex: Geometry-as-Texture Encoding and History-Grounded 4D Occupancy Forecasting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33693v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33693v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 02:58:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 02:58:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Shijun Guo, Yaming Yang, Haoran Xu, Wei Zhao, Ziyu Guan, Cai Xu, Peixi Peng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 4D occupancy forecasting models 3D scene evolution to support future prediction and scenario generation in autonomous-driving world models. Semantic occupancy abstracts image color and texture into discrete labels, leaving 3D voxel arrangements as the primary cues for distinguishing local shapes within each class. Existing methods borrow image-encoding frameworks to obtain compact latent representations through height channelization or plane factorization, but do not sufficiently model within-column height patterns and cross-column relations jointly before compression, weakening these geometric cues. Forecasting also underutilizes complementary history and struggles to retain reliable observation evidence. We therefore propose GeoTex, an occupancy world model combining geometry-as-texture encoding with history-grounded forecasting. GeoTex Representation jointly encodes within-column height patterns and cross-column relations into compact continuous latent representations and restores 3D structure through content-dependent height unfolding and geometric interaction. GeoTex Prediction retains initial evidence throughout rollout in fixed cross-observation relational memory and incorporates complementary recent history through target-referenced residual updates. After prediction training, decoder-only adaptation improves semantic decoding of predicted states. GeoTex improves average forecasting mIoU and IoU by 9.79% and 11.91% relative to GenieDrive while reducing parameters from 3.47M to 2.33M, with gains increasing at longer horizons. Code is included in the supplementary material.



### Efficient Scene-Distribution-Aware Second-Order Optimization for 3D Gaussian Splatting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33698v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33698v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 03:07:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 03:07:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Hyunseung Son, Young Sun Choi, Youngsik Yun, Kibeom Hong, Youngjung Uh
- **Comment**: 16 pages, 2 figures, 5 tables. Accepted to ACCV 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) optimization updates the Gaussian parameters one training view at a time, tens of thousands of times. Unlike such single-view updates, multi-view batches reduce dependence on individual training views, and second-order optimization reduces the number of updates with larger, structured parameter changes. Because fewer updates leave fewer chances to correct a change that fits only the views used in one update, scene coverage becomes more important for each second-order update. Multi-view second-order updates, however, are expensive. Pixel sampling reduces this cost, but two issues remain. First, selecting views without considering overlap leaves some views redundant. To address this, we propose a view selection strategy that reduces this redundancy. Second, existing work processes the sampled pixels of each view in a separate pass and recomputes alpha compositing for these pixels from scratch. We introduce two implementation strategies that reduce both overheads by processing the sampled pixels of multiple views in joint passes and reusing the transmittance and accumulated color cached during rendering. On three datasets, our method reaches higher PSNR than the sampled second-order baseline in less than half its optimization time, with competitive SSIM and LPIPS.



### Nepali Currency Denomination Classification Using Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and MobileNetV2: An Experimental Comparison
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33713v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33713v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 03:27:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 03:27:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Sakar Lamichhane, Sandesh Bhatta, Aayush Adhikari, Aaron Sapkota
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Automated currency denomination recognition is a computer vision task with applications in cash handling, financial technology, and assistive systems. This study classifies seven Nepali currency denominations (Rs. 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 500, and 1,000) using three configurations: a conventional convolutional neural network (CNN), MobileNetV2 with transfer learning, and MobileNetV2 with hyperparameter tuning. A compiled dataset of 16,249 images was used, comprising 10,500 training, 3,500 validation, and 2,249 independent test images. Images were resized to 128 x 128 pixels, normalized, and augmented to vary orientation, scale, illumination, and appearance. Models were evaluated using accuracy, precision, recall, F1-score, and precision-recall and ROC analysis. The hyperparameter-tuned MobileNetV2 achieved the highest validation accuracy (95\%), with precision, recall, and F1-score of 0.93, 0.91, and 0.92, but reached only 84\% accuracy on the held-out test set. MobileNetV2 with transfer learning but no additional tuning achieved 89\% test accuracy, and the conventional CNN achieved 85\%. Transfer learning therefore provided a competitive approach under the evaluated conditions, while higher validation accuracy from tuning did not yield higher test accuracy. To demonstrate practical deployment, a Flask-based web prototype using TensorFlow Lite was developed, supporting image upload and live webcam recognition with both visual and voice output. The study shows the feasibility of combining lightweight deep learning with a voice-assisted web interface for Nepali currency recognition, and highlights the need for more diverse datasets, greater robustness under uncontrolled imaging conditions, and broader real-world evaluation.



### VTK-Bench: A Diagnostic Benchmark for Distinguishing Language Priors and Text Modal Bias in Visual Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33725v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33725v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 03:38:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 03:38:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Yao Lu, Yuhang Zheng, Yaxin Gao, Zhe Tang, Zhaowei Zhu, Jiaheng Wei, Yunhao Wang, Shanqing Yu, Qi Xuan
- **Comment**: 25 pages, 4 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual language models (VLMs) have demonstrated exceptional capabilities across various multimodal tasks, but they remain susceptible to hallucinations, undermining their reliability. Existing hallucination benchmarks primarily focus on identifying hallucinations and describing their manifestations, often neglecting their underlying causes. Specifically, multimodal hallucinations can stem from two distinct failure modes: text modality bias (TMB), reflecting an over-reliance on misleading text input; and language prior (LP), reflecting a reliance on previously learned knowledge. To differentiate these failure modes, we propose VTK-Bench, a benchmark that explicitly separates three information sources: visual information (V), textual information (T), and prior knowledge (K). By establishing mutually exclusive answers corresponding to V, T, and K, VTK-Bench enables fine-grained attribution analysis of model responses. Furthermore, we introduce discrete attribution and soft attribution metrics to characterize information source preferences. Extensive experiments on 11 representative open-source VLMs show that LP is more prevalent than TMB in most evaluated models. Notably, TMB decreases substantially with model scaling, whereas LP exhibits relatively limited improvement and remains the dominant failure mode. These findings highlight the importance of different information source preferences for a deeper understanding of multimodal hallucinations.



### DualCast: Dual-Image Conditioned Diffusion Transformers for Digital Human E-Commerce Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33734v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33734v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 03:54:18+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 03:54:18+00:00
- **Authors**: Lifan Jiang, Yifan Huang, Jixuan Zhang, Peifeng Wang, Yuying Ma, Dehai Zhao, Deng Cai, Boxi Wu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent advances in world models for game interaction and control have increased the demand for large-scale gameplay videos paired with camera trajectories. However, capturing synchronized camera trajectories often requires game-specific pipelines, limiting scalability. We therefore propose GameWeaver, a unified diffusion framework that automatically produces paired video--camera data through three complementary tasks: camera pose estimation from observed video, video generation along a prescribed camera trajectory, and joint video--camera generation from a reference image and text. First, each tipxel stores the normalized 3D coordinates of a pixel ray's endpoint in a reference coordinate frame. The resulting three-channel tipxel fields and RGB frames share the pretrained video model's VAE. Next, a fully trainable dual-stream Transformer exchanges appearance and geometry features through bidirectional gated attention and conditions both streams on shared text features. Finally, deterministic fitting recovers camera intrinsics and normalized trajectories from generated tipxel fields. In our evaluation tasks, GameWeaver achieves a normalized translation MSE of 0.0029 for camera control; for joint generation, it scores 85.48 out of 100 on instruction adherence versus 64.71 for the best cascade on that metric, validating the feasibility of the proposed approach.



### Teaching Students to Stand on Their Own: Distilling Vision Foundation Models for Fine-Grained Few-Shot Class-Incremental Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33749v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33749v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 04:10:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 04:10:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Junsu Kim, Seungryul Baek
- **Comment**: pre-print version
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Fine-grained few-shot class-incremental learning (FG-FSCIL) aims to incrementally learn visually similar new classes from limited images without catastrophic forgetting. Prior work typically learns strong representations on base classes or leverages vision foundation models (VFMs) directly as backbones or via knowledge distillation. However, VFM backbones incur high computational costs and do not guarantee fine-grained recognition. Although knowledge distillation reduces inference cost, our preliminary experiments reveal that distilled students still lag substantially behind their VFM teachers, particularly on new classes. To address this limitation, we propose STAND, a systematic VFM-to-student distillation design tailored for FG-FSCIL. Specifically, STAND connects the teacher and student by equipping the student with a fixed copy of the teacher's classifier, aligning their outputs via projectors, and feeding both models composite images that combine different training images. On these composite images, relation distillation matches how the teacher and the student relate each image to other images, while difference distillation matches how this relation changes between two composite images. Finally, we freeze the student and classify new classes using length-calibrated prototypes, which restore the reduced similarity of few-shot prototypes. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that STAND achieves state-of-the-art performance across FG-FSCIL datasets at low computational cost, while generalizing across student backbones and broader datasets.



### Spectral Colorimetry Based on GMoE for Temperature Measurement in Explosion Fields
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33750v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33750v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 04:11:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 04:11:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Jie Fang, Wenhao Li, Jianning Yang, Nan Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Spectroscopic thermometry in explosion fields has attracted considerable attention in related engineering domains, primarily because it can mitigate the interference of traditional contact methods with temperature distributions and address the limitations of high-temperature measurement scenarios. However, existing physics-model-driven methods typically rely on regressing a single radiance-ratio pair, which limits their adaptability to wide-range temperature estimation. This limitation arises from notable variations in the mapping between grayscale ratios and radiance ratios at different temperatures. In contrast, existing data-driven methods often suffer from limited performance due to the scarcity of training data, a challenge exacerbated by the sensitivity of explosion fields. In this work, we propose a gated mixture of experts (GMoE) framework that integrates a divide-and-conquer strategy in deep learning with physical prior knowledge for temperature estimation, thereby decomposing the complex task into multiple expert-specific subtasks. Specifically, each expert is developed using physically calibrated polynomial functions to model the relationship between grayscale ratios and radiance ratios for a given pair of spectral bands, with temperature then estimated using a physical model incorporating the corresponding parameters. Additionally, a gating network is designed to adaptively weight the outputs of different experts and generate the final temperature estimate. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves the best performance when implemented with the Transformer-based gating network, yielding a mean RMSE of $12.70 \pm 1.32$ K and a mean MAE of $7.96 \pm 0.86$ K. Compared with conventional spectral colorimetry, the proposed framework reduces the mean RMSE and MAE by 63.4\% and 70.8\%, respectively.



### VAPOR: Improving Multimodal Reasoning with Sparse Visual Updates
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33751v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33751v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 04:11:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 04:11:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Mingyu Su, Wei Wu, Erle Zhu, Chengxing Xie, Deyao Hong, Hongning Wang
- **Comment**: 19 pages, including appendix. Code: https://github.com/Su-my/VAPOR
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made substantial progress in visual reasoning, but extended generation over large visual contexts remains computationally expensive. During autoregressive decoding, visual attention is recomputed for every generated token, even when consecutive tokens rely on similar visual evidence. We introduce VAPOR (Visual Attention Persistence with On-demand Refresh), a training-free framework that reduces this redundancy by reusing visual attention outputs across decoding steps and refreshing them when needed. VAPOR caches the visual attention output while continuing to update textual attention at every step, helping reduce visual grounding drift during extended reasoning. To decide when to reuse cached outputs, VAPOR estimates visual attention mass using a low-rank approximation. It also uses prefill-based attention consistency and cache age to make independent Persist and Refresh decisions for each attention head. VAPOR retains the full visual key-value (KV) cache, so all visual tokens remain available when new evidence is needed. Experiments on eight image and four video reasoning benchmarks across two MLLM families show that VAPOR improves accuracy over dense attention on nearly all benchmarks while achieving 1.11-1.21x decoding speedups. Compared with the strongest state-of-the-art acceleration baseline in each setting, VAPOR further improves accuracy by 0.35-2.15 percentage points while achieving comparable or lower decoding latency. Our code is available at https://github.com/Su-my/VAPOR.



### Show Me the Change You Want: Visual Example-Guided Change Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33756v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33756v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 04:15:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 04:15:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuan Qian, Jiaqi Cui, Jie Ma
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Change detection identifies where images differ, but in practice users often seek only a particular kind of change. Existing referring change detection methods rely on predefined categories, textual descriptions, or annotated masks, limiting flexibility or requiring additional annotation. In this paper, we propose a new way to perform change detection, where an unannotated before-and-after image pair serves as a visual example to guide change localization in another pair. Our framework, Visual Example-Guided Change Detection (VECD), learns comparable attribute states from single images and introduces an example-conditioned readout to match changes across different objects and scenes. The example itself defines the request, without text, example masks, or test-time fine-tuning. We evaluate VECD on four datasets spanning rendered scenes and natural image edits: EditCLEVR, MagicBrush, HumanEdit, and MaskEdit. Experiments demonstrate cross-dataset generalization without fine-tuning: a single fixed example pair guides change detection across multiple scenes, even when examples and queries originate from different datasets. VECD selects matching changes in scenes with multiple simultaneous changes and returns an empty mask when the requested change is absent. It further generalizes to attribute families withheld from paired training, achieving 74.54\% to 93.38\% mIoU across four holdout settings. Code is available at https://github.com/qianyuancs/VECD



### GlassGround: GUI Grounding Beyond Native Screenshots with Egocentric Wearable Vision
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33760v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.HC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33760v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 04:18:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 04:18:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Yun Wang, Siyue Sun, Jiaying Du, Xinyi Wei, Xiaoming Zhai, Zhenlin An, Ninghao Liu
- **Comment**: 27 pages, 14 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Wearable assistants must localize interface elements from camera observations when direct screenshot access is unavailable, yet existing GUI grounding benchmarks primarily evaluate native screenshots. We introduce GlassGround, a new benchmark that pairs native screenshots with wearable camera observations while holding GUI states, instructions, and targets fixed. GlassGround contains 3,250 tasks across Android and iOS, captured using four wearable devices under four viewing conditions. Across ten open-source models, mean grounding accuracy drops from 94.8% on screenshots to 48.5% on camera images. Further analysis reveals that changes in target scale, screen geometry, and background cannot fully explain this performance degradation. We then study two ways to narrow this gap. At inference time, local zooming and screen rectification help but leave most of the gap. For training-time adaptation, we build on GlassGround's paired-view design and introduce PairDistill, a baseline that aligns target representations across views alongside coordinate supervision. Trained on independently collected data and using no screenshots at inference, it improves camera-image accuracy by 9.4 and 12.2 points on two backbones and transfers to unseen devices and operating systems. Combined with local zooming, it reaches 69.6% and 64.4%, but does not close the gap. The GlassGround dataset and code are available at https://github.com/yunnw/GlassGround.



### ScanAR: Parallel Row-Scan Decoding for Efficient Image Generation in Unified Autoregressive Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33761v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33761v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 04:18:55+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 04:18:55+00:00
- **Authors**: Jingen Qu, Xi Chen, Yingbo Tang, Xinghang Li, Tao Kong
- **Comment**: 12 pages, 3 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Unified multimodal autoregressive (AR) models represent text and images in one token sequence and generate all modalities with a shared next-token prediction head. Their image generation, however, decodes an $H\times W$ visual grid token by token in raster order, which takes $HW$ sequential steps and dominates inference time. Existing acceleration methods for visual AR generation either change the tokenizer, the prediction head, or the model interface, or keep the model but cannot reach high parallelism without losing quality, so they do not carry over to unified AR models. We propose ScanAR, a row-scan parallel decoding method that only redefines the predecessors of visual tokens. The first visual row is generated from left to right as usual, and each later row is generated in a single parallel step, with every token predicted from the vertically aligned token of the previous row. Nonvisual tokens keep the standard autoregressive rule. ScanAR reduces the visual decoding depth from $HW$ to $H+W$ without changing the backbone, vocabulary, or output head and without adding parameters, and it supports both training from scratch and lightweight post-training. On Emu3.5-Image-34B, ScanAR post-training reaches a GenEval score of 82.36, the best among post-training acceleration methods trained on the same data, and generates $512\times512$ images $13.8\times$ faster than raster decoding under the same inference setting. On ImageNet, ScanAR trained from scratch has the highest throughput at every model size with FID comparable to raster LlamaGen. On the 34B Xiaomi-Robotics-U0 model, ScanAR preserves the overall quality of embodied scene generation, interleaved image-text generation, and video generation.



### Triage Before Fine-Tuning: Feasibility-Gated Selection and Monitored Adaptation of Embodied Foundation Models for a Fixed Physical Platform
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33772v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33772v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 04:57:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 04:57:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Zilin Huang, Zhengyang Wan, Zihao Sheng, Boyue Wang, Ruolin Yang, Yuhao Luo, Sikai Chen
- **Comment**: Project page: https://zilin-huang.github.io/Triage/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Embodied foundation models are increasingly deployed by selection: a practitioner picks a public checkpoint by benchmark rank and fine-tunes it on local data. The platform is fixed before the model is chosen, with little or no local data. We ask what such a checkpoint loses on a fixed platform and which part of the loss local data can buy back. We split the gap by what can repair it. A sensing gap, information the sensors do not capture, cannot be closed with their data; a latency gap, output arriving after the control loop needs it, belongs to the loop, not the weights; only a distribution gap is fine-tuning's to repair. TRIAGE turns this into feasibility-gated selection and monitored adaptation in five stages: gates on the sensor contract and on end-to-end latency that no score can override, probes on ten minutes of local logs, a ranking by target-relevant score among the candidates that pass, and fine-tuning that is monitored and checked in closed loop before release. On driving benchmarks, a deadline below a leading closed-loop model's latency makes it collide on 80 to 90% of routes, and losing field of view moves plans 2.2 to 5.7 times more than losing resolution at equal pixels. Local frames close 75% of a stability gap but raise perturbation sensitivity at small budgets, and picking by score violates a gate in two of four deployment profiles. On a full-scale vehicle, the factory camera failed the sensing gate and an added camera repaired it. The latency gate removed the two highest-scoring candidates, neither of which completed a closed-loop trip. The selected model passed acceptance in 4 of 5 closed-loop trips, and the closed-loop check withheld a fine-tuned model that had improved offline. TRIAGE lets organizations that cannot train foundation models from scratch find out whether a public checkpoint can run on the robot or vehicle they own, and what to fix if not.



### CHD-Bench: Diagnosing Congenital Heart Disease through Interactive Examination
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33775v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33775v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:00:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:00:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Gautham Krishnan P, Shrisharanyan Vasu, Aniketh Vijesh, Rajagopalan Rameshbabu, Harikrishnan Anil Maya, Sarin Xavier, Mahesh Kappanayil, Gilad Gressel
- **Comment**: 21 pages (8 main + references + appendix), 9 figures, 11 tables. Code: https://github.com/Maitri-Labs-Amrita/CHD-Bench
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Diagnosis of congenital heart disease (CHD) requires understanding the spatial relationships between cardiac structures, which clinicians assess by scrolling through CT or cardiac magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) slices and rotating 3D reconstructions. Although automated methods have advanced cardiac segmentation, identifying congenital heart defects from volumetric scans remains an open challenge. The two existing approaches rely on handcrafted diagnostic rules limited to static anatomical abnormalities. We introduce CHD-Bench, the first benchmark for interactive CHD diagnosis, defining a multi-label task and evaluation protocol over 247 CT and MRI scans from three cohorts covering 38 CHD types. We also present CHD-Harness, open-source software that runs a VLM in a 3D Slicer environment through 22 tools, manages each examination, and records the resulting diagnosis and supporting views. We evaluate off-the-shelf medical, open weight, and closed weight VLMs and compare them with a 3D CNN classifier and published rule-based results. Gemini 4 Argon with CHD-Harness achieves the highest macro F1 across all three cohorts (0.63, 0.63, and 0.43). Allowing for interactive examination raises its macro F1 by up to 20 points over static views. These results establish CHD-Bench and CHD-Harness as a foundation for developing and evaluating VLMs for interactive CHD diagnosis.



### Agentic Reinforcement Learning with Immediate and Prospective Credit Assignment for Medical Visual Reasoning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33777v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33777v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:01:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:01:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuqi Cheng, Rui Meng, Yijin Li, Chandra Sekhar Reddy Edula, Xiuwei Zhang
- **Comment**: version 1 for arxiv
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Medical visual question answering (VQA) requires acquiring useful evidence and deciding where to look next. Yet final-answer rewards do not reveal which observations support answering or guide subsequent inspection. We introduce MedPACT, an agentic reinforcement learning framework that learns selective observation policies through immediate answer support and prospective guidance. Our content-controlled estimator compares next actions induced by real and matched alternative observations under a shared real-observation history, isolating how observed content improves subsequent decisions. We combine both forms of credit within GRPO, assigning observation credit to the corresponding tool-call tokens alongside trajectory-level outcome supervision. MedPACT uses a single vision-language model and image-question-answer supervision, with credit estimation confined to training. Experiments with 4B and 8B backbones on six medical VQA benchmarks demonstrate improved accuracy, strong out-of-distribution generalization, and substantially lower inference costs. Case studies further illustrate how MedPACT avoids repeated observations and uses acquired evidence to guide subsequent inspection, demonstrating real-world practice potential.



### DuoWM:Integrating Dual 6-DoF Visual Flows into Action-Conditioned Drone World Model
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33779v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33779v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:04:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:04:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Zixuan Li, Kangyao Huang, Meng Xu, Wan Tian, Xiaoyu Tang, Jinge Sun, Yingliang Yao, He Chen, Lingzhong Meng, Yunzhi Xue, Jingyu Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Action-conditioned video world models generate future observations from an initial frame and a camera trajectory, but often fail to align camera commands with visual dynamics or represent full 6-DoF scene transformations. We present DuoWM, a first-person drone video world model with two complementary visual conditions. Action flow projects future camera positions and orientations into each target frame, while Object flow forward-warps the initial observation and overlays local 6-DoF transformations to ground the induced scene motion. We further introduce transform-aware pretraining, which pairs object appearance with explicit transformation axes before standard RGB video training. On Sekai-drone dataset, DuoWM achieves the best results across video quality, 3D consistency, and trajectory following. Compared with Captain Safari, the state-of-the-art method, our approach reduces real-domain FVD and LPIPS by 17.2\% and 21.6\%, respectively, while improving game-domain AUC@30 from 0.5003 to 0.5719 and AUC@15 from 0.3088 to 0.3747.



### Bridging Transferability and Discriminability via Positive-Unlabeled Domain Adaptation for Building Damage Mapping Across Disaster Events
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33790v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33790v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:14:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:14:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Qirui Wang, Hengwei Zhao, Xiaoshu Yang, Jingtao Li, Bo Li, Sorin Popescu, Rusty Feagin, Wenzhe Jiao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Mapping the damage to buildings following natural and human-caused disasters is essential for disaster response and humanitarian assistance. However, differences in hazard types, geographic environments, building characteristics, and imaging conditions cause cross-event distribution shifts that limit the generalization of deep learning models to new disaster events, even for geospatial foundation models (GFMs) pretrained on extensive Earth observation data. Unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA) can mitigate these shifts, but adaptation remains challenging when damage-proportion and damage-appearance shifts coexist and interact. We identify enhancing target-domain discriminability as key to addressing this challenge. To this end, inspired by positive-unlabeled (PU) learning, we introduce positive-unlabeled domain adaptation, a paradigm that incorporates PU weak supervision to enhance target-domain discriminability, requiring only a few labeled damaged buildings in the target domain. Building upon this straightforward intuition, we develop Positive-ANchored Domain Adaptation (PANDA), comprising two complementary and mutually reinforcing components: Pairwise Alignment with Intra-class Robustness (PAIR) for discriminative representation learning and Refine Gate and Verifier (RAV) for supervision refinement. On a global-scale multi-disaster benchmark, PANDA generally outperforms representative domain adaptation and PU learning methods. Results on the 2025 Los Angeles wildfires show its effectiveness in new disaster scenarios with few labeled damaged buildings. Further experiments demonstrate the potential of integrating PANDA with GFMs for cross-event adaptation. These findings highlight PANDA's practical potential for rapid, reliable, and cross-event building damage mapping.



### Spatial Transcriptomics Prediction from Histology via Topology-Informed Graph Attention and Gene-Semantics Conditioning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33792v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33792v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:16:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:16:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuqi Cheng, Boshuai Zhang, Ziqi Zhang, Xiuwei Zhang
- **Comment**: Accepted by WACV2027: https://wacv.thecvf.com/virtual/2027/poster/2019
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Spatial transcriptomics (ST) profiles gene expression with spatial context but remains costly, motivating models that infer expression from H&E images. Existing approaches face three key limitations: (i) a single patch embedding is often insufficient: morphologically similar regions can exhibit distinct expression due to spatial context, while naive neighborhood aggregation can over-smooth boundaries; (ii) conventional fixed, gene-indexed output heads treat genes independently and cannot directly support strict zero-shot prediction for unseen genes; and (iii) common metrics under-measure spatial pattern fidelity and boundary resolution. To address these challenges, we propose TIGEM, a topology-informed, gene-conditioned framework for histology-to-ST inference. TIGEM learns topology-aware spot representations by regularizing graph attention with tissue-level topology and morphology cues to avoid cross-compartment smoothing, and uses gene semantic modulation to replace gene-indexed heads, enabling zero-shot prediction for unseen genes. We also introduce Graph Spectral Correlation (GSC) to evaluate spatial pattern fidelity and boundary resolution complement to PCC/MSE. Across three large-scale ST datasets, TIGEM achieves overall state-of-the-art performance and significantly improved boundary prediction resolution, and further demonstrates the ability to predict expression patterns for genes unseen during training, extending prior zero-shot histology-to-ST efforts with topology-aware spatial propagation and PLM-based gene conditioning.



### Keep or Roll Back? Out-of-Loop Judges for Self-Improving VLA and End-to-End Embodied Policies
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33794v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33794v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:20:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:20:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Zilin Huang, Zhengyang Wan, Kangan Qian, Zihao Sheng, Sikai Chen
- **Comment**: Project page: https://zilin-huang.github.io/SealedLoop/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Self-improvement is now a practical recipe for embodied policies: a policy collects experience, is retrained, and is kept when a judge rates it higher. However, the judge is usually trained or chosen inside the same loop, so its evidence is not independent of the policy. We introduce SealedLoop, an evaluation framework that adds a sealed judge: an evaluator outside the loop that the improver cannot see or optimize against. A new policy is kept only when a sequential test on the sealed judge confirms the gain, with a bounded probability of keeping a worse one. Running the same loop under each judge, we separate two sources of a false gain, which we call judge error (the loop's judge is wrong on its own data) and transfer gap (a real gain there does not carry over to unseen conditions). We evaluate on LIBERO manipulation, NAVSIM driving, and a full-scale real vehicle. In manipulation, a success classifier accepted 65 of 196 failures in one generation. In driving, policies that gain on the loop's own frames lose in an unseen city, so even an exact in-loop judge would keep 8/15 of them. When the sealed test decides, a driving loop built to improve ends with a better policy than under its own critic (0.674 against 0.649 PDMS, from 0.638), and on driving pairs with known sealed gain the test accepted 11 of 12 real gains and none of 59 losses. In a real-world field experiment on a published improvement step, the reinforced version drove further into a curve than the version it started from in all 6 blind pairs, though neither completed it. These results show that evidence from inside the loop, however accurate, is not enough to decide whether a policy has improved. SealedLoop offers a foundation for trustworthy self-improvement of embodied policies, in which progress is judged by evidence the improving system cannot influence.



### SprintGS: Eliminating Redundancy for Fast 3D Gaussian Splatting Training
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33796v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33796v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:21:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:21:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Ryosuke Watanabe, Tomoaki Konno
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) renders photorealistic novel views in real time, but its per-scene training (optimization) remains slow. Recent fast training methods such as FastGS shorten training to a few minutes by reducing the number of Gaussians. Profiling FastGS, we find two costs that remain even in its compact model. First, rasterization is still the largest cost, and redundant work remains in it. Second, the loss computation does not shrink with the number of Gaussians, so its share of the training time grows as the model becomes compact. We present SprintGS, a training framework built on FastGS that keeps the model compact and reduces both costs. For rasterization, scheduled tile culling limits each Gaussian to the tiles where it matters, using a coarse footprint that is annealed to the exact one before training ends; convergence-aware pruning removes converged, nearly transparent Gaussians; and a shuffle-efficient backward pass reduces the data exchanged between GPU threads. For the loss, a fused loss computes the loss and its image gradient in the forward pass without a loss map, removing most of its cost. SprintGS trains 2.15x faster than FastGS at comparable quality, and 1.81x faster in its high-quality setting. It also accelerates other fast training methods and dynamic, sparse-view, and surface reconstruction.



### DriftWAM: Faster World-Action Models via Action-Aware Video Drifting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33797v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33797v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:23:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:23:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Minyang Li, Siyu Xu, Yunke Wang, Ying-Cong Chen, Chang Xu, Daochang Liu
- **Comment**: 16 pages, 5 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World-action models (WAMs) unify future video prediction and action generation for robotic control, but their reliance on iterative denoising incurs substantial inference costs. Distilling these models into few-step generators offers a promising route to efficient deployment, yet maintaining task performance under aggressive step reduction remains challenging. Existing methods rely on point-wise trajectory consistency distillation, which pursues high visual fidelity while leaving downstream action information unexploited in optimizing video generation, and also lacks explicit distribution-level alignment. To address this, we propose DriftWAM, a few-step WAM distillation framework that incorporates the actions induced by predicted videos into video learning. Specifically, DriftWAM integrates action-aware video drifting, which performs video distribution matching through a joint visual--action similarity kernel to steer sample evolution under the guidance of these induced actions, with action-guided video regularization, which leverages the compatible teacher--student matches identified by the joint kernel to backpropagate action-level gradients directly into the video generator. Evaluations on the RoboTwin 2.0 and LIBERO benchmarks, as well as in real-world robotic manipulation tasks, show that DriftWAM consistently outperforms prior consistency distillation methods across few-step settings at the same inference speed.



### Dynamic Forcing: Learning Action-Grounded Sparse Dynamic Representations with Dense Future Guidance for World Action Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33799v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33799v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:27:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:27:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziqiang Li, Shisheng Chen, Yunnan Wang, Wenyao Zhang, Zili Lin, Yuyang Zhang, Xudong Yang, Fangzhuo Jin, Chenyu Zhou, Wenjun Zeng, Xin Jin
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World action models (WAMs) support robot control through visual prediction, but dense future modeling repeatedly encodes static context already available in the current observation, while action-relevant changes are often spatially sparse. We study sparse dynamic representation learning, retaining the current observation as a contextual anchor and using a small set of compact dynamic tokens to represent anticipated changes. We develop CompactWAM, a sparse-dense joint predictive framework that enables sparse representation learning within a video-supervised shared backbone, without requiring a reconstruction objective for the sparse pathway. Since a limited token budget alone does not specify which information to retain, we introduce Dynamic Forcing, which pairs direct action supervision with privileged dense future guidance to promote action relevance and dynamic fidelity.
