While action prediction gradients directly drive compact tokens to capture control-relevant cues, dense future guidance further shapes them via action functional guidance that matches sparse and dense contributions to the action expert, and dynamics-aware distribution matching, which aligns temporal relational changes relative to a static reference. At inference, a single visual forward pass encodes current observations and compact dynamic tokens for action generation, eliminating dense future modeling. CompactWAM achieves 98.4\% success on LIBERO and 77.9\% on LIBERO-Plus, retaining respective success rates of 98.3\% and 76.6\% with only 9.2\% of the dense future token budget. Evaluations on RoboTwin~2.0 and real-world manipulation further demonstrate the effectiveness of the learned sparse predictive representations.



### SpiderSkel: Sub-Pixel Multi-Tangent 2D Skeletonization from Inscribed-Disc Geometry
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33805v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33805v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:34:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:34:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhexian Zhou, Lian Ren, Xiao Feng, Sebastian Scherer
- **Comment**: 13 pages, 4 figures, 5 tables, plus 6 pages of supplementary material. Submitted to IEEE Transactions on Image Processing
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Skeletonization reduces a binary shape to a compact medial representation, but pixel-grid discretization perturbs the boundary-contact geometry underlying the medial axis, creating tension among connectivity, reconstruction fidelity, and skeleton compactness. SpiderSkel is a family of training-free two-dimensional skeletonization methods built on multi-tangent inscribed-disc geometry. A tie-aware exact Euclidean distance transform retains tied nearest boundary sites rather than collapsing them to a single label; medial candidates are localised to sub-pixel positions from the inscribed disc's tangent contacts, filtered by the angular span of those contacts, and completed by connectivity-aware refinement. Across nineteen public benchmarks comprising 11,323 images of shapes, retinal vessels, cracks and roads, and document images, a single operating point fixed across datasets yields skeletons that, on every image, have exact skeleton topology under the component/hole certificate, remain fully connected, and have exact per-object reconstruction topology. The three SpiderSkel variants are the only evaluated methods to satisfy all three properties across every category; within each category, every variant is Pareto-optimal with respect to all twenty-two baselines in strict sub-pixel reconstruction Dice versus reduction rate. Incremental engines for all three variants reproduce the skeleton of a complete recomputation exactly across 23,040 verified edit steps, with median update latencies of 15.4-18.1 ms per foreground edit and 4.3-4.4 ms per canvas edit, and speedups reaching 33.0-fold on canvas edits.



### EvoVAU: Tool Evolution for Video Anomaly Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33808v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33808v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:38:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:38:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Benfeng Wang, Chao Huang, Yudong Cheng, Chengliang Liu, Wei Wang, Wenqi Ren
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video anomaly understanding (VAU) requires reasoning about event progression, interactions, and the contextual conditions that distinguish abnormal behavior from common activity across diverse scenes. Although video agents enable multi-round analysis, the predefined analytical operations may not adequately satisfy these diverse reasoning demands. In this paper, we present EvoVAU, a framework that evolves a reusable tool library for a frozen video solver. Guided by task outcomes and execution trajectories, an external proposer identifies potential limitations in the library and refines it, retaining task experience as analytical operations that can be reused across questions. Specifically, Cross-Version Contrastive Feedback guides tool refinement by combining current errors with paired execution trajectories from historical corrections and regressions. Adoption-Aware Selection accepts a candidate library only when overall development-set accuracy improves and corrections outnumber regressions among questions on which the solver invokes new or modified tools. Using a small development set, EvoVAU alternates tool refinement with candidate evaluation and selection. At test time, the evolved library remains fixed, with no further involvement from the proposer. Experiments on several VAU benchmarks demonstrate strong performance and the flexibility of the evolved library across anomaly domains.



### Probabilistic Monocular Biomechanical Reconstruction via Guided Flow Matching
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33809v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33809v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:40:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:40:28+00:00
- **Authors**: R. James Cotton, Fabian Sinz
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reconstructing 3D joint kinematics from a single video is ill-posed due to depth and stature ambiguity, limb occlusion, and missing uncertainty estimates. We introduce the Biomechanical Flow Model (BFM), a continuous-time flow-matching prior over subject anthropometry, anatomical joint-angle trajectories, and virtual markers, trained on multi-camera clinical recordings from 565 participants and steered toward monocular 2D keypoints at test time without paired video training. In a head-to-head comparison on the same clinical trials against a state-of-the-art deterministic trajectory optimizer fitting the identical skeleton, BFM achieves comparable joint-angle accuracy across walking and timed-up-and-go (TUG). Across held-out control, prosthetic, and neurologic participants, BFM reaches 3.91 deg and 4.97 deg mean absolute joint-angle error on walking and TUG while providing credible intervals that bridge temporal and whole-limb occlusions. Where keypoints are hidden, the intervals widen with the error and keep their coverage near nominal. Supplying individual anthropometric calibration removes most monocular pelvis depth error, whereas testing on out-of-distribution pediatric crouch gait produces biased, overconfident extension predictions, highlighting the need for pathologically diverse movement priors.



### FlipVLA: Backdoor Attacks on Vision-Language-Action(VLA) Models Only Through Bit Flips
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33810v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33810v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:40:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:40:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Zexi Zhao, Zizhen Liu, Jing Justin Ye, Huawei Li
- **Comment**: 14 pages, including references and supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models generate robot actions from visual observations and language instructions in real-world environments, making malicious model tampering a potential threat to physical safety. We study whether object-triggered backdoors can be implanted through a small, fixed set of parameter bit flips without retraining. The central challenge is to make persistent weight modifications disrupt trigger-present behavior while preserving performance on non-trigger inputs. We propose FlipVLA, a two-stage selector-amplifier method that exploits existing visual features within an FC-GELU-FC structure. The selector constructs a trigger-dependent response in a selected channel, using GELU to attenuate background activity. The amplifier modifies a connected downstream weight to convert this response into an action-relevant perturbation. Channel screening and discrete bit search construct both components under a limited modification budget. Experiments on OpenVLA-OFT and GR00T N1.7 across four LIBERO suites achieve attack success rates of 90.22% to 100%, with task success decreasing by at most 1.5 percentage points on clean scenes.



### Spectral Optimal Transport for Dynamics-Aware Sign Language Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33818v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33818v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:47:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:47:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Nabeela Khan, Bowen Wu, Runwu Shi, Hanlong Li, Benjamin Yen, Carlos Toshinori Ishi, Kazuhiro Nakadai
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Sign language generation (SLG) translates spoken-language text into pose sequences that convey its meaning. Recently, flow-based models with optimal transport (OT) have become a strong SLG baseline by handling the one-to-many nature of text-to-sign. While classifier-free guidance (CFG) could be applied to further strengthen the semantic alignment theoretically, its training involves conditional and unconditional training, which may contradict the pose-space OT, thus reducing the generation quality. Under the condition dropout, one noise sample can be paired with target motions exhibiting incompatible motion dynamics, interfering with the semantic guidance when the text condition is present. To address this issue, we propose Spectral Optimal Transport (SOT), which augments the pose-space cost with a spectral feature distance, reweighting the coupling toward the temporal-frequency structure of the target motions, thereby improving the performance when using CFG. The quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.



### MMotion: Collaborative Cross-Scale Memory for Text-to-Motion Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33819v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33819v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:48:24+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:48:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhaoxin Fan, Qingchen Yu, Kejian Wu, Jian Yang, Zeming Liu, wenjun wu, Lili Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text-to-motion generation aims to synthesize realistic human motions from natural-language descriptions, enabling applications in animation, virtual avatars, and embodied agents. Despite recent progress, existing methods either retrieve motion exemplars or retain long-range context, but rarely exploit memory to jointly reason about semantics, dynamics, and generation history. In this paper, we propose MMotion, a collaborative multi-scale memory framework that unifies scale-conditioned semantic retrieval, latent dynamics modeling, and causal working memory. Specifically, semantic memory provides motion priors at different generation scales, dynamics memory captures latent state transitions, and working memory preserves causal context from previously generated motions. A collaborative fusion mechanism integrates these complementary memories to determine what motion to generate, how it should evolve, and how it should remain consistent with its history. MMotion is architecture-agnostic and can be readily integrated into both diffusion-based and next scale autoregressive models. Extensive experiments across standard benchmarks and diverse backbones demonstrate consistent improvements over strong baselines.



### Revisiting Monocular In-Hand Object Scanning: Dataset, Pose Estimation, and Surface Reconstruction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33824v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33824v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:54:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:54:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Di Wu, Jeeeun Kim, Shu Kong
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In-Hand Object Scanning (IHOS) from monocular video captured by a commodity smartphone holds immense potential to democratize 3D digitization for non-expert users, facilitating applications in digital archiving, augmented reality, and custom fabrication. Yet, IHOS remains largely understudied due to a lack of realistic datasets and systematic benchmarks. To bridge this gap, we introduce ToysRUs, a high-quality dataset featuring diverse figurines captured by 4K Ultra High-Definition (UHD) monocular videos. Studying IHOS from first principles, we examine structure-from-motion (SfM) for camera pose estimation, which is key to IHOS. We identify two key challenges: small baselines between consecutive frames, and sparse feature matches across large-view, long-range frames, further exacerbated by dynamic hand occlusions. Guided by these insights, we propose performing SfM on video sub-sequences sampled at sufficient angular intervals, and iteratively stitches the corresponding sub-trajectories into a unified global trajectory. Furthermore, for high-fidelity surface reconstruction, we introduce occlusion-aware 2D Gaussian Splatting, which incorporates opacity rendering and omits the opacity loss on hand regions. Quantitative and qualitative evaluations demonstrate that our framework significantly outperforms existing approaches, including neural implicit representations and 3D foundation models, in both geometric accuracy and photometric fidelity.



### MotionODE: Enhancing Autoregressive Text-to-Motion Generation with Continuous-Depth Modeling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33826v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33826v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 05:55:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 05:55:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhaoxin Fan, Jian Yang, Kejian Wu, Zeming Liu, Wenjun Wu, Lili Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Autoregressive text-to-motion generation requires local motion predictions to reconcile cross-temporal textual requirements with the available motion context. While existing methods primarily improve motion representations and generation order, the internal formation of conditioned motion representations remains less explored. We introduce MotionODE, a continuous-depth post-training framework that refines hidden-state evolution in pretrained autoregressive generators. MotionODE reinterprets selected Transformer residual branches as local vector fields and replaces their original updates with midpoint Runge--Kutta (RK2) integration, while preserving the motion representation and outer generation mechanism. To address the mismatch between pretrained update paths and newly introduced intermediate states, we propose mixed one-step/two-step RK2 post-training under motion-token supervision, with optional distillation and update-map constraints to preserve pretrained behavior. This minimally invasive framework supports both next-token and next-scale generation without training the generator from scratch. Extensive experiments on HumanML3D demonstrate consistent improvements in text--motion alignment across MoScale, BAMM and LMR, highlighting internal representation updates as a complementary direction for improving autoregressive motion generation.



### 3D CoCa v3: Contrastive Learners with Scene Graphs for Urban Scene Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33835v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33835v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:03:25+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:03:25+00:00
- **Authors**: Nannan Zhang, Ting Huang, Zeyu Zhang, Haoyu Chen, Yanjie Zhu, Hao Tang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Understanding large-scale 3D urban scenes requires representations that capture both multi-scale geometric structures and complex spatial relationships. However, existing 3D vision-language methods primarily emphasize global semantic alignment or object-level representations, leaving structured relational modeling underexplored within unified contrastive and generative frameworks. In this paper, we present 3D CoCa v3, a scene-graph-enhanced contrastive and generative framework for urban 3D scene understanding. Our framework features a Pyramid Vision Encoder for multi-scale geometric representation learning and a language-model-based decoder with question-guided caption retrieval and visual cross-attention. We further introduce MSG-based relational descriptions to enrich contrastive 3D-language alignment with structured spatial relationships. To support semantic and relational supervision, we construct City3D-9.6K, a multi-view urban vision-language dataset with aligned visual-geometric observations, scene captions, and scene graphs. Extensive experiments on City-3DQA demonstrate that 3D CoCa v3 achieves 88.35% and 64.79% overall top-1 accuracy on the sentence-wise and city-wise splits, surpassing 3D CoCa v2 by 20.88 and 3.57 percentage points, respectively. Furthermore, 3D CoCa v3 achieves the best performance on GeoEval3D, demonstrating its effectiveness in urban spatial reasoning and geometric understanding. Code is available at https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/3DCoCav3.



### PRISM: Perceptual Rate-Controllable Extreme Image Compression with Visual State-Space Models

- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generative codecs built on pretrained diffusion priors achieve high perceptual quality at ultra-low bitrates (below 0.05 bpp). However, most methods still require a separate model for each bitrate, and offer no control over the amount of detail synthesized. Their rate efficiency also depends on the entropy model, where Mamba-based state-space models (SSMs) have recently improved autoregressive context modeling. However, conventional SSM scans are not aligned with the decoding order, and each decoding pass restarts from an empty state, discarding context accumulated in previous passes. We propose PRISM, an extreme image compression method that addresses these limitations. A single model provides two continuous controls: a rate knob that traverses the rate-distortion curve and a realism knob that adjusts the level of detail synthesized. Its visual state-space (VSS) context model introduces a decode-order-aligned (DOA) scan, which predicts each latent from a state that summarizes all decoded context, and cross-tap state carry, which initializes each tap with a summary of the previous one. Experiments show that PRISM outperforms both variable-rate and fixed-rate codecs, requiring on average 22% less bitrate than the strongest baseline at equal FID while also achieving higher PSNR.



### The Missing Root: Geometric Imagination Without Images in Large Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33841v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33841v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:11:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:11:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Loïc Magnan, Tomasz Kozlowski
- **Comment**: 17 pages, 7 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: This paper proposes a new paradigm and method for giving large language models spatial understanding. Standard language models operate exclusively over discrete words (secondary representations of things rather than continuous space itself), forcing them to guess geometry verbally. Whereas most multimodal approaches equate spatial grounding with camera images or video frames, spatial structure itself is not a picture: it consists of coordinate frames, solid boundaries, and how objects move and relate to each other in continuous space. Motivated by this distinction, we place continuous object tokens combining pose (position, orientation, and scale) and signed-distance shape embeddings alongside word tokens inside a single uninterrupted transformer sequence, allowing language and geometry to attend to each other as object scenes are built and updated step by step. Control tokens switch output automatically between natural-language words and continuous object state updates to a persistent object workspace, while an attention mask blocks earlier prose when generating the answer, so responses must be read directly from settled geometry. To prove this method works, we attach a narrow parallel spatial stream (+4.4% weights) alongside unfrozen Qwen3-1.7B layers. In our two-dimensional tests, the model spawns and transforms multi-object scenes from English descriptions, transfers shapes to held-out nouns, and relies causally on the workspace to answer spatial questions.



### SPiXSeg: Rethinking MLLM-based Image Segmentation as Superpixel Classification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33842v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33842v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:11:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:11:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Piyush Tiwary, Sohini Dutta, Prathosh A. P
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are widely used for image segmentation. However, existing methods either use a decoder or use patch-based approach which trades boundary precision against the number of decisions as coarse grid cells mix foreground and background pixels, while finer grids require many more predictions, and autoregressive mask generation adds sequential decoding overhead. We propose SPiXSeg, which shifts the output from per-patch to per-superpixel label assignment, using superpixel tokens formed by aggregating patch-level visual features. An off-the-shelf model partitions each image into task-agnostic superpixels, so the partition is computed once and reused across tasks. A Spix-to-Mask (S2M) module scores each superpixel token in a single MLLM forward pass by directly comparing the MLLM's hidden representations, without autoregressive generation or a separate scoring network, and the selected superpixels are mapped directly to pixels without refinement. Without any trainable decoder head or post-hoc refinement, SPiXSeg achieves an average cIoU of 80.5 with LLaVA-1.5-7B and 81.9 with InternVL2.5-8B on RefCOCO, RefCOCO+ and RefCOCOg and, when trained only on COCO-Stuff, extends to open-vocabulary semantic segmentation with mIoU of 29.0, 59.5 and 95.4 on ADE20K, PASCAL Context and PASCAL VOC, outperforming several SoTA MLLM baselines. Our results suggest that a fixed, query-agnostic superpixel partition is enough for accurate language-guided segmentation.



### Continuous Foundation-Guided Pseudo-Label Weighting for Breast Ultrasound Domain Adaptation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33843v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33843v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:13:50+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:13:50+00:00
- **Authors**: Xinyu Fan, Xinyi Li
- **Comment**: 8 pages, 3 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Breast ultrasound segmentation models often generalize poorly across scanners, institutions, and patient populations. We investigate continuous foundation-guided pseudo-label weighting for unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA). An exponential-moving-average (EMA) teacher generates target-domain pseudo-labels, while a frozen ultrasound segmentation model (AutoSAMUS) modulates their contribution using a bounded weight that combines the prior's certainty with its probabilistic agreement with the teacher. In contrast to our earlier selective BiRP rule, the proposed formulation supervises every target pixel with a weight in [0.5, 1] and requires no foundation-model inference at test time. We evaluate the method on five public breast ultrasound datasets, spanning three source domains and eleven source-target directions. Under the TransUNet protocol, continuous weighting outperforms both all-pixel EMA and original BiRP on all four BUSI-source targets, and achieves the highest mean Dice and lowest HD95 among the compared methods on all four BUS-BRA-source targets. Results with BrEaST as the source are mixed: continuous weighting performs best on BUSI and comparably to original BiRP on OASBUD, whereas none of the adaptation methods surpasses source-only training on BUS-UCLM. In a separate U-Net pipeline, agreement-only weighting performs comparably to the full formulation, leaving the incremental value of prior certainty unresolved. Because the weighting rule was developed after some target-test results had been examined, we treat the study as exploratory and report adverse as well as favorable outcomes.



### Hybrid Reinforcement Learning for One-step Degraded Infrared and Visible Image Fusion
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33857v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33857v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:33:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:33:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Hongwei Yu, Daoqing Zha, Jiawei Li, Yancheng Bai, Lei Sun, Xiangxiang Chu
- **Comment**: 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026)
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Infrared and visible image fusion (IVIF) is essential for robust perception, yet existing methods remain limited under complex degradations. While diffusion models (DMs) offer powerful modeling capabilities to combat such degradations, their iterative process incurs high computational cost. One-step distillation effectively accelerates inference but typically leads to over-smoothed results. To resolve these limitations, we propose a one-step fusion framework with hybrid reinforcement learning, termed OneHRL. We treat degraded inputs as intermediate noisy states along a generative trajectory, enabling a distilled one-step generator to rectify them directly to the clean fusion results. Concurrently, to mitigate the over-smoothed results in distillation, we incorporate the Flow-GRPO paradigm to introduce stochasticity into the deterministic one-step generation. This restores the stochastic exploration necessary for sampling-based reinforcement learning. Furthermore, we design a coarse-to-fine reward that integrates a global semantic reward with an object-level reward. This hybrid reward ensures that the generator captures both naturalness and fine-grained texture. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves superior fusion quality and efficiency.



### Structured Noise Diffusion for Echocardiography Haze Removal
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33860v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33860v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:36:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:36:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Zike Luo, Hao Li, Mengxiao Wang, Qingya Li, Ching-Hui Sia, Lei Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Echocardiographic images are frequently degraded by spatially structured haze artifacts that obscure anatomical boundaries and hinder clinical analysis. Conventional diffusion models typically employ pixel-independent Gaussian noise, limiting their ability to represent localized ultrasound haze. In this study, we propose a structure-informed diffusion framework that explicitly models ultrasound haze as an accumulation of localized Gaussian components constrained by the ultrasound fan geometry. The forward process generates spatially correlated corruption by sampling component locations and scales, providing a formulation that better reflects the spatial characteristics of real echo artifacts. During reverse restoration, a dual-head network predicts an anchor probability map and Gaussian scale parameters to progressively remove structured artifacts while reducing the center bias associated with direct coordinate regression. We evaluated the framework on the public DehazingEcho2025 benchmark and two private clinical datasets, without retraining or fine-tuning on either private cohort. Our method achieved the highest mean generalized contrast-to-noise ratio across all three datasets, reaching $0.715 \pm 0.090$ on the public benchmark while preserving tissue edges.



### OmniMeld: Enhancing Audio-Visual Understanding via Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33861v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33861v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:41:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:41:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Daili Hua, Yifan Dai, Bohan Zeng, Yuran Wang, Zihao Wu, Chengzhuo Tong, Guoqing Jiang, Liwei Chen, Zhiheng Qin, Xuanping Li, Wentao Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Audio-visual understanding requires perceptual grounding, temporal understanding, and semantic inference. Multi-teacher distillation can consolidate these capabilities, but direct probability matching does not explicitly capture teacher prediction changes across audio-visual conditions. We introduce OmniMeld, a multi-teacher on-policy distillation method that integrates domain expertise through modality-response-guided distribution matching. We train four teachers covering grounding and correspondence, temporal dynamics and event structure, causal and inferential reasoning, and contextual and holistic understanding. Each sample is routed to its corresponding teacher for supervision conditioned on student-generated answer prefixes. We derive transport costs from teacher prediction changes over shared candidate tokens across audio-visual conditions, using optimal transport to match student predictions to those of the teacher under complete audio-visual input. We also construct OmniMeld-20K for domain-specialized teacher training. The dataset spans four capability domains, with responses structured into evidence, reasoning, and answer sections and verified through consistency checks and media-based review. Experiments on audio-visual and general video understanding benchmarks demonstrate competitive teacher performance among similarly sized open-source models. The student improves over its base model across all evaluated benchmarks and surpasses the teachers' base model on several benchmarks.



### Measuring the Value of One Shared Image at Robot Handoffs in Vision-and-Language Navigation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33865v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33865v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:43:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:43:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuze Lu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: When the lead robot cannot continue, what should it send the teammate that must finish the route? We replay a frozen receiver at each contact under alternative messages. Across 3,920 autonomous departures in 106 environments, adding the view before the lead's last action block to its executed actions raised success by 7.51 percentage points (pp) per RxR handoff and 8.54 pp per SAGE handoff. Per departure the gains were 2.23 and 1.44 pp, respectively. The lead continuing in simulation defines the empirical full-history ceiling (Solo). At contact, the lead had accumulated a median of 41 frames. Sending one frame when available recovered 24% of the full-history advantage over actions alone in SAGE and 30% in RxR. Over an estimated progress cue in SAGE, the image rescued 100 routes and reversed 54 successes. Near-goal receivers finished outside less often (63.1% to 51.7%, post hoc). In secondary comparisons the earlier view outperformed the contact view by 6-7 pp per handoff. Exact-route specificity and image gains at controlled starts with mostly unknown progress or outside the goal region remained unresolved. Gains were detectable from about 7 kB per handoff. Random transmission matched within size bins nearly equaled the learned sender's team success. At a required handoff, a view of the approach to the last maneuver helps the receiver finish.



### MAFNet-UR$^3$: Mask-Aware Fusion Network for Unified Raindrop and Reflection Removal
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33866v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33866v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:44:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:44:17+00:00
- **Authors**: Yeongjin Jeong, Hyunjin Cho, Minsang Kim, Jiwon Lee, Jae Woong Soh
- **Comment**: Accepted at the ECCV 2026 LoViF Workshop. Received the Outstanding Challenge Paper Runner-Up Award
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Raindrops and reflections frequently co-occur on windshields and jointly obscure scene content, making their unified removal, referred to as UR$^3$, a highly ill-posed problem. Existing approaches are mainly based on diffusion models and thus can produce visually plausible restorations. However, they may generate scene-inconsistent structures in severely corrupted regions. To address this limitation, we propose MAFNet-UR$^3$, which adopts a three-stage framework to exploit multiple observations for scene-consistent unified raindrop and reflection removal. Specifically, Stage I suppresses raindrops while preserving the reflection layer, and Stage II performs prior-guided reflection removal followed by image-space refinement. Stage III estimates degradation masks from intermediate restoration results to provide degradation cues and performs mask-aware multi-scale fusion across observations to preserve scene structure and fine details. As a result, our proposed MAFNet-UR$^3$-L achieves state-of-the-art performance on the RDRF-testing benchmark, outperforming DiffUR$^3$ by 2.33 dB in PSNR and 2.7634 in the challenge score. Our method ranked third in the LoViF 2026 UR$^3$ Challenge, achieving a final score of 41.1416.



### Concept Generalization in Visual Brain Decoding via Encoder-Generated fMRI
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33870v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33870v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:50:44+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:50:44+00:00
- **Authors**: Peter Yongho Kim, Juhyeon Park, Dongsheng Li, Yansen Wang, Dongqi Han, Mingqing Xiao, Taesup Moon
- **Comment**: 33 pages, 7 figures, 20 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual brain decoders are typically trained and evaluated on stimuli drawn from similar semantic distributions, leaving their ability to reconstruct unseen concepts poorly understood. We study this concept generalization problem by withholding semantic concepts from a target subject's training data. We find that three recent fMRI-to-image decoders degrade substantially for the held-out concepts, even when the concept remains available for multi-subject models. In contrast, brain encoders that map a pretrained visual representation directly to voxels are markedly more robust to the same semantic holdout. Motivated by this asymmetry, we use such an encoder to generate synthetic fMRI responses for new images and add the resulting image-fMRI pairs to decoder training. Across three decoders, four subjects, and twelve semantic concepts, synthetic data recovers a substantial fraction of the performance lost on unseen concepts without requiring additional, time-consuming fMRI acquisition. We further show that the same approach improves general decoding performance across a wide range of data budgets. Analyses of encoder training conditions, encoder designs, and synthetic-data controls show that the gains depend on both encoder generalization and the semantic content of the synthetic supervision, rather than merely increasing the amount of training data. Together, our results show that encoder-generated fMRI can transfer semantic generalization to decoders.



### X-Matte: VAE-Free Pixel-Space Flow Matching for Single-Step Image Matting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33874v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33874v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:53:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:53:33+00:00
- **Authors**: Zanyi Wang, Jiaang Li, Dengyang Jiang, Ziru Wang, Chengzu Li, Davit Abrahamyan, Harry Yang, Serge Belongie, Mengmeng Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pre-trained generative models offer rich visual priors for image matting, but their adaptation often relies on variational autoencoders (VAEs), whose spatial compression can degrade fine alpha details and introduce additional inference overhead. To address these limitations, we propose X-Matte, a VAE-free pixel-space flow matching framework for single-step image matting. By eliminating latent compression, X-Matte formulates the RGB-to-alpha transition as a continuous flow directly in pixel space. We adopt trajectory-wide x-prediction with transition-aware alpha supervision, enabling the model to learn from intermediate states while directly predicting the target alpha matte in a single forward pass. This design avoids both VAE encoding and decoding and iterative sampling. Extensive experiments demonstrate performance competitive with state-of-the-art trimap-free matting methods on P3M benchmarks, together with promising zero-shot generalization to RWP-636 and AIM-500. Moreover, X-Matte achieves up to a 9.4x inference speedup over a constructed single-step latent counterpart at high resolution, demonstrating the effectiveness of VAE-free pixel-space modeling for accurate and efficient image matting.



### Off the Driven Path: Transferable Road Understanding in Driving VLAs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33878v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33878v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 06:55:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 06:55:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Lena Wild, Christian Helten, Katie Z Luo, Jakob Thumm, Rafael Valencia, Patric Jensfelt, Marco Pavone
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: End-to-end driving policies increasingly operate without explicit HD maps at inference, raising the question of what road knowledge they actually internalize. To probe this knowledge through model outputs, we introduce Road-KSU, a framework that automatically converts HD maps and actor annotations into a road-centric scene graph. From this, we derive road-focused question answering and reasoning targets, complemented by map-based trajectory compliance metrics. Pairing Road-KSU with geographically disjoint splits yields a $2 \times 2$ evaluation crossing output modality (language vs. action) with place familiarity (seen vs. unseen roads). On NAVSIM, 95% of test samples lie within 10m of a training sample, and scores of 13 published planners on turns rise steeply with the number of training drives through the test location, pointing to memorized road geometry. Retraining on geographically disjoint splits and probing with Road-KSU confirm that action-space performance degrades most on unfamiliar turns, with failures dominated by geometric compliance. Language accuracy, however, drops on road-centric VQA on straight and curved roads alike, and remains stable on dynamic scene content. Scaling the training data further reveals a double dissociation: more drives through the same places improve driving on unseen roads but leave road question answering unchanged, whereas spreading a fixed data budget over more places increases road question answering with little change in driving performance. These findings show that improved driving scores need not imply broader road understanding on unseen roads, and identify geographic diversity as key to transferable road knowledge.



### LayoutRecall: Preserving Appearance in Multi-Round 3D Layout-Controlled Image Editing
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33885v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33885v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:00:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:00:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Yankuan Chi, Xin Lin, Baihan Yang, Yuheng Liu, Jason Li, Truong Nguyen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D layout-controlled image editing gives users explicit control over object placement and camera viewpoint. In a sequence of edits, however, the latest image can lose the appearance of an object when it is occluded or leaves view. The target layout specifies where the object should reappear, while its appearance must be recovered from earlier observations. We introduce LayoutRecall, a multi-round editor that stores and reuses appearance in the local coordinate systems of 3D boxes. Box-Surface Latent Memory retains image latents on box faces and retrieves them along rays in the target view. Geometry-Aligned Memory Conditioning supplies the retrieved tokens to a FLUX.2 editor using target-view positions, shared box-local embeddings, and object-wise attention. A correspondence loss encourages the editor to attend to matching observations during denoising. We also introduce LayoutLoop, a multi-round 3D layout-controlled image editing benchmark with calibrated layouts and designed revisits. We conduct extensive experiments on LayoutLoop and real-scene data to verify the ability of our method. The project page is at https://ykchizs.github.io/layoutrecall.github.io/.



### DeltaRAE: Derivative Tokenization for Long Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33886v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33886v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:00:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:00:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Mingzhuo Yu, Shengcao Cao, Chengzhi Mao, Yong Cheng, Di Jiang, Lijun Yu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Latent video generators are trained on clips of a few seconds, and much of this limit lies in their tokenizers, which spend as many tokens on a static frame as on a moving one and need future frames that do not exist during generation. We introduce DeltaRAE, a causal representation autoencoder for video. Its derivative tokenization encodes periodic anchor frames in full and all remaining frames with a few tokens each, and its causal encoder and decoder read a frozen pretrained image encoder. On UCF-101 and Kinetics-600, a standard diffusion transformer trained on DeltaRAE latents is competitive with state-of-the-art video tokenizers. DeltaRAE halves the tokenizer GFLOPs on 16-frame clips relative to keeping the same number of patch tokens for every frame, and it represents a minute of video with 18.8x fewer tokens. The tokenizer trains end to end on one-minute videos, and in video prediction a generator on its latents produces a full minute of video. The same generator collapses within 15 seconds in most videos when the tokenizer has the same token budget but no pretrained encoder.



### SIA: Selective Image Analogy
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33894v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33894v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:14:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:14:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Amirhossein Alimohammadi, Kian Izadpanah, Bardiya Kariminia, Yiorgos Chrysanthou, Ali Mahdavi-Amiri
- **Comment**: SIGGRAPH Asia 2026 (Conference Papers). 28 pages including supplementary material. Project page: https://selective-image-analogy.github.io/ Code: https://github.com/alimohammadiamirhossein/selective-image-analogy Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/selective-image-analogy/SIA-Multi-Edit-Analogy
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Image analogy transfers a demonstrated transformation from a before/after pair (A, A') to a new query image B. Existing methods assume all changes in (A, A') should be fully transferred, limiting flexibility when users want only a subset of the demonstrated edits. We introduce Selective Image Analogy (SIA), a new setting where a single demonstration pair encodes multiple edits, and the user selects at inference time which edits to apply to B. To support this, we curate a multi-edit analogy dataset with full-edit and partial-edit targets. We then train a model that first learns to transfer the complete demonstrated transformation, and then gains selective control through a mask-aligned suppression mechanism: a token-gated LoRA that restricts adapter updates to suppressed edit tokens during denoising, paired with a concept suppression vector that weakens the selected tokens in the text embeddings before denoising. Together, these components allow SIA to suppress unwanted edits that remain visible in A', while preserving the user-selected ones, all without requiring a new demonstration pair for each edit combination. We perform extensive quantitative and qualitative evaluations along with user studies to demonstrate the efficacy of our approach.



### TWIST: Teacher-Weighted Initial-Noise Spherical Transform for Quality-Preserving Diversity in Distilled Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33895v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33895v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:16:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:16:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Shayan Baghayi-Nejad, Jeongsol Kim, Jong Chul Ye
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Timestep-distilled diffusion models generate high-quality images in one or a few steps, but often suffer from reduced mode coverage and limited diversity. Recent methods improve diversity by optimizing the initial noise at inference time, requiring costly backpropagation through the denoiser. Moreover, their diversity objectives typically repel generated samples without accounting for how the corresponding teacher model distributes its outputs. We introduce Teacher-Weighted Initial-noise Spherical Transform (TWIST), a lightweight network that transforms the low-frequency components of initial noise to improve diversity without inference-time optimization. TWIST uses a teacher-guided determinantal point process (DPP) objective to align the feature-space diversity of student samples with that of the teacher. By amortizing noise optimization into a single network, TWIST eliminates inference-time backpropagation while preserving the efficiency of few-step generation. On SDXL-Turbo, TWIST matches the diversity of MoNO at less than 25% of its inference cost, while achieving higher recall and better PickScore, ImageReward, and CLIPScore under matched compute budgets. On SDXL-Lightning, it achieves comparable recall at lower cost without the ImageReward degradation observed with MoNO.



### RelPRM: Grounding Visual Process Reward Models in Relational Evidence
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33903v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33903v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:24:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:24:09+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhaoxing Zhang, Ziqiu Gui, Cheng Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual process reward models (PRMs) can detect calculation errors yet assign high scores to steps that misread visual relations. Supervision based on subsequent answer success does not directly establish whether a step's relational claim holds. To strengthen visual-relation verification, we first build an automated pipeline that programmatically verifies each claim and identifies the required objects, relations, and image regions. It produces RelProcessTrain and RelProcessBench (RPB), which tests whether scores respond appropriately to relational evidence through controlled image edits and step rephrasings. Building on this pipeline, we further introduce RelPRM, a visual-relation-oriented method for process reward modeling with two training mechanisms. Relation-evidence self-distillation constructs views preserving necessary objects, relations, and dependencies, reducing the burden of finding decisive evidence in complex scenes and transferring reliable evidence-assisted judgments to full images. Relation-evidence-driven consistency training encourages appropriate score changes when relation edits reverse a step's truth, stability under irrelevant edits, and similar score changes across paraphrases. At inference, RelPRM directly scores the current step using only the original image and reasoning context. RelPRM achieves 66.2% on VisualProcessBench (VPB) and 68.4% on RPB, gains of 4.2 and 9.9 percentage points over VisualPRM-8B. These results show that training for visual relations improves both relational verification and step assessment across broader multimodal reasoning tasks.



### Tortho-Gaussian++: Suppressing Geometric Drift and Floaters for Robust Gravity-Aligned TDOM Generation in Large-Scale Aerial Scenes
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33905v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**

- **Authors**: Xiang Wang, Xin Wang, Zongqian Zhan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has demonstrated remarkable performance in aerial scene reconstruction, motivating its recent extension to true digital orthophoto maps (TDOMs) generation. We propose Tortho-Gaussian++, a novel 3DGS training framework that suppresses geometric drift and floaters and automatically prunes unreliable peripheral regions, coupled with a robust gravity-aligned TDOM rendering solution. First, we explore geometric support via viewing-ray-based geometric observability analysis, quantitatively characterizing the reliability for each Gaussian and subsequently pruning peripheral regions with particularly weak geometric support. Second, the optimizer state initialization is refined for newly created Gaussians during densification, intrinsically suppressing drift and floaters without external priors. Lastly, a robust gravity direction estimation solution is designed, combining image-derived gravity cues, camera-distribution priors and nadir views, to achieve a gravity-aligned orthogonal projection reference for TDOM rendering. Extensive experiments on multiple large-scale UAV (Unmanned Aerial Vehicle) datasets demonstrate that our Tortho-Gaussian++ outperforms vanilla 3DGS by 0.5-3.5 dB in average PSNR and yields comparable or superior performance to prior-guided 3DGS methods. Notably, it reduces the training time, VRAM and Gaussian number by 45.5%, 47.2%, and 58.6%, respectively. Qualitative evaluations confirm that our method effectively suppresses drift and floaters, while robust gravity direction estimation reduces tilt-induced facade artifacts in TDOMs.



### SEEK: Self-Evolution through Execution Knowledge for Agentic Robot Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33915v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33915v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:37:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:37:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Zeyang Guan, Qihao Zhao, Rui Gong, Zhang Haitian, Yalun Dai, Yangyu Huang, Lingao Xiao, Haozhe Du, Xuehui Yu, Ye Lu, Jianjun Gao, Ruoyu Wang, Haowen Bai, Hao Li, Scarlett Li, Kim-Hui Yap
- **Comment**: 17 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: General-purpose multimodal foundation models enable robots to adapt during deployment from observations and execution feedback, without task-specific demonstrations or training. However, the robot retains what it learns only in the model's working context. Once the task ends, it loses this execution knowledge, such as how to carry a bowl between two grippers. On the next attempt at the task, the robot may take just as long and repeat the same mistakes. To avoid this repetition, robots must retain execution knowledge across attempts. Yet replaying past actions is insufficient, because their effects depend on the current conditions. Procedures that determine actions from the current scene address this, but when they meet conditions that earlier attempts did not cover, revising them requires the measured effects of earlier actions. We therefore propose SEEK, an agentic framework that retains both, enabling robots to improve across attempts while keeping the underlying model fixed. To adapt retained knowledge to the current scene, SEEK adopts a Self-Evolving Programmatic Policy (SEPP) that encodes manipulation as executable procedures and determines each action from current observations. As the robot executes these procedures, SEEK records the measured effects of its actions, including those from failed attempts, in a Persistent Action-Effect Bank (PAEB). Where a retained procedure applies, it acts without a model request; where it meets a condition it does not cover, this evidence guides its revision. On RoboDojo, knowledge retained over ten attempts raises SEEK's average task score from 27.0 to 54.4 and its success rate from 19.7% to 48.0% across all 42 tasks. On 34 of these tasks with GPT-6.2 Astra, SEEK reaches 80.1 after five attempts, against 49.6 for the same model acting directly, and uses 77.6% fewer model requests. https://zeyangguan.github.io/SEEK-Agentic-Robot-Manipulation/



### Event-WAM: Learning to Act with Event-Derived Futures
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33917v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33917v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:37:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:37:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Jongseong Bae, Hyunwoo Kim, Yeongin Lee, Eubin So, Ha Young Kim
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World Action Models (WAMs) leverage future visual modeling for robotic action learning, with recent studies exploring alternative or complementary objectives beyond conventional RGB prediction. We introduce Event-WAM, which incorporates event-derived visual changes as auxiliary future supervision alongside RGB. Event-WAM constructs temporally structured event representations from RGB demonstrations and encodes them using a frozen pretrained RGB video VAE, without training an event-specific VAE. Joint RGB--Event latent flow matching provides appearance- and change-oriented supervision without requiring event observations or explicit future generation at inference. Across simulated benchmarks, Event-WAM maintains comparable performance under standard conditions, while robustness gains under perturbations are benchmark-dependent, including improvements on LIBERO-Plus. Real-world evaluations further demonstrate improved manipulation success under both standard and unseen perturbation conditions. Further analyses show that policy improvements are not consistently accompanied by higher future prediction fidelity, suggesting the potential of change-oriented supervision for action learning.



### UPP-Gauss: Ultrasound Propagation Physics-informed Gaussian Splatting for B-mode Novel-View Synthesis
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33920v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33920v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:39:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:39:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Guil Jung, Seok-Hwan Oh, Myeong-Gee Kim, Young-Min Kim, Hyeon-Jik Lee, Sang-Yun Kim, Hyeok-Jun Kwon, Hyuk-Sool Kwon, Hyeon-Min Bae
- **Comment**: 5 pages, 3 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Accurate novel-view synthesis of B-mode ultrasound images requires modeling acoustic propagation through tissue and the resulting appearance variations across probe poses. We propose Ultrasound Propagation Physics-informed Gaussian Splatting (UPP-Gauss), which integrates these acoustic interactions into an explicit Gaussian representation. UPP-Gauss constructs acoustic maps by sampling three 3D Gaussian fields using the probe pose and acoustic beam propagation direction, then combines these maps according to ultrasound propagation to synthesize B-mode images. Across three acoustically diverse datasets, UPP-Gauss achieves the best reconstruction quality with efficient rendering, improving PSNR over the best baseline on each dataset by 1.43 dB on average. These results demonstrate accurate and efficient B-mode novel-view synthesis through explicit Gaussian modeling of ultrasound propagation.



### iMFPO: Improving Mean Flow Policy Optimization with Drift-Aware Velocity Alignment for Embodied Control
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33922v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33922v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:43:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:43:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Lisiyu Pan, Ting Huang, Zeyu Zhang, Hao Tang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Flow-based generative policies offer expressive action modeling for embodied control, yet achieving efficient one-step generation under reward-driven policy optimization remains challenging. In particular, evolving action distributions during online reinforcement learning may introduce inconsistencies between instantaneous-velocity learning and interval-averaged action generation. To address this challenge, we propose iMFPO, a one-step generative policy optimization framework with decoupled velocity learning and drift-aware velocity alignment. A shared interval-conditioned actor supports instantaneous-velocity supervision and interval-averaged action generation. JVP-corrected velocity regression is jointly optimized with reward-driven policy objectives, with action-level guidance from a frozen PPO teacher during training. At inference, iMFPO directly generates actions through interval-averaged velocity prediction, achieving one-step control without iterative flow integration. Across Go2, G1, H1, and Spot, zero-latent evaluation yields mean returns of 35.69, 34.44, 416.23, and 29.86. On Go2, action-generation latency drops from 15.57 ms to 0.41 ms relative to 64-step RFPO. Code: https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/iMFPO. Website: https://aigeeksgroup.github.io/iMFPO.



### Towards 360$^\circ$ Embodied UAV Tracking Without Turning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33934v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33934v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:54:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:54:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Bingyu Li, Wu Du, Zhiyuan Zhao, Junyu Gao, Xuelong Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Conventional embodied UAV tracking couples target observability with heading control because targets may leave the field of view of forward-facing cameras. We investigate a complementary paradigm that uses full-spherical perception to follow designated targets without active heading adjustment. To support this setting, we introduce 360UAV-ETrack, a simulation benchmark comprising 2,652 sequences, over 1.73 million synchronized observations, eight tracking tasks, and 150 held-out closed-loop scenarios. We propose 360UAV-TVLA, a panoramic vision--language--action (VLA) framework integrating persistent visual referring (PVR), compressed temporal memory (CTM), and geometry-aligned trajectory supervision (GTS) for instance identification, motion reasoning, and three-dimensional control. Experiments across two VLA backbones show consistent gains over standard fine-tuning, with normalized average tracking frames increasing from 22.56\% to 35.28\% for $π_0$ and from 25.68\% to 33.64\% for $π_{0.5}$. Our benchmark further reveals a substantial gap between offline imitation accuracy and closed-loop tracking reliability, establishing a challenging testbed for future research https://libingyu01.github.io/360UAV-ETrack_360UAV-TVLA/



### BayesPath: Reliability-Aware Bayesian Reasoning for Whole-Slide Pathology Agents
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33935v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33935v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:54:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:54:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Bryan Wong, Xun Xu, Jongwoo Kim, Huazhu Fu, Nancy F. Chen, Mun Yong Yi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Diagnosing whole-slide images (WSIs) requires hypothesis-driven reasoning: pathologists form a differential diagnosis, seek discriminative evidence, weigh its reliability, and stop once it suffices. However, existing training-free WSI agents lack an explicit framework for such reasoning. They typically use a large language model (LLM) to aggregate the region descriptions of a multimodal LLM (MLLM), without a belief over the candidates or regard for the descriptions' reliability. Consequently, a repeated model error counts as independent evidence for an incorrect diagnosis. We introduce BayesPath, a training-free WSI agent that casts the pathologist's Scan-Look-Weigh-Decide process as reliability-aware Bayesian reasoning. An explicit belief over the candidates is updated with each observation through a reliability-aware likelihood and guides where to look next and when to stop. This likelihood corrects for the models' answer bias, discounts correlated observations, and captures diagnostic confusions estimated without labels. The updates form an interpretable evidence trail of how each observation shapes the belief. On five MultiPathQA tasks spanning slide classification and slide VQA, BayesPath achieves the highest average accuracy among training-free agents, 58.6% against 34.1% for the next best, with large gains on slide classification. On identical observations, Bayesian updating outperforms LLM aggregation on average, even with a larger LLM. BayesPath's belief is also better calibrated than naive Bayes, so uncertain cases can be deferred to pathologists. Code is available at https://github.com/bryanwong17/BayesPath



### TAT-Det: A Dataset for Testicular Appendage Torsion Detection and Segmentation in Ultrasound Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33940v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33940v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 07:57:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 07:57:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Ying Jiang, Juntao Jiang, Linxuan Fan, Lingling Li, Yaqi Wang, Xiuzhen Yang, Yong Liu, Jiangning Zhang, Jingjing Ye
- **Comment**: 17 pages, 9 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Background: Testicular appendage torsion (TAT) is the leading cause of acute scrotal pain in prepubertal children. Yet, its diagnosis remains challenging due to nonspecific clinical manifestations and the absence of standardized datasets for pediatric acute scrotal emergencies.   Results: We present TAT-Det, an expert-annotated ultrasound dataset specifically designed for TAT. TAT-Det comprises 2,600 ultrasound images from 1,203 patients collected at the Children's Hospital, Zhejiang University School of Medicine. Each TAT-positive image is accompanied by lesion-level bounding boxes for detection and pixel-level segmentation masks for fine-grained delineation. Annotations were produced through a multi-stage workflow involving independent annotation by board-certified pediatric sonographers, consensus review, and refinement. Baseline benchmarks for object detection and semantic segmentation using representative deep learning models are further provided, with YOLO11m achieving the best detection performance and RWKV-UNet the best segmentation performance.   Conclusions: TAT-Det addresses the lack of publicly available resources for pediatric emergency ultrasound and establishes a foundation for developing and evaluating artificial intelligence methods for automated TAT analysis.



### A Computational Model of Continuous Recognition Memory for Natural Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33947v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33947v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:08:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:08:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Sheng Zhao, Yijin Wang, Bei Xiao, Yuhao Zhu
- **Comment**: 18 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recognizing an image we have seen before is a basic function of human visual memory. Understanding human continuous recognition memory (CRM) can help machine vision systems predict which images people are likely to remember and generate more memorable images. It can also inform the diagnosis of memory impairments and the development of interventions to improve memory. This paper proposes the first computational model for CRM. The model captures three key components of continuous recognition memory (CRM): image encoding, memory decay, and decision making. It encodes each image as a distinct random variable in a perceptual space. As the memory of an image fades, the distribution of its random variable drifts within that space. To decide whether the current image has been seen before, the observer retrieves samples from the distributions associated with previously encoded images and compares them with the current image. Our model predicts human observers' trial-by-trial responses in CRM tasks within the variability observed across human participants. Our model is parameter efficient, requiring only four shared parameters and two parameters per observer. It can accurately predict the memorability of an image no worse than prior methods that require millions of parameters.



### Where to Approximate: Understanding and Accelerating Looped Multi-View Reconstruction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33948v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33948v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:09:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:09:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Siyuan Zhang, Yuhan Chen, Yangyang Ning, Nan Wang, Jun Yan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Looped multi-view reconstruction refines scene features by repeatedly applying a shared transformer block. Making these updates cheaper requires knowing how an approximation at one step affects the rest of the refinement. We study this with paired interventions in Déjà View and stacked reconstruction transformers, separating the error an approximation injects, how that error propagates, and how it affects the output. In Déjà View, approximation errors are attenuated more strongly than matched random perturbations under a scale-adjusted state-error measure, and a local Jacobian decomposition separates the contribution of channel gating from the response to spatial error structure. Spatial approximations made early benefit from subsequent refinement, whereas cross-step reuse depends on which quantity is cached and how reuse steps are spaced. At equal final norm, approximation errors change the predicted rotations 1.55 times as much as random errors. These findings guide our proposed method, which reduces spatial resolution and pools keys and values early in refinement, then interleaves attention-output reuse with full attention, and is adapted through lightweight self-distillation. Across five reconstruction benchmarks, the balanced configuration runs $1.63\times$ faster than the official 16-step Déjà View model with a 1.86-point reduction in average pose AUC@$3^\circ$. At matched latency, it exceeds the interpolated curve of a trained baseline using full refinement blocks by 4.73 points in AUC@$3^\circ$ and improves the corresponding geometry metrics.



### RefMix: Expanding Endpoint Proposals for Diversity-Preserving Reward Fine-Tuning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33957v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33957v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:18:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:18:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Jaemin Kim, Christian Simon, Dongseok Shim, Wei-Yao Wang, Qiyu Wu, Masato Ishii, Shusuke Takahashi, Jong Chul Ye, Yuki Mitsufuji
- **Comment**: Project page: https://kjm981995.github.io/RefMix
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Online forward matching aligns flow-based generative models by repeatedly generating, rewarding, and fitting model-produced endpoints. However, this behavior creates an on-policy feedback loop: as high-reward modes become increasingly favored, alternative modes become less likely to appear in subsequent training pools, progressively reducing visual diversity. We introduce RefMix, a reference-assisted endpoint proposal strategy that addresses this limitation by injecting alternative candidates into the training pool while retaining the original task reward and forward matching objective. In particular, RefMix uses a reference model during early high-noise steps to expose alternative visual modes, followed by the current reward-tuned model for endpoint completion. Across state-of-the-art online forward matching methods, RefMix consistently improves visual diversity while preserving task reward performance. Controlled inference experiments further demonstrate that training-time endpoint exposure is critical to improve reward--diversity trade-offs, beyond the impact of reference-assisted inference. By rethinking how training endpoints are proposed, RefMix achieves up to 44% higher diversity over forward matching baselines at comparable reward levels.



### Where Does Free-Text 3D Grounding Fail? An Error Analysis of Report-Sentence Segmentation in Chest CT
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33958v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33958v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:20:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:20:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Abdullah Arif Sheikh, Kamalaker Dadi, Raju S. Bapi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Free-text 3D grounding, segmenting in a CT volume the region described by one sentence of the radiology report, could link reports to images at scale, but it is unclear where current models fail. We analyse the publicly released VoxTell model on all 381 findings of the 200-scan ReXGroundingCT validation set, where it grounds 53.5% of findings. Its failures are rarely about which lesion or side is meant. No miss lies on the side opposite to the one named, and findings are almost never confused with one another. Instead, predictions are too large, land on a neighbouring lesion on the correct side, or are missing for small findings, and diffuse findings are rarely grounded. Removing location words halves performance, to the level of label-only prompting, and anatomical rules derived from the sentence never improve the full-sentence output. The same pattern holds on the hidden 300-scan test set. Further progress therefore depends on extent estimation and small-lesion sensitivity rather than on anatomical post-processing.



### Targeted Adaptation of World-Action Models via Interaction and Motion Heads
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33963v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33963v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:28:00+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:28:00+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuze Zhu, Jacky Li, Yusen Luo, Ken Jiankun Zheng, Chancharik Mitra, Trevor Darrell, Roei Herzig
- **Comment**: Project page: https://headlight-wam.github.io/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World-Action Models (WAMs) are a promising new paradigm of robot manipulation policies that jointly generate future video and actions. Successful manipulation requires both identifying where to interact and modeling how the robot and objects move. Yet standard adaptation methods, such as full fine-tuning and LoRA, do not explicitly select parameters aligned with these manipulation-specific capabilities. Motivated by this challenge, we discover two functionally specialized families of attention heads: Next-Interaction Heads, which localize upcoming grasp-and-release targets, and Motion Heads, which track movement across frames. Furthermore, we introduce HeadLight, a parameter-efficient refinement method that jointly adapts Next-Interaction Heads and Motion Heads. On RoboTwin tasks unseen during fine-tuning, HeadLight achieves 62.28% success, compared with 57.89% for full fine-tuning and 58.66% for LoRA, while updating only 1.1% of parameters. Inference-time ablations further validate the functional importance of these heads: ablating Motion Heads reduces manipulation success from 57.89% to 0.94%, while ablating Next-Interaction Heads lowers it to 40.61%, both substantially more than ablating equally many random heads. Finally, we show that our approach is effective across multiple WAM architectures and real-robot manipulation settings.



### Embodied-VGGT: Preserving Reliable Local Geometry for Long-Sequence Reconstruction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33964v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33964v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:28:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:28:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiheng Han, Ziang Chen, Zhuyong Fu, Yiqing Yin, Nan Ma
- **Comment**: 18 pages,8+3 ref+7 sup,6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long sequence reconstruction must correct accumulated drift while preserving locally consistent geometry. In robotics and autonomous driving, embodied acquisition provides recurring platform- environment geometry and repeated observations of scene structure and platform motion. We present Embodied-VGGT, which exploits these cues to establish reliable local relationships and selectively preserve them during global refinement. Memory-Augmented Grounded Chunks (MAGC) builds on Scal3R's global context memory and uses relative scale references derived from predicted ground geometry to regularize test-time memory updates, encouraging cross-chunk consistency while limiting changes to reliable local geometry. Superpoint Alignment (SA) and Cross-Loop Optimization (CL) jointly address the robust establishment and preservation of geometric relationships. SA balances ground and non-ground evidence across spatial regions to estimate inter-chunk translation. CL uses geometric and motion consistency across interleaved windows to select coherent chunk groups, each receiving a shared Sim(3) correction that preserves its internal relative transformations during global optimization. Experiments on five public driving benchmarks demonstrate consistent and substantial improvements in long sequence reconstruction. On KITTI Odometry, Embodied-VGGT achieves a mean absolute trajectory error of 7.30 m and a Chamfer distance of 2.67 m, reducing these errors by 46.8% and 23.3% compared with the state-of-the-art baseline under same evaluation protocol. To facilitate reproducibility, we released our code: https://github.com/Ang-Sheep/Embodied-VGGT.



### FasterGRN: Reducing the Refinement Redundancy for Accelerating Generative Refinement Networks
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33982v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33982v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:41:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:41:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziqian Zhang, Pengcheng Wang, Yulong Chen, Yaxing Wang, Senmao Li, Kai Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Departing from the paradigm of diffusion and autoregressive models, Generative Refinement Networks (GRN) enables high-quality image generation through iterative refinements. However, this repeated processing over the entire visual representations results in substantial computation overhead. Furthermore, the binary representations and global refinement mechanism in GRN disable the direct application of acceleration strategies developed for other generative models. To accelerate GRN model, we first observe several key properties by analyzing the entire generative process. That includes the stage characteristic, dual branch similarity and more importantly, the $\textbf{refinement redundancy}$. Motivated by the observations, we present $\textbf{FasterGRN}$, a training-free acceleration method for the Generative Refinement Network. $\textbf{FasterGRN}$ first divides the GRN into the semantic establishment stage and detail refinement stage according to its semantic establishment. For alleviating the refinement redundancy mainly resulting from the detail refinement stage, we propose Adaptive Retention Expansion ($\textbf{ARE}$) and Selective Token Freezing ($\textbf{STF}$) to shrink refinement steps and reduce token computations, respectively. In the experiments on GenEval and DPG-Bench benchmarks, our method $\textbf{FasterGRN}$ achieves a $2.03\times$ sampling speedup while largely preserving image generation performance. Our evaluation on COCO datasets additionally demonstrates that $\textbf{FasterGRN}$ even outperforms the original GRN in image fidelity.



### geo-eval-harness: An Evaluation Harness for Geospatial Foundation Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33990v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33990v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:45:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:45:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Nils Lehmann, Caleb Robinson, Isaac Corley, Jennifer Marcus, Inbal Becker-Reshef, Juan M. Lavista Ferres, Hannah Kerner
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Geospatial foundation model (GFM) research papers are plagued by inconsistent evaluation settings (e.g., different pre-processing and probes), making it difficult to separate performance from evaluation choices. We propose geo-eval-harness}, an open-source framework for controlled model comparisons. We use this harness to evaluate 100 GFMs and general vision models on 6 classification and 14 segmentation datasets, using multiple probes on frozen or low-rank-adapted encoders. We show that under controlled evaluation, general vision backbones are competitive on RGB classification and high-resolution segmentation, but GFMs shine on multispectral tasks. When we compare any two of the top-ten classification models on a single dataset, their 95% confidence intervals overlap 73% of the time. We show that bigger models are not always better: inference FLOPs correlate weakly with segmentation performance. Moreover, translating inference compute into large-area mapping workloads shows how small accuracy differences have outsized deployment cost differences: with frozen encoders on SpaceNet2, a 0.9-point mIoU difference corresponds to 5.9x the compute and days of additional projected GPU time. Our findings stress the importance of comparing models under explicit and fixed settings, and reporting uncertainty and deployment compute alongside accuracy. Our geo-eval-harness makes controlled experiments easy, fast, and fully reproducible. We release the code, configurations, and results at https://github.com/torchgeo/geo-eval-harness.



### QK-Bench: Benchmarking Quantitative Kinematic Understanding for Dynamic Spatial Intelligence
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33991v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33991v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 08:45:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 08:45:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Lennox Wei, Jiaqi Yao, Shixin Sun, Yuanzheng Zhang, Jianhua Han, Meng Tian, Shaohui Liu, Wangmeng Zuo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Spatial intelligence concerns both scene structure and its temporal evolution. Yet a semantically correct description can conceal a quantitative error: recognizing that objects are approaching does not determine their closing speed or whether they will meet. Such errors are critical in collision avoidance, trajectory tracking, and reactive grasping, where actions depend on motion magnitude, timing, and relative geometry. We study quantitative kinematic understanding in multimodal large language models (MLLMs): resolving motion magnitude, rate, reference-frame dependence, and future spatial evolution from visual evidence.
