For challenge 2), VT-CoRe imposes latent-space semantic alignment on registered representations through a self-distillation architecture and further proposes temporal variation modeling to constrain representation trajectories over time. Experiments on 8 downstream tasks across 16 benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and applicability of VT-CoRe across pixel-level, frame-level, video-level, and vision-based robotic learning scenarios. Complementary representation analysis shows that VT-CoRe learns a more structured latent space for variation-aware temporal correspondence learning.



### AnyCam4D: Ray-Based 4D Gaussian Rendering for Arbitrary Camera Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34223v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34223v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 11:47:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 11:47:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Sascha Kirch, Rafael Pagés, Jan Ondřej, Clara Pérez-Molina, Sergio Martin
- **Comment**: Project page: https://sascha-kirch.github.io/anycam4d/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Real cameras are not pinholes, yet most dynamic Gaussian splatting assumes they are. Footage is therefore undistorted first, losing field of view or detail, especially with wide-angle and fisheye cameras. Static Gaussian splatting has largely solved this with ray-based rendering, which suits any camera. Native 4D Gaussians, which model motion in space-time, cannot use it directly: each Gaussian's orientation and size cannot be recovered reliably from its covariance. We present AnyCam4D, which trains native 4D Gaussians on original footage by ray-based rasterization, preserving field of view and resolution at about the per-step cost of standard projection. Our key contribution is a numerically stable way to obtain orientation and size: we condition a square root of the 4D covariance. Trained models render through other cameras, with rolling shutter, depth of field or chromatic aberration. With a synthetic 170-degree fisheye camera, training on original images improves peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) by 3.0-8.1 dB. On real footage, AnyCam4D has lower perceptual error than the standard projection adapted to each camera, at comparable PSNR and without camera-specific derivations. With the synthetic camera rotated 60-80 degrees away from the capture rig's view, it leads by 6.2 dB against ground truth.



### EgoMyo: Physical Hand State Estimation from Egocentric Video and Wrist Electromyography
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34224v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34224v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 11:49:01+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 11:49:01+00:00
- **Authors**: Guanglong Sun, Kanglei Zhou, Ziteng Wang, Nana Wang, Hengkai Tan, Xiaolong Liu, Hang Su, Yi Zhong, Jun Zhu
- **Comment**: Website: https://egomyo.github.io/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Physical hand interaction depends on configuration, contact, and force, yet nearly identical appearances can conceal different contact states and exerted forces. We therefore formulate physical hand state estimation from egocentric video and wrist surface electromyography (sEMG), combining visual geometry with neuromuscular evidence to jointly recover these coupled components. To support this task, we introduce EgoMyo, a benchmark spanning 5.75M video frames, 50 participants, 34 contact configurations, and repeated donnings, synchronizing these inputs with 3D hand pose and with contact and normal force registered onto the hand surface from bare-hand pressure. Controlled analysis reveals that vision resolves spatial structure while sEMG better estimates force magnitude, that sEMG's gains concentrate in regions where vision is least reliable, and that contact localization limits force accuracy. Guided by these findings, we propose MyoBridge, which makes the hand region the unit of vision-sEMG fusion, lets each region gate its own share of sEMG, and distributes regional force magnitudes only over predicted contact vertices at inference. On held-out trials, MyoBridge reduces pose and force error by 39.8% and 18.5% over the strongest baseline and achieves the lowest pose error on unseen sessions and subjects.



### LiveRelight: Sub-Second Global Illumination Editing for Gaussian Interiors
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34226v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34226v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 11:51:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 11:51:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Noah Rothenberger, Dor Verbin, Serge Belongie, Bernhard Kerbl
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Relighting reconstructed 3D scenes remains challenging due to the complex nature of light transport: slow or approximate visual feedback restricts the editor's ability to fine-tune the results to satisfaction. We present a hybrid relighting approach that enables interactive, high-fidelity updates of direct and indirect indoor illumination without per-edit optimization. Our approach permits the editor to make a wide range of relighting edits at room-scale, with near-instant feedback. Our key insight is to precompute geometric light exchange rather than illumination responses, retaining an inexpensive transport solve that accommodates changes in emission and surface reflectance. We evaluate direct illumination on reconstructed 2D Gaussians using visibility-aware sampling of one-sided emitters, while propagating diffuse interreflection through sparse radiosity on a triangle proxy. A sparse projection fitted through Gaussian alpha compositing transfers the proxy solution to the reconstruction. Our formulation further supports fast fixture transformations and emitter insertion by recomputing light source injection. Across eight relighting conditions of our synthetic test suite, our sub-second edits improve relighting fidelity over prior path-traced editing by an average of 5\,dB PSNR, while reducing editing latency by up to three orders of magnitude. Qualitative results further demonstrate realistic, interactive relighting of real-world captures.



### Weakly Conditioned Diffusion Model Augmentation for Prostate Segmentation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34230v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34230v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 11:52:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 11:52:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Chibike Ugbam, Muhammad Bilal, Jia Wu, Shafaq Murad, Rizwan Qureshi, Hazrat Ali
- **Comment**: 15 pages, preprint
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Deep learning models for prostate MRI segmentation often struggle to generalize when trained on limited heterogeneous data, leading to boundary errors and over-segmentation. We present an augmentation framework using mask-conditioned synthetic and diverse T2-weighted MRI volumes to improve boundary precision and reduce over-segmentation. We employ a 2.5D weakly mask-conditioned denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM) via channel concatenation. The diffusion model conditions on stacks of adjacent binary masks from training folds. The model generates T2 volumes consistent with the conditioning anatomy while diversifying tissue appearance. We evaluate the proposed approach on two cohorts of data: (i) the LUND probe cohort (n=432) for strategy selection and training nnU-Net from scratch with a 23% synthetic data mix, and (ii) the Medical Segmentation Decathlon (MSD) prostate cohort (n=32) evaluated with a 20% synthetic data mix. For the LUND cohort, we found that training an nnU-Net model from scratch with a 23% synthetic data mix reduced the mean false-positive voxels per case from 11,102 (baseline) to 10,757. On the MSD cohort, the augmented model with a 20% synthetic mix achieved a mean cross-validated Dice of 0.9094, improving over the baseline (0.8935) by 1.59 percentage points and reducing mean false-positive voxels by 23.1% (4,201 to 3,229). Experimental results on both these cohorts suggest that mask-conditioned DDPM augmentation can reduce over-segmentation errors and improve boundary adherence in data-constrained settings.



### OS-Omni: Benchmarking Generalist Computer-Using Agents on Long-Horizon Workflows Across Platforms
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34232v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34232v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 11:55:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 11:55:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Hui Shen, Yunta Hsieh, Jianing Ma, Zhuoran Yin, Zhuhan Bao, Ziyuan Liu, Qi Han, Xiuqi Xu, Xianbang Wang, Wenhao Chai, Sihan Xu, Chenyang Zhao, Yanheng Shang, Dongfu Jiang, Rui Sun, Xingzhi Li, Chenqi Yin, Zesen Zhao, Boyuan Zheng, Jisen Li, Xiaoxia Wu, Ruoyan Zhang, Ruiyao Liu, Jing Xiong, He Xiao, Nan Zheng, Hangxiang Chen, Shengyang Tao, Deyang Luorong, Yifeng Liu, Jingxuan Zhang, Chaofan Tao, Yuren Wang, Jiaqi Mo, Rui Hu, Xing Wei, Xiaofeng Liu, Xin Xu, Han Wu, Mengyao Wen, Guobin Zhang, Shatong Zhu, Hei Ting Una Chan, Sicheng Chen, Kai Zhang, Mengkang Hu, Zhongwei Wan, Xin Wang, Chuanyang Zheng, Ziheng Zhang, Hengyuan Zhang, Zunhai Su, Kanzhi Cheng, Dehan Kong, Yian Wang, Hanrong Zhang, Jiawei Lu, Yifan Zhang, Junnan Li, Changyi Yang, Jintao Qu, Wenyao Gao, Jiaxuan Luo, Jiaxin Deng, Yuhao Chen, Xuesong Ye, Jingwen Gu, Yichi Zhang, Qun Lou, Shen Yan, Ben Athiwaratkun, Qiushi Sun, Mi Zhang, Ping Luo, Wenhu Chen, Ngai Wong
- **Comment**: NeurIPS 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Computer-using agents are increasingly expected to complete realistic user goals rather than isolated GUI actions. However, existing benchmarks typically isolate web, mobile, or desktop environments, leaving it unclear whether a single generalist agent can execute long-horizon workflows that span multiple operating systems and persistent application states. We introduce OS-Omni, a live cross-platform benchmark and execution environment for evaluating computer-using agents across Windows, Ubuntu, macOS, iOS, Android, and Web environments. OS-Omni is released in versioned waves of 300 tasks each; this paper focuses on the first release, v1, which contains 48 tasks per platform and 12 cross-platform workflows that pair a desktop with an Android phone. OS-Omni provides reproducible initialization, platform-specific adapters, trajectory logging, and executable final-state evaluators, and includes controlled service-backed applications for workflows involving account, cart, budget, calendar, message, and other persistent state. Human studies show that OS-Omni tasks require 67.54 interaction steps, substantially exceeding OSWorld references of 15.0 steps. On v1, the strongest evaluated agent, Claude Opus 5.5, reaches 68.5% average success, trailing the 77.3% human reference by 8.8 points; the gap is uneven across platforms, with frontier models exceeding humans on Linux but trailing them by more than 25 points on iOS and macOS. These results indicate that current agents remain far from platform-robust, long-horizon computer use, especially when success requires sustained state tracking and durable final-state changes.



### VibeVideoAgent: Closing the Perceptual Loop for Code-Driven Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34237v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34237v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 11:59:44+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 11:59:44+00:00
- **Authors**: Xuanyu Zheng, Luoyuan Zhang, Yancheng Long, Haoyang Xu, Muxi Diao, Yuzhe Chen, Kaiyu Jiang, Bin Wen, Tingting Gao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Code-driven video generation asks a coding agent to write and run rendering programs. This gives exact control over typography, data visualizations, synchronized audio, and multi-shot scenes, and it leaves an editable project behind. A program that exits cleanly can still render overlapping labels, a camera aimed away from its subject, broken object interactions, or narration that drifts out of sync, and none of these raise a runtime error. Feeding rendered frames and audio back into the coding session bloats its long-horizon context, while critiquing only the finished full-resolution render turns early design mistakes into costly rewrites. We introduce VibeVideoAgent, a training-free framework that lets coding agents inspect their drafts throughout production. Multimodal perception runs in short-lived contexts kept apart from the coder and returns compact, grounded Findings. Seven primitives operate on a unified multi-clock representation of assets and drafts: they explore media from coarse to fine, verify quoted requirements with evidence from every required modality, compare a draft with a reference or an earlier revision on separate clocks, and measure stream signals locally. A lightweight staged protocol has the agent test its riskiest decision on a minimal preview first. On all 239 VibeVideoBench tasks, with matched agent runtimes and a fixed perception model, VibeVideoAgent improves holistic audiovisual quality (L3) for Claude Opus 5.5 (4.03 -> 4.43, while L2 rubric compliance rises from 98.87% to 99.35%), GPT-6 Astra (4.03 -> 4.27), and Claude Sonnet 5.5 (3.97 -> 4.10), with the largest gains in audio-visual synchronization (up to +0.56). Controlled ablations show that each component contributes, and trajectory audits show that the granularity of code edits governs the trade-off between rubric adherence and perceptual quality.



### Analemma $\infty$: A Temporal Benchmark for Geospatial Foundation Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34240v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34240v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:03:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:03:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Alex López-Cifuentes, Laura Martínez-Ferrer, Juan Ignacio Bravo Pérez-Villar
- **Comment**: 30 pages, 13 figures, 17 tables. Includes supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Satellite time series capture how land surfaces change over time. Pixel-wise temporal foundation models read a year of satellite observations per pixel, yet public benchmarks lack a temporal axis or subsample it to a few dates. We introduce Analemma, a temporal benchmark of six datasets that carry every 10 and 20 m Sentinel-2 band and a time series spanning a growing season, completed where their releases omit geometry, dates, or cloud masks. One frozen-encoder protocol evaluates all models across label ratios and three seeds, with two baselines without pretraining. The TESSERA model ranks first overall. A from-scratch model on monthly composites outranks every foundation model except OlmoEarth v1.3-Base, suggesting that temporal input explains most of the performance. Three evaluation choices left unstated by the source publications each shift TESSERA v1 by 7.6 to 18.6 mIoU, against top-two gaps of 0.6 to 2.8. Data, harness and leaderboard will be publicly released soon.



### Detector Operating Behavior Inference via Data-Space Sampling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34252v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34252v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:14:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:14:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Stefan Becker, Simon Weiss, Wolfgang Hübner
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A detector scores candidate detections, but the recall and false alarms at a given confidence threshold depend on the input distribution. We introduce Detector Operating Behavior Inference (DOBI), a second-level inference task: estimating a fixed detector's threshold-dependent, distribution-level operating response from the internal evidence of a single unlabeled image. DOBI treats the detector's threshold response, i.e. recall, false positives, and precision across thresholds, as a latent behavioral state under the current data condition. Controlled data-space sampling probes this state space offline, and a lightweight monitor learns the inverse map from one image to this state, whose single functional output supports multiple operating policies. In a controlled proof of concept with a fixed YOLOv10-Nano person detector and COCO-derived degradations, predicted PR behavior reduces mean absolute deviation from 0.128 for an image-independent mean-behavior prior to 0.054, and predicted functional distances track empirical regime distances (Spearman 0.96). From one unlabeled image, the inferred behavior selects operating points as well as a non-deployable lookup that knows the true regime (macro-regime $F_1$ 0.6604 for both, versus 0.6407 under pristine calibration; oracle 0.6620). On this discrete benchmark, regime-supervised curve lookups are strong controls. Pre-specified continuous and compositional probing shows where functional inference matters: on unseen combinations, the functional decoder has a lower mean curve error than a template lookup, increasingly with distance from the training conditions, whereas the lookup is more accurate for interpolation. These results establish the feasibility of single-image inference of distribution-level detector behavior over the probed conditions. Source code is available at https://github.com/beckerio/dobi



### TORE: Token-Wise Taylor Order Selection via Decomposed Residual Error Estimation for Diffusion Acceleration
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34261v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34261v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:24:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:24:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Lei Tang, Baoquan Zhang, Yakun Zhang, Yunming Ye
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Diffusion models excel at image and video generation, but iterative denoising incurs substantial inference latency. Recent token-adaptive acceleration methods select Taylor prediction orders for the full-model residual using shallow-layer proxy scores. However, candidate orders are evaluated on these proxy features but used to predict the full-model residual, creating a proxy--target mismatch: the proxy-preferred order may not minimize residual prediction error. To address this mismatch, we propose \textbf{TORE} (\textbf{T}aylor \textbf{O}rder selection via \textbf{R}esidual \textbf{E}rror estimation), which selects token-wise Taylor orders based on estimated prediction errors for the full-model residual. Yet direct error evaluation requires future ground-truth residuals, which are unavailable during order selection. TORE therefore estimates prediction error by analyzing how errors arise in Taylor extrapolation. Specifically, it decomposes prediction error into truncation error and finite-difference derivative error, then estimates both contributions from cached residuals to preselect token-wise orders for upcoming skip steps. Extensive experiments show that TORE achieves $4.31\times$ speedup on FLUX.1-dev and $4.18\times$ on Wan2.1 while maintaining high generation quality and fidelity.



### Semantic State Reading for Joint Image-Semantic Diffusion Transformers
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34279v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34279v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:36:00+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:36:00+00:00
- **Authors**: Guanxi Lai, Zixuan Li, Zhiwei Li, Qi Li
- **Comment**: 28 pages, 14 figures, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pretrained visual representations improve diffusion-transformer training, yet exploiting both global context and local spatial structure requires deciding which semantic states to model and how to access them. We introduce Semantic State Reading (SSR), which jointly denoises image latents, a global CLS token encoding image-level context, and a compact patch state preserving spatial correspondence. SSR couples this global-local modeling with distinct reading paths: local states are fused with corresponding image tokens at the input, while independent projections inject the current global state into every transformer block. Both semantic scopes thus remain accessible throughout denoising. Ablations show complementary benefits from local-state modeling and repeated global reading, and semantic-trajectory interventions alter image layout under a fixed class label. On ImageNet $256 \times 256$, SiT-XL/2 with SSR reaches or surpasses the reference FIDs of SiT, REPA, and Representation Entanglement for Generation (REG) with $100\times$, $40\times$, and $2\times$ fewer training iterations, respectively. SSR achieves unguided FID 1.78 at 2M iterations and FID 1.34 with classifier-free guidance alone. Combining classifier-free guidance with autoguidance yields FID 1.31 at 1M iterations, achieving the best FID in our main comparison table.



### ReMeDi: Single-Image PBR Material Estimation via Reparameterization between Metal and Dielectric Hypotheses
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34284v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34284v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:37:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:37:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Fan Fei, Xin Huang, Yixin Zhu, Jiawei Lu, Xin Yang, Jinglin Liang, Ruining Tang, Yifei Feng, Boxin Shi, Chunchao Guo
- **Comment**: 27 pages, 16 figures. Project page: https://costrice.github.io/remedi/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Physically based rendering (PBR) material estimation facilitates relighting, material editing, and 3D content creation. As the reflectance of a surface heavily depends on whether it is a metal or a dielectric, producing any map that determines albedo involves a decision on metalness, which we call the "intended metalness" of the map. Rendering maps with mismatched intended metalness yields physically implausible or misinterpreted materials. The metallic--roughness workflow makes metalness explicit and thus encourages base color to match it, but does not enforce this match, especially for automated methods directly predicting each map. Therefore, we propose ReMeDi, which enforces the match through two components. First, we reparameterize base color as a metalness-weighted blend of its two variants, estimated under the all-metal and all-dielectric hypothesis, respectively. Second, as available supervision cannot teach a single network both hypotheses at every pixel, we restrict each of two separate networks to one hypothesis, so that each variant is ensured to follow its hypothesis. Besides enforcing the match, this also improves each estimated material map. Experiments show that ReMeDi outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both material accuracy and relighting fidelity.



### Rehearse in 3D, Render in 2D: Physically Consistent and Editable Robot World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34288v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34288v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:41:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:41:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Guangyu Liu, Hanzhang Wang, Huijia Zhang, Hanbei Zhan, Linjie Hu, Bin Xiao, Xuan Zhou, Dong Zhang, Hanwei Liu
- **Comment**: Includes supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Embodied world models aim to predict future interactions that are both visually realistic and physically consistent. Yet explicit 3D motion prediction alone does not guarantee that object structure and contact relationships are preserved. Beyond motion prediction, 3D representations provide an explicit state space for imposing structural and physical constraints. Building on this insight, we introduce RehearsalWorld, an editable robot world model driven by 3D Interaction Rehearsal (3DIR). Given an initial RGB-D observation and robot actions, Interaction-Consistent Rehearsal (ICR) uses a pretrained 3D point-flow model as a motion prior. It refines the predicted trajectories through response-aware object grouping, persistent grasp memory, and contact structure refiner, preserving scene stability, object structure, and grasp attachments. Rehearsal-Aligned Video Generation (RAVG) then uses geometric projections of the rehearsal as temporal conditioning. We fine-tune a video generator on projection-video pairs to synthesize realistic interaction videos. Thanks to the decoupling of physical response from visual synthesis, RehearsalWorld also supports independent editing of material properties and camera viewpoints. Compared with PointWorld on real DROID grasping sequences, RehearsalWorld reduces gripper-relative offset by approximately 79% to 84% across deformable, articulated, and rigid objects and lowers reference motion error. For video prediction, it achieves a PSNR of 23.92 dB and an FVD of 149.



### StateWeaver: Multi-Agent Coordination of Story-State Transitions for Long-Horizon Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34290v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34290v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:43:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:43:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Modi Jin, Xuying Zhang, Yijing Guo, Haoran Li, Yanwen Ma, Jing Wang, Chenyu Ge, Ming-Ming Cheng, Haoyang Huang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Modern video foundation models generate high-quality short clips, but preserving long-video consistency and correctly depicting event-driven state changes remain challenging. Existing agentic workflows face these challenges because continual planning and repair accumulate context, while reference updates remain limited. We introduce StateWeaver, which combines Active States and Hierarchical Collaboration to connect review-driven visual reference updates with cross-shot state inheritance. Active States translate state discrepancies identified during review into concrete visual asset requirements that guide reference construction, validation, and regeneration, preserving identity and already-correct content while repairing target attributes. Confirmed generation results are linked to the corresponding asset states, story time, and reference versions for verification and reuse by subsequent shots. Hierarchical Collaboration isolates execution contexts by task, clip, and repair round, and uses hierarchical memory to organize this process across multiple sets. Across multiple benchmarks, StateWeaver outperforms existing methods and produces long videos with sustained cross-shot consistency under predefined and autonomously planned storyboards.



### WebVibe: Multimodal Interactive Website Generation via Reverse Synthesis and Visual Self-Distillation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34292v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34292v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 12:46:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 12:46:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Tianxiang Jiang, Sheng Xia, Yilin Long, Kanghui Tian, Linquan Wu, Yongheng Zhang, Daixian Liu, Di Yin, Yi Wang, Yu Qiao, Qinglong Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Although multimodal large language models translate single screenshots into static HTML, generating interactive functional websites from design specifications still struggles with multi-page routing and stateful widget transitions. Examining existing training pipelines reveals two bottlenecks: (1) in forward data synthesis (X -> Y), writing the specification X before code execution leaves unimplemented or altered requirements in X as supervision noise; and (2) in reinforcement learning, scalar rewards discard the rich state transitions observed during replay. Based on these findings, we introduce WebVibe and Interactive-SDPO to train InteractiveCoder. WebVibe reverses synthesis into Y -> X by verifying cross-page and in-page interactions in the browser before deriving multimodal inputs from replay-confirmed states to build the 31,542-site WebVibe-32K corpus with reusable execution contracts. Interactive-SDPO then conditions a weight-sharing self-teacher on the replay trace and a 2x2 state-transition montage to provide token-level supervision at zero inference-time overhead. On the public Vision2Web L2, one-shot InteractiveCoder-9B nearly 3x its base model's functional score (20.2 to 55.2 FS) and matches a 1T-parameter coding agent in a single pass with 111x fewer total parameters and 78x fewer tokens. On our WebVibeBench, InteractiveCoder-4B and 9B further outperform all evaluated baselines by over 23 points.



### PostMesh: Self-Rewarded Preference Post-Training for Flow-Based Artist Mesh Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34319v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34319v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 13:05:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 13:05:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Gihyun Kwon
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Artistic mesh generators turn a dense 3D object into an artist mesh: a compact, quad-dominant mesh that is easy to edit. Most of current models are auto-regressive methods which decode a mesh token by token, take tens to hundreds of seconds per mesh. Diffusion- and flow-based generators are much faster, but their meshes still fall short of artist quality. To our knowledge, PostMesh is the first post-training method for a diffusion- or flow-based artist mesh generator, and it needs no expensive artist-made ground truth: the generator is trained on its own samples, scored by a reward computed from the dense input and the output. We propose an artistic mesh reward that keeps the output consistent with the dense input while making the mesh structure into artistic. We also propose a multi-negative flow preference optimisation that transfers the reward to the generator. After a short LoRA post-training, PostMesh is comparable to or better than slow autoregressive generators on most artist-mesh quality metrics and in a blind VLM preference test, at a fraction of their time.



### SPARC: Semantic Calibration with Latent Multimodal Knowledge for Robust Image-Text Retrieval
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34344v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34344v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 13:16:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 13:16:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhongkuan Mao, Wenzhuo Zhao, Haidong Chen, Shangze Li, Yidong Wang, Jinhe Bi, Yunpu Ma, Junfeng Fang, Fei Shen, Tat-Seng Chua
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Robust image-text retrieval aims to maintain accurate cross-modal matching when image or text queries are incomplete, supporting reliable information access in real-world applications. However, existing methods typically focus on adapting to distribution shifts, without adequately addressing candidate discrimination for incomplete queries and ranking instability. To this end, we propose semantic projection and alignment with relational constraints (SPARC), which elicits latent multimodal knowledge from frozen encoders to improve candidate discrimination and ranking stability. Specifically, we first devise a semantic calibration module on a product of spheres that refines incomplete visual and textual features through tangent-space updates, maintaining the unit-sphere structure used for cosine similarity. Second, we introduce a robust semantic guidance module that combines latent multimodal knowledge encoded in complete image-text features with cross-modal semantic anchors to promote semantic alignment of the calibrated features. Finally, we introduce a matching relation constraint module that fuses original query features with their calibrated counterparts and applies a soft ranking constraint to penalize reductions in initially positive matching margins, thereby limiting ranking perturbations. Extensive experiments on Flickr30K and COCO demonstrate that SPARC outperforms competing methods under various image occlusion and word deletion settings, while achieving consistent gains across different pretrained backbones.



### AIRIS: Benchmarking Social Embodied Intelligence in Egocentric Streaming Videos
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34347v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34347v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 13:18:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 13:18:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuchen Zhou, Hongsheng Wu, Jiayu Tang, Yunxuan Mao, Hanjing Ye, Shenghao Zhang, Jing Tang, Chun-Kai Fan, Yufei Huang, Yonggen Ling, He Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Embodied agents operating around people must not only recognize social cues, but also decide when the available evidence is sufficient, whether to intervene, and how to respond without violating social norms. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate these abilities in isolation or with access to completed videos, obscuring where an online social decision fails. We introduce AIRIS, a causal streaming benchmark that formulates social embodied intelligence as a time-anchored chain of Attention, Intention, Reaction, and Interaction (A-I-R-I). AIRIS contains 8,000 expert-curated questions grounded in 357 egocentric clips across 11 context categories and 36 capability dimensions. At each query, models receive only the observation prefix available at that time; decision-aware metrics reward calibrated waiting and complete reactions while penalizing premature commitment. Evaluating 45 proprietary and open-source general-purpose, embodied, streaming-video, and omni-modal models reveals fragmented and temporally miscalibrated competence. General-purpose VLMs excel at passive cue grounding and intent recognition, whereas embodied specialists lead on isolated reaction sub-decisions; yet across all families, models frequently guess intents before sufficient evidence emerges and fail to jointly align intervention timing, action domain, and concrete execution within the same episode. AIRIS thus provides a diagnostic foundation for bridging the gap between passive social perception and reliable embodied interaction.



### One Patch, Many Policies: Object-Associated Physical Patch Attacks on Robotic Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34360v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34360v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 13:28:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 13:28:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiqian Liu, Yuqi Lin, Hui Lu, Jiaming He, Qi Zhu, Deyi Ji, Bing Li, Kaizheng Wang, Zhiyuan Zha, Yi Yu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) and World Action Models (WAMs) are vulnerable to adversarial visual interference, motivating the study of universal and transferable physical patch attacks. We propose Object-Associated Patch Attack (OAPA), a universal and transferable physical patch framework that enables an attacker to select which task object's manipulation to disrupt through patch placement, without re-optimizing the patch. Our framework combines object-associated placement, differentiable physical compositing, and final action-expert representation divergence. Physical compositing accounts for perspective and occlusion across camera views, while representation divergence guides patch optimization using either relative RMS or cosine distance. Experimental results demonstrate strong attack transferability across task suites and policies, together with the ability to selectively disrupt manipulation of an attacker-chosen task object through patch placement.



### MoCA4D: Monocular 3D Bounding Box Lifting and Tracking with Camera and Object Motion
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34370v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34370v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 13:34:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 13:34:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Changwoo Jeon, Jongyoo Kim
- **Comment**: 20 pages, 5 figures, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Monocular 3D bounding box tracking must account for both object motion and changes in camera viewpoint. Framewise box lifting with temporal filtering leaves these motion components implicit, while static-scene fusion assumes stationary objects. We introduce MoCA4D, a framework for 3D box lifting and tracking from monocular video, associated 2D box prompts, and camera intrinsics. An observation branch estimates per-frame geometry, while a dynamic branch predicts a camera transformation shared across objects and a separate rigid transformation for each object. We propose Star Optimization to refine current depth and orientation using the observation and historical boxes propagated through these transformations. The refined box then guides subsequent propagation. Across all evaluated objects in Argoverse 2 and ADT, Star Optimization reduces center motion error by 38.1% and 38.7%, respectively, relative to Kalman filtering of the same observations. On MOVi-F, the dynamic branch achieves the lowest corner and rotation errors and the highest IoU among the evaluated methods over eight-transition motion windows. Real-data camera-only controls further link predicted object motion to improved localization on dynamic-object tracks.



### Plausible Is Not Proven: Testing Failure Explanations in Monocular Depth Estimation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34393v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34393v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 13:54:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 13:54:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Zeeshan Ali, Md Rayyan, Anshuman Chakravorty, Omer Faraz, Anshi Bajaj, Amit Saini
- **Comment**: 17 pages, 7 figures, 14 tables. Code, ratings and results: https://github.com/Md-Zeeshan-Ali/vision-error-observatory
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Failure analyses of monocular depth models often name a cause for each error, such as an occluding boundary, a thin object or a reflective surface, but such labels are rarely tested. We present the Vision Error Observatory (VEO), an audit that finds regions failing the $δ_1$ criterion on sensor-measured pixels, labels each with a likely cause or abstains, and then subjects the labels to four tests: agreement with object annotations on data the label model never saw, agreement with blinded human raters, response to targeted image edits, and value as a training signal. We apply it to eight models on NYU Depth V2, DIODE and iBims-1, with labels also checked on Hypersim, and fix most decision rules before their results. The occluding-boundary label agrees with annotations (AUROC 0.85 on NYU, 0.81 on Hypersim, 0.69 on iBims-1), but a single depth-edge cue does as well, and most boundary failures of the strongest model vanish under a 2-pixel tolerance. The reflection label does not transfer to synthetic data, the textureless label is near chance against annotations, and only one of four edit tests, an exploratory one, supports its label. Evaluated against five blinded raters, VEO's labels matched the human consensus only at chance level (Cohen's $κ$ = 0.05). In addition, an increase in the weight on the diagnosed regions during fine-tuning led to an increase in the error on targeted pixels at every weight tested. The architecture-specific failure fingerprint claimed by an earlier version of this project also failed to replicate across the datasets. Plausible failure explanations, in short, need to be tested before they are trusted; we release the protocol, code, ratings and every result file.



### Strong Starts, Simple Recipes: Unified Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation from Multimodal Specialists
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34397v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CL, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34397v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 13:59:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 13:59:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Wenzheng Chang, Taiqiang Wu, Yuqi Xu, Yongfu Zhu, Zelin Peng, Ji Zhao, Shuai Dong, Wenpu Liu, Caoyuan Ma, Xuehui Wang, Ziyue Wang, Weichu Xie, Haiyuan Wan, Ngai Wong, Tong He, Wenqi Shao, Jiaqi Wang
- **Comment**: Project page, code, and model weights: https://amberheart.github.io/Uni-MOPD/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) integrates separately trained domain specialists into a single unified model. Existing methods focus on refining the distillation procedure while initializing the student from a base model or a multi-domain SFT checkpoint, which leaves capability integration largely to distillation itself. Instead, we treat the starting point as a design variable and propose Unified MOPD (Uni-MOPD), which pairs a strong start with a simple recipe: a training-free uniform merge of specialists from the same base model initializes the student, and short MOPD with uniform domain sampling and full-vocabulary reverse KL refines it. Across five multimodal domains and 19 benchmarks, our study establishes three findings. (1) The starting point matters more than subsequent training choices: before any training, the uniform merge already matches the score that the base model reaches after 200 MOPD updates, and after short distillation it matches higher-scoring, search-based starts. (2) Short MOPD recovers most of the capabilities the merge leaves unintegrated; it improves every domain, with the largest gains where the domain teacher still leads. (3) From a strong start, uniform domain sampling and full-vocabulary reverse KL match or exceed every evaluated sampling strategy and divergence, without schedules, per-token weighting, or truncation hyperparameters. Uni-MOPD outperforms Open-MOPD at the same training budget while training 3.3 times faster than its released code. We will release the training framework, specialist checkpoints, and unified student.



### SemProxy: Semantic 3D Proxies for Controllable Agentic 3D Modeling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34414v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34414v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:08:05+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:08:05+00:00
- **Authors**: Shuyuan Zhang, Changjian Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Coding agents are increasingly capable of generating 3D models, yet enabling users to effectively control the modeling process remains challenging. Existing workflows rely primarily on text prompts and reference images, which often struggle to convey precise spatial intentions and require iterative corrections. We introduce SemProxy, a lightweight intermediate representation that bridges intuitive human interaction and agentic 3D modeling. Instantiated as labeled 3D bounding boxes, semantic 3D proxies allow users to directly specify object parts and spatial arrangements while leaving geometric details to coding agents. These proxies can be serialized into structured text and rendered as images, making them directly accessible to existing coding agents without additional training or modifications. We further develop ProxyStudio, a user interface that integrates text, image references, and interactive 3D proxy editing into agentic modeling workflows. We demonstrate the versatility of SemProxy across object- and scene-level modeling tasks with different coding agents, and evaluate its effectiveness in supporting controllable human-agent collaborative 3D modeling through a user study.



### SPGSR: A Small Pixel-Space Generator for One-Step Super-Resolution
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34418v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34418v1)

- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:10:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Yun Yue, Luming Liang, Yiqi Zhong
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In one-step super-resolution (SR), vision-only models extract semantic cues directly from the low-resolution (LR) image, keeping those cues spatially aligned with the structures they help restore. Recent models achieve high restoration quality with generators of up to 1.4 billion parameters. Given how much structure the LR image already provides, could a much smaller generator achieve comparable quality? We introduce SPGSR, a vision-only framework that adds a learned RGB correction to the LR image. A frozen DINOv2 guides prediction, and a lightweight block refines the correction locally. SPGSR achieves higher PSNR/SSIM and lower LPIPS/DISTS than VOSR, a leading vision-only SR method, across three benchmarks. Its $31.8\mathrm{M}$-parameter generator is just $6.5\%$ the size of VOSR-0.5B's. SPGSR also achieves competitive quality against T2I-based SR methods. SPGSR's generator uses only $0.60\%$ of the HR pixels used to train and distill VOSR's generator. For $512\times512$ outputs, SPGSR achieves $2.80\times$ and $3.35\times$ GPU-pipeline speedups over VOSR-0.5B and VOSR-1.4B, respectively.



### DocSEAM: Bidirectional Cross-Page Encoding for Multi-Page Document Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34420v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34420v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:11:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:11:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Hoang-Nhat Nguyen, Chen Change Loy
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multi-page document vision-language models encode each page independently, creating a feature-level discontinuity at page boundaries: visual tokens are formed without access to content on adjacent pages, and the language model must reason over these page representations formed in isolation. We introduce DocSEAM (Document Selective Enrichment via Anchored Masking), a lightweight module inserted between a pretrained vision encoder and the language model that injects the inductive bias of local cross-page context into visual representations. For each page, DocSEAM forms a local triplet with its two neighbors, applies center-anchored masked self-attention with directional salience gates to fuse information from neighbor pages, and returns only the enriched center-page tokens, leaving the visual token count unchanged. After a supervised cross-page warm-up and multi-page fine-tuning with DocSEAM frozen, our 9B-parameter model achieves state-of-the-art results among the evaluated open-weight models of up to 10B parameters on five multi-page document VQA benchmarks. We further show that under controlled re-pagination, DocSEAM reduces sensitivity to boundary placement and improves cross-page span localization



### Can Visual World Models See Force?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34422v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34422v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:12:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:12:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Felix Tristram, Benjamin D. Killeen, Nassir Navab
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World models and vision-language-action policy models are increasingly proposed as foundations for robot manipulation. They predict how scenes evolve and which actions to take, yet successful manipulation depends not solely on following observable trajectories but also on the hidden variable of interaction force. Neurophysiology has shown that humans possess a visual prior for this hidden variable, motivating us to ask the question: Do the representations of visual foundation models also capture the forces involved in an interaction? We define force awareness as the ability of representations to (1) indicate when contact force is applied, (2) track how it changes during an interaction, and (3) reflect how large it is. We probe frozen image, video, world- and policy model representations with a lightweight readout trained on synchronized video and force measurements from two manipulation datasets. Our experiments show that most models capture when contact occurs but not how much force is being applied. Surprisingly, robot post-training does not improve this either, highlighting an important gap in current models capabilities.



### One Representation to Rule Them All -- Open-Vocabulary Change Detection from Text or Visual Examples
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34430v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34430v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:16:57+00:00
