- **Authors**: Blaž Rolih, Matic Fučka, Filip Wolf, Luka Čehovin Zajc
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Open-vocabulary change detection (OVCD) aims to localise user-specified changes without retraining for predefined categories. Existing approaches typically rely on textual descriptions and combine separate models for region proposal, semantic recognition, and temporal comparison. This limits how target concepts can be expressed and makes detection dependent on several intermediate predictions. We introduce OVTSID3, a training-free framework that unifies region grouping, concept identification, and temporal comparison in the representation space of DINOv3. OVTSID3 supports two complementary modes of concept specification: zero-shot textual queries or one-shot visual prompting through a masked reference pair. OVTSID3 outperforms all prior training-free OVCD methods on two building and two natural-disaster benchmarks, raising the average F1 by 8.9 points. OVTSID3 also supports semantic change detection and achieves state-of-the-art on SECOND. These results demonstrate the flexibility of a unified representation for text- or example-guided change detection without task-specific model training.



### Most of the Way There: Turning a Strong Video Prior into an Action-Conditioned World Model
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34435v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34435v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:19:27+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:19:27+00:00
- **Authors**: Zanyi Wang, Xia Chen, Liu Liu, Xianglong Guo, Dengyang Jiang, Haodong Li, Tingyang Xiao, Wenhao Hu, Zhizhong Su
- **Comment**: Project Page: https://xmz111.github.io/Dianjing/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Modern video generators excel at producing visually plausible futures guided by text and image prompts. Yet their emphasis on semantic plausibility does not guarantee fidelity to the physical consequences of specific actions. Action-conditioned world models (ACWMs), in contrast, aim to simulate how the world responds to robot actions, enabling scalable policy evaluation and improvement without costly real-world rollouts. The challenge is not merely to generate a plausible future, but to predict what follows from a specific physical action. As video priors grow stronger, we ask how little adaptation is truly needed to bridge this gap. We introduce Dianjing, a minimal recipe that preserves and unlocks the capabilities of strong video priors through unified physical action conditioning, training concentrated at the high noise levels where motion and layout, and thus the consequences of actions, are decided, and native history conditioning. Built upon MiniMax H3, a 33B video foundation model, Dianjing freezes the pretrained backbone and trains only 0.34% of its parameters using approximately 65 hours of robot video across two embodiments, without additional robot-specific pretraining or camera calibration. Controlled ablations show that where training is allocated across noise levels matters more than how actions enter the network. On WorldOracle, which evaluates predictions against paired real-world executions, Dianjing achieves the best scores on four of the five evaluation tracks and ranks second on AgiBot-Long. Our findings suggest that strong video priors already bring us most of the way to world modeling; lightweight physical action grounding can unlock their potential for real-world prediction.



### PreserveReward: Reliable Consistency Reward Modeling for Image Editing via Dual-Teacher On-Policy Self-Distillation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34446v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34446v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:24:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:24:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Xueheng Li, Yong Liu, Wen Xue, Shi-Yu Tian, Zhuofan Xia, Chengjun Xie, Yipeng Sun, Simiu Gu
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 5 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Instruction-guided image editing has a wide range of applications, yet existing models may inadvertently alter content that should remain unchanged. Reliable consistency evaluation is therefore essential for assessing content preservation and providing reward signals for Reinforcement Learning (RL). However, existing reward models may overlook subtle visual changes or fail to distinguish necessary modifications from unintended alterations, limiting the reliability of consistency assessment. To address this challenge, we propose PreserveReward, a consistency reward model for image editing that integrates visual On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD) to enhance fine-grained visual perception and consistency assessment. PreserveReward learns from reference preservation scores through a regression-based scoring loss and employs two complementary teacher views for self-distillation. The local teacher uses matched crops to identify subtle visual changes, while the joint teacher combines local details with global context to determine whether these changes align with the editing intent. To improve the reliability of distillation supervision, we calibrate teacher score distributions via minimum-KL projection under reference-score error constraints and adaptively select teacher process supervision based on gradient alignment with the scoring objective, mitigating scoring bias and optimization conflicts. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that PreserveReward achieves strong alignment with human preference on non-edited content preservation and generalizes well across diverse editing scenarios.



### ProxyGenBench: Benchmarking Proxy-Guided Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34457v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34457v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:31:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:31:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhenchen Tang, Haolin Lu, Songlin Yang, Xiaotong Zhao, Ran Zhang, Bo Peng, Shuai Li, Haotian Fan, Haobin Zhong, Yanni Zhang, Songlian Li, Ning Zhang, Xun Gao, Alan Zhao, Jing Dong
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent video generators produce realistic videos, yet controlling how scenes unfold remains challenging. Structural proxies, such as animated 3D blockouts, guide generation by specifying scene layout, motion, camera behavior, and event order. However, these coarse inputs leave details unspecified and may contain artifacts that should not be reproduced. Successful proxy-guided generation must therefore Preserve intended structure, Complete missing content and correct proxy artifacts, and Bind text, image, and audio conditions to the right entities and events. We introduce ProxyGenBench to study these interacting requirements, with 312 evaluation cases from 60 scenes across three scene tasks and controlled variations in proxy fidelity, text detail, and multimodal inputs. We evaluate five video generators using verified, case-specific checklists. Experiments show that proxies improve spatiotemporal control, but greater proxy detail can coincide with poorer completion, while strong structural adherence does not ensure correct binding.



### SceneDesigner++: A Unified 9-DoF Pose Control Framework for T2I and T2V Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34461v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34461v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:33:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:33:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Xincheng Shuai, Zhenyuan Qin, Henghui Ding, Dacheng Tao
- **Comment**: Project Page: https://henghuiding.com/SceneDesigner++/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Precise 9-DoF pose control is essential for visual content generation, enabling users to specify object location, size, and orientation in 3D space. However, existing methods typically focus on either text-to-image (T2I) or text-to-video (T2V) generation, with limitations in pose controllability and temporal coherence. We present SceneDesigner++, a unified framework for 9-DoF pose control across both T2I and T2V generation. We construct ObjectPose9D++, a dataset containing high-quality images and videos with accurate pose annotations. Trained on ObjectPose9D++, SceneDesigner++ combines absolute and relative pose encoding strategies to decouple frame-wise spatial grounding from inter-frame motion modeling. Specifically, absolute pose encoding leverages CNOCS map sequences to provide explicit, frame-wise 9-DoF pose guidance. Meanwhile, our relative pose encoding, termed VPRoPE, incorporates relative pose information into rotary position embeddings to explicitly model inter-frame pose transitions, thereby promoting temporally coherent object motion. A learnable orthogonal transformation is further introduced for each attention head, allowing different heads to capture complementary geometric relationships while preserving input feature norms to promote training stability. We further adopt a two-stage training strategy to learn the absolute and relative pose encoding mechanisms separately. Benefiting from this lightweight and decoupled design, SceneDesigner++ also supports integration with an independently trained camera-control module, enabling separate or joint control over object and camera motion. Finally, we introduce T2IVPoseEval, a benchmark for evaluating diverse object poses and motion patterns in both T2I and T2V tasks. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SceneDesigner++ consistently outperforms same-backbone baselines in pose fidelity and motion quality.



### Evidence-Aligned Benchmarking and Learning for Medical Video Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34465v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34465v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:35:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:35:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhongpai Gao, Anwesa Choudhuri, Meng Zheng, Benjamin Planche, Terrence Chen, Ziyan Wu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A medical video language model should answer from the visual evidence its question asks about. We find three places where this breaks down and address each with an approach we call MedEvidU. First, benchmarks reward answers that ignore the video: on one dataset, the position of a clip within its recording predicts the surgical step in 37% of questions, and a constant answer beats every trained model on a standard safety-assessment metric. We build MedVidBench-v2, 34 tasks from 18 surgical and clinical video datasets, in which prompts and metrics are audited with answers given without video, and which tests unseen hospitals and windows cut blindly from whole recordings. Second, common video encoders merge consecutive frames into shared tokens, so a question about one frame is answered from a blend of two. Query-slot inputs give each queried frame its own tokens at almost no extra cost. Third, a fine-tuned model has memorized its training questions, which leaves little to learn from. Label-matched question resampling (LQR) adds unseen questions about the same recordings while keeping the answer distribution fixed, in a final supervised epoch and in reinforcement learning. Together, these raise the grounding IoU of a 4B model from 30.4 to 44.4, above a supervised 27B model, and instrument grounding at an unseen hospital from 33.9 to 72.5. On blindly cut windows, event-presence accuracy improves by 5.5 points and localization F1 by 8.1 points. Project page: https://gaozhongpai.github.io/MedEvidU/



### Attacking Hard-Label Large Vision-Language Models with Model-Sensitive Adversarial Patch Designs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34467v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34467v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:36:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:36:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Nian Ai, Guangke Chen, Xiaowen Cai, Zhongliang Guo, Daizong Liu, Pan Zhou, Ognjen Arandjelović
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in handling multi-modal downstream tasks, gaining increasing popularity. However, recent studies show that LVLMs are susceptible to both intentional and inadvertent attacks. Existing attackers ideally optimize adversarial perturbations with backpropagated gradients from LVLMs, thus limiting their scalability in practical scenarios as real-world LVLM applications will not provide any LVLM's gradient or details. Motivated by this research gap and counter-practical phenomenon, we propose a novel hard-label attack method for LVLMs, named HardPatch, to generate visual adversarial patches by solely querying the model. Our method provides deeper insights into how to investigate the vulnerability of LVLMs in local visual regions and generate corresponding adversarial substitution under the practical yet challenging hard-label setting. Specifically, we first split each image into uniform patches and mask each of them to individually assess their sensitivity to the LVLM model. Then, according to the descending order of sensitive scores, we iteratively select the most vulnerable patch to initialize noise and estimate gradients with further additive random noises for optimization. In this manner, multiple patches are perturbed until the altered image satisfies the adversarial condition. Extensive LVLM models and datasets are evaluated to demonstrate the adversarial nature of HardPatch. Our empirical observations suggest that with appropriate patch substitution and optimization, HardPatch can craft effective adversarial images to attack hard-label LVLMs.



### TACC: Target-Aware Carrier Compression for Dense-Context Novel View Synthesis
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34476v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34476v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:41:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:41:33+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiming Fan, Yijia Guo, Zewen Guo, Liwen Hu, Yuheng Yuan, Yang Li, Zhiwei Li, Xitong Ling, Caihong Yuan, Shengbo Chen, Tiejun Huang, Lei Ma
- **Comment**: 31 pages, 10 figures, 21 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent feed-forward approaches mark a paradigm shift in novel view synthesis by eliminating the reliance on per-scene optimization, directly inferring 3D representations from input views. However, their reliance on global scene-token context incurs substantial memory and latency overhead, constraining their ability to handle increasingly dense source-view contexts. Compressing source representations offers an intuitive way to limit this token budget. Prior general-purpose compression methods are not tailored for novel view synthesis, as their fixed, target-agnostic nature fails to adapt evidence allocation to individual target views. To overcome this, we introduce TACC, a target-aware compression module tailored for feed-forward latent NVS. Specifically, TACC organizes each source view into a progressive carrier hierarchy. Driven by source-target geometric relevance, it adaptively retrieves tokens to dynamically allocate exact computational quotas. This selective retrieval directly yields a compact, target-conditioned representation. Extensive experiments on the RE10K demonstrate that under dense input settings, TACC achieves $9.23\times$ and $64\times$ higher Effective FPS than Full Context on LagerNVS and LVSM, respectively. Effective FPS excludes one-time source encoding and Carrier construction.



### Feed-Forward Adaptive Gaussian Splatting for Interactive Medical Volume Rendering
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34478v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34478v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:43:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:43:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhongpai Gao, Benjamin Planche, Meng Zheng, Anwesa Choudhuri, Chaoyi Zhou, Terrence Chen, Ziyan Wu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Path-traced rendering shows CT and MR volumes with realistic lighting, but it restarts after every change of view, clipping plane or transfer function (TF), the map from voxel intensity to colour and opacity, which limits the frame rate. Feed-forward Gaussian splatting models such as RenderFM predict the Gaussians directly from the volume instead of fitting them to each scan, and are fast. RenderFM, however, handles only CT, runs its network again after every TF edit, has no clipping operator, and places its Gaussians on a uniform 3-mm grid that blurs organ boundaries and cut faces. We present RenderFM-Next, a feed-forward model that addresses these limits. One network handles both CT and MR. Its Gaussians are predicted once per volume: a new TF needs no pass of this network, and a clipping plane cuts them exactly, using operators from prior work. The model predicts coarse 3-mm Gaussians for the whole volume and refines them to 1.5 mm only where refinement improves the image. A small score head ranks the coarse Gaussians by how much refining them would reduce the rendering error; at render time the user chooses how many are refined, and those next to a clipping plane are always refined, which sharpens cut faces. We build a benchmark of about 2,000 CT and MR scans with path-traced references under clinical presets, TF edits and clipping. Our RenderFM-Next improves over RenderFM retrained on this benchmark by 2.8 dB on CT and 2.4 dB on MR and renders a 1600x1600 view in 4-6 ms. Refinement adds 0.30 / 0.69 dB over the same model trained without it (0.51 / 1.17 dB on cut faces), and refining a learned 10% of the coarse Gaussians matches a random 50% with 2.4x fewer Gaussians. Project page: https://gaozhongpai.github.io/renderfmnext/



### ProManO: Beyond Sim(3) for Metric Upgrade of Feed-Forward Geometry with Visual-Inertial Anchors
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34480v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34480v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:43:18+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:43:18+00:00
- **Authors**: Lingxiang Hu, Zhenjun Zhao, Qi Shao, Naima Ait Oufroukh, Fabien Bonardi, Raymond Ghandour, Shenghai Yuan, Javier Civera, Baoru Huang
- **Comment**: 12 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Upgrading feed-forward geometry to metric scale with visual-inertial odometry (VIO) raises the question of which correction should align dense feed-forward prediction with sparse metric VIO anchors. Existing methods use either a similarity transformation, which cannot represent depth-dependent distortion, or a projective transformation, which alters pre-calibrated intrinsics. We prove that the transformations compatible with pre-calibrated intrinsics form a ten-dimensional manifold. Beyond Sim(3), this manifold contains three degrees of freedom (DoF) that locate the plane at infinity and leave residuals growing quadratically with depth range after any similarity alignment. These DoF are invisible in the image and can be resolved only by metric depth along viewing rays or by multi-view constraints. To estimate them, we propose ProManO, a probabilistic manifold optimization framework in which every anchor is weighted by its along-ray uncertainty and inlier probability within a single expectation-maximization (EM) procedure. ProManO certifies the similarity synchronization and refines the plane at infinity only where it is observable, which guarantees an objective value no worse than that of the optimal similarity alignment. Experiments on EuRoC, CID-SIMS, Oxford Spires, and KITTI-360 demonstrate that ProManO achieves the highest mean F1 and outperforms Sim(3) alignment on all four datasets. The project page is available at https://h0xg.github.io/ProManO/.



### Who Is Ego? Scaling Cross-View Camera Wearer Identification and Segmentation beyond Humans
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34489v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34489v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:48:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:48:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Yifan Jin, Zheng Zhang, Tianwen Qian, Dong Wang, Bo Zhao, Yanwei Fu, Yuqian Fu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A first-person (ego) image reveals what a camera sees, but not who or what carries it. Given a synchronized third-person (exo) image, camera wearer identification asks who is ego: which candidate carries the ego camera? We extend this task beyond humans to robots, vehicles, animals, and drones, and broaden its spatial scope (where is ego) from selecting a provided candidate mask to wearer segmentation with absence prediction and mutual camera localization across ego-exo views. We introduce FindEgo-1M, comprising 1.16M real and simulated ego-exo pairs across 12 wearer types, 17 domains, and 111 test settings. Our approach exploits a geometric relation: the ego camera's projection into the exo image, i.e., the epipole, lies on or near its wearer. We introduce EgoRay-ZS, the first method to turn a frozen feed-forward 3D reconstruction model, VGGT-$Ω$, into a training-free solution for wearer identification and segmentation. Its geometric readout ranks candidate masks or segmentation proposals by angular offset and distance to the ego camera, while the relative pose provides mutual camera directions. We then develop the full EgoRay model through pose-supervised training on FindEgo-1M and label-free test-time adaptation constrained by candidate masks. Without task-specific training, EgoRay-ZS achieves 78.0% identification accuracy on TF2023's original labels, versus 52.9% reported for the dataset-trained PEN. On FindEgo-1M, EgoRay achieves state-of-the-art chance-corrected accuracy of 87.1 and 77.7 on seen and unseen settings. On matched queries, EgoRay also outperforms GPT-6 Astra by 31.6 and 21.3 points on seen and unseen settings, respectively, while running $18\times$ faster. Data, code, and models will be released.



### Angular Interpolation in Sparse-View Low-Dose CT Reconstruction: Placement Effects and Data-Consistency Step-Size Scaling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34508v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34508v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:57:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:57:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Yitian Tang, Yuncheng Zhang, Hualiang Wang, Zuozhu Liu
- **Comment**: 12 pages, 3 figures, 3 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Dual-domain networks combine projection and image processing for sparse-view low-dose computed tomography (CT). We quantify the contribution of angular interpolation before learning and distinguish its roles in sinogram initialisation and the image residual base. On synthetic parallel-beam data, parameter-free interpolation reduces noise-free missing-view mean absolute error by 99.7% relative to zero filling at 60 views. Full-view filtered back-projection (FBP) of the jointly learned completed sinogram adds 1.27 dB of peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) over interpolated FBP. In a data-consistency dual-domain network (DC-DuNet), a 2x2 configuration ablation controls branch width, depth and training schedule while varying these placements and their associated input channels. After 20 epochs, the PSNR contrasts are +1.98 dB for sinogram initialisation and -2.16 dB for the interpolated image base; the image-base ranking also reverses during training. The sinogram-initialised variant exceeds an image-only U-Net by 0.96 dB at 60 views and 10% dose, and by 1.28 dB on independently generated synthetic data. The U-Net leads at higher view counts and with a shared interpolated image base. Without retraining, the variant retains a 0.12 dB advantage on held-out CHAOS abdominal CT under simulated degradation; the image-base contrast rounds to zero. The implemented projection/backprojection pair has empirical dominant gain of approximately 0.85NK, where N is image-grid width and K acquired view count. These results distinguish interpolation placement from network architecture and support NK normalisation for step calibration.



### Long-Horizon Planning with Latent World Models via Graph Anchors
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34509v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34509v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 14:58:31+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 14:58:31+00:00
- **Authors**: Syed Nazmus Sakib, Abdul Monaf Chowdhury, Peizhen Li, Nafiul Haque, Chandak Chakma, Md Mehedi Hasan, Zehong Cao, Shifat E. Arman
- **Comment**: Project page: https://syed-nazmus-sakib.github.io/graph-anchors/ | Code: https://github.com/syed-nazmus-sakib/graph-anchors
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Latent world models plan in a compact latent space by predicting the outcomes of actions. Their planners judge a plan by the latent distance between its   predicted end state and the goal image. We show that this distance fails in two distinct ways. First, it loses directional signal a few steps from the   goal, causing distant goals to fail. Second, it weighs the robot arm over the manipulated object, causing manipulation to fail at contact. We measure   both failures directly on recorded data by asking whether a lower latent distance corresponds to true progress toward the goal. Across five   environments, six visual representations (including raw pixels and V-JEPA 2), and nine Meta-World tasks, this ranking test cleanly separates the two   failures, and the graph distance to a goal predicts where closed-loop planning breaks down. Both failures share a single remedy: replacing the distant   goal with a nearby target that lies on a recorded trajectory. We realize this principle with Graph Anchors, a training-free planner for frozen latent   world models. It builds a sparse graph once over the model's own training frames and aims each planning step at a node a few moves ahead along the   shortest path to the goal, keeping paths on recorded transitions wherever contact matters. On goals up to 400 steps away, Graph Anchors raise LeWM's   success from 13% to 85% on PointMaze-Medium, from 8% to 87% on PointMaze-Large, and from 5% to 71% on Push-T, where stronger search toward the goal   fails and where prior training-free planners break down under missing step horizons, subsampling shortcuts, or fragmented contact.



### Decoder-as-a-Probe: Verbalizing and Steering Visual Representations in LVLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34515v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34515v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:04:57+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:04:57+00:00
- **Authors**: Adhemar de Senneville, Xavier Bou, Jérémy Anger, Rafael Grompone, Gabriele Facciolo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: It is commonly accepted that high-level concepts are represented linearly in the representation space of large neural networks. For Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs), finding those interpretable directions in visual representations is important for interpretability. To find them, the classical approach is to train a linear classifier on the representations, but it needs a large amount of labels and only recovers what can be read from the representations, not what the model actually uses. In this paper we start from the following insight: The LLM Decoder of an LVLM is also a classifier whose classes are the possible next tokens. So we use its first-order Taylor expansion as a linear classifier. Hence, we introduce Decoder-as-a-Probe (DaP), which significantly outperforms classical probing and steering baselines especially when training data is sparse. (1) It verbalizes what an LVLM perceives in a given image. (2) It can surgically change the perception of the LVLM about specific attributes (e.g. change only the color, the clothes worn or the action done). Due to its simplicity and data efficiency, we believe DaP can serve as a plug-and-play tool for LVLM research, such as interpretability, hallucination mitigation or instruction tuning.



### PURA: Video Token Pruning via Unified Spatiotemporal Redundancy Assessment
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34521v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34521v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:08:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:08:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuyao Sun, Hongli Yuan, Shuang Li, Tao Deng, Deqing Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video large language models incur substantial inference costs from long visual token sequences. Existing video token compression methods mitigate these costs by exploiting spatial redundancy within frames and temporal redundancy across frames. However, many approaches impose predefined processing orders or fixed allocation ratios for spatial and temporal compression, limiting their ability to adapt to the varying balance of spatiotemporal redundancy across videos. To address this limitation, we propose PURA, a training-free method that prunes visual tokens through unified spatiotemporal redundancy assessment. For each token, PURA constructs spatial and temporal reference candidates and selects the reference with the lowest representation error across the two dimensions. The representation error is combined with token importance to determine each token's removal cost. To avoid the time overhead of removing the lowest-cost token one by one, we introduce Coordinated Pruning, which iteratively groups mutually compatible low-cost removals into batches for simultaneous execution and updates the pruning state after each batch until the target budget is reached. Extensive experiments on three video large language models and five video understanding benchmarks demonstrate that PURA achieves a favorable trade-off between task performance and inference efficiency.



### Rank Before You Encode: Reading a Few Percent of a Whole-Slide Image
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34523v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34523v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:11:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:11:13+00:00
- **Authors**: Haoyu He, Mahdi S. Hosseini
- **Comment**: CVPR 2027 submission
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Classifying a whole-slide image requires encoding thousands of high-resolution tiles with a pathology foundation model, which accounts for most of the inference cost. Existing efficient methods reduce this cost by selecting fewer tiles, but typically train a new model around their selection strategy. A largely unexplored alternative is to keep an existing full-bag model frozen and run it on selected tiles at the same encoder cost. We introduce this comparison, alongside random tile sampling at matched cost. To select tiles, we fit a lightweight selector with 3.3K parameters to the attention of a frozen model, without additional slide labels. The selector ranks regions from a slide thumbnail, encodes the top K regions at 5x magnification, and selects b for processing at 20x. We evaluate seven frozen MIL aggregators on six tasks and compare them with nine efficient methods trained on the same features and splits. At a budget of (K,b)=(1000,10), encoding costs about 6.6% of the full bag and is 10-14x faster, excluding tiling. The AUROC drop is no more than 0.005 in 33 of 36 aggregator-task pairs. At (500,10), the cost falls to about 4% and inference is 13-22x faster, but AUROC drops by up to 0.035 on CAMELYON17, where small metastases are easily missed. Selection improves over random sampling by about 0.10 AUROC on tasks with focal lesions, while random sampling performs well on diffuse tasks. In our comparisons, the frozen model at (500,10) is ahead of nine efficient methods in 55 of 60 paired comparisons. Giving an existing WSI model fewer tiles is thus a baseline that a new efficient model should be compared with at matched encoder cost.



### HAS: Hierarchical Adaptive Frame Sampling for Video Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34544v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34544v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:23:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:23:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuyao Sun, Daiqi Shi, Shuang Li, Liao Zhang, Yang Cai, Deqing Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Due to context-length constraints, most MLLMs cannot process full-length videos and therefore rely on sampling a subset of frames as input. However, existing sampling methods, ranging from uniform sampling to relevance-based selection, are often driven by a single sampling principle and thus struggle to accommodate the heterogeneous evidential requirements of different queries: some demand a holistic understanding of how the video unfolds over time, while others hinge on fine-grained events within a short temporal window. To address this limitation, we propose \textbf{Hierarchical Adaptive Frame Sampling (HAS)}, a two-stage frame sampling framework. In the first stage, \emph{Backbone Frame Construction}, we apply a Determinantal Point Process (DPP) to sample frames that are both query-relevant and non-redundant. These selected frames capture key moments across the video and form the backbone of the sampling set, providing a foundation for subsequent enrichment. In the second stage, \emph{Adaptive Contextual Enrichment}, we analyze the temporal distribution of the backbone frames to infer the query's requirements and adaptively allocate the remaining frame budget between Local and Global Context. The Local Context enriches the backbone with fine-grained temporal dynamics and short-range causal relations, whereas the Global Context connects temporally isolated evidence and provides a holistic view of the entire video to support global understanding. Through such a hierarchical two-stage process, HAS effectively addresses diverse query requirements. Incorporated into three leading MLLMs, it demonstrates consistently superior performance across the Video-MME, LongVideoBench, and MLVU benchmarks.



### Recalling the Past to Answer the Present: Context-Aware Multi-Evidence Reasoning for Streaming Video Question Answering
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34545v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34545v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:24:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:24:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Cui Yakun, Tiantian Geng, Huixiang Kong, Xingqun Qi, Sirui Han, Yike Guo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models (VLMs) have achieved remarkable progress in video understanding, yet existing video question answering (VQA) benchmarks mainly evaluate models in offline settings. Real-world streaming scenarios instead require models to answer context-dependent queries by retrieving and reasoning over evidence distributed across the video history. We introduce StreamRecall, a benchmark for context-aware Multi evidence reasoning in streaming video QA. StreamRecall requires models to retrieve, associate, and reason over temporally scattered evidence to answer queries at the appropriate moment. It contains 4,983 questions over 462 videos, with context-aligned query times and fine-grained temporal evidence annotations. We further propose the Evidence Utilization Cascade to diagnose model reasoning through evidence localization, grounding, chain completion, and answer derivation. To enable efficient long-term streaming reasoning, we present EviStream, a training-free causal visual memory mechanism that preserves reasoning-critical evidence under a fixed KV budget. Experiments reveal substantial gaps between current VLMs and realistic streaming requirements, establishing StreamRecall as a foundation for developing evidence-aware streaming video intelligence.



### CADENCE: Sensor-GroundedWorld-Consistent Gaussian SLAM for Collaborative Mobile Reconstruction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34549v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34549v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:26:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:26:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Reshad Ul Karim, Abrar Samin, Md Rashad Al Hasan Rony
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Collaborative reconstruction with consumer phones promises faster and more complete indoor maps. However, heterogeneous devices introduce degraded observations and unreliable cross-device associations, so a single poor contributor or incorrect match can corrupt the shared Gaussian map. To address this, we propose \textbf{CADENCE}, a collaborative Gaussian simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM) system that separates finding cross-device matches from deciding whether and how peer observations update the map. To find matches, a sensor-grounded regional memory summarizes committed observations and ranks candidate peer regions using appearance, relative pose, gravity, barometric altitude, and observation quality. Deterministic geometric verification and optional physical checks then decide which of the proposed matches are accepted. Once a match is accepted, separate cue-based confidences control how its observations contribute to pose constraints, geometry, appearance, and memory. As a result, useful depth can still contribute even when color is unreliable. Throughout this process, local tracking remains isolated from mapping, verification, and networking. We evaluate CADENCE on eight Matterport3D houses that were not used to train the memory. On these houses, regional memory achieves an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) of $0.95$ to $0.97$ and thus outperforms appearance retrieval, while verification keeps false-link rates below $0.5\,\%$. Furthermore, soft confidence weighting reduces the mean receiver peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) loss from $2.66$ to $1.05$\,dB across eight degradations while retaining useful peer depth. Finally, using inertial and platform motion, CADENCE tracks all twelve held-out iPhone scans with a mean absolute trajectory error of $6.98$\,cm, and its refined maps achieve better average rendering quality than the baselines.



### M$^{2}$AdaptVLA: Multimodal Memory-Augmented Vision-Language-Action Model with Predictive Test-Time Adaptation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34560v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34560v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:32:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:32:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Liufan Tan, Tong Wang, Yuehao Yin, Jian Chen, Yihang Yin, Ying Qu, Wenyu Li, Ruyi Gan, Hao Wang
- **Comment**: 16 pages, 11 figures, 8 tables, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Effective robotic manipulation calls for multimodal memory and rapid adaptation to shifts in contact dynamics. However, many existing policies, which retain either visual or wrench history, may miss key cues from the other modality. Moreover, even with wrench memory, fixed policies can struggle when contact conditions shift at deployment. To this end, we introduce M$^{2}$AdaptVLA, a vision-language-action model that integrates visual and wrench memories with predictive test-time adaptation. For visual memory, the model uses test-time training (TTT) to encode observation history in fast weights. Each entry in the bounded TTT bank combines the corresponding frame's features with a readout from these weights, preserving cues that predate the oldest retained frame. For wrench memory, a context gate controls how much the action expert draws on wrench history as it jointly denoises future actions and wrenches. For predictive test-time adaptation, the auxiliary wrench prediction loss also provides online self-supervision. Using measured wrenches as targets, we update low-rank policy adapters by backpropagating only through the final denoising step. Real-world experiments show that the TTT bank outperforms visual memory baselines on progress tracking, scene recall, and demonstration following. On contact-sensitive tasks, the context gate yields higher success rates than direct injection or gated cross-attention. Predictive test-time adaptation improves robustness to shifts in contact dynamics. On eight tasks requiring visual and wrench memory, M$^{2}$AdaptVLA achieves a mean success rate of 82.71%, a 31.46% absolute gain over the strongest baseline. The project page is available at https://tlf-tlf.github.io/M2AdaptVLAPage/.



### Compact Vision-Language Navigation in Continuous Environmentsvia On-Policy Training and Route-Bearing Output
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34561v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34561v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:32:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:32:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Reshad Ul Karim, Syeda Maliha Tabassum, Md. Khalilur Rhaman, Md Rashad Al Hasan Rony
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Instruction-following robots need navigation policies that reach high accuracy and remain inexpensive to train and to run on a single graphics processing unit (GPU). The most accurate vision-language navigation policies for continuous environments rely on language models with 4 to 8 billion parameters, long observation histories, and large auxiliary corpora. Two properties of the standard training setup limit smaller policies. Imitation labels cover the states that an expert visits, whereas the running policy reaches states that its own errors produce. In addition, an image-plane waypoint exists only while the route lies inside the camera view. We present \textbf{\paper}, a monocular red-green-blue (RGB) policy with 2 billion (2B) parameters that addresses both limitations. \paper adapts Qwen3-VL-2B with low-rank adapters and processes an eight-frame memory and the current view in one forward pass. The policy predicts the route direction as a bearing in the body frame of the agent, which remains defined for every heading, and a deterministic controller converts this bearing into motion. This design allows training to relabel the states that the policy itself visits. On-policy correction improves success, while bearing decoding outperforms image-plane waypoint scanning across all evaluated start headings with identical weights. On the \valunseen split, \paper achieves \rFinSR\% success on Room-to-Room in Continuous Environments (\rtor), averaged over three seeds, and \rRxFinSR\% on its Room-Across-Room counterpart (\rxr). Inference requires \rDecMs\,ms and \rInferGB\,GiB of memory, while training takes \rCostTrainTyp\ GPU-hours on one 12\,GB consumer GPU.



### Score-based Black-box Attack via Adversarial Condition Guided Diffusion Model
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34564v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34564v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:33:29+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:33:29+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuhang Chen, Chih-Hong Cheng, Kurt Debattista, Siddartha Khastgir, Xingyu Zhao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generative models enable unrestricted adversarial attacks that aim to fool classifiers while preserving the natural appearance and semantics of the original image. However, existing diffusion-based black-box attacks largely rely on transferability from surrogates, making their effectiveness sensitive to differences between surrogate and victim. Improving transferability can also distort semantic content and degrade image quality. Moreover, retaining the original prompt to preserve fidelity leaves the diffusion model's conditional prior underused for adversarial guidance. We address these limitations by adapting score-based black-box attacks to reverse diffusion, using the victim's output probabilities to guide latent updates without accessing its gradients. We further introduce adaptive-target guidance, which uses the victim's current runner-up class as an adversarial condition to steer denoising towards the decision boundary. Because such guidance can introduce unwanted semantic changes, we also propose target-search guidance, which searches for a small perturbation of the original condition embedding using a score-based attack. This achieves comparable attack effectiveness with better image quality than runner-up guidance. Experiments on the ImageNet-Compatible dataset show that our framework outperforms transfer-based baselines in both attack effectiveness and image quality.



### Physics-Guided Generative Modelling of Oxy-Combustion Flame Images: From Conditional LSGANs to Denoising Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34568v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34568v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:35:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:35:33+00:00
- **Authors**: Imane Er-rabhi, Gorkem Oztarlik, Jean Caudal, Renaud Gaudron
- **Comment**: 9 pages, 4 figures. Accepted for the 12th International Conference on Combustion Science and Processes (CSP 2027)
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Validating industrial oxy-combustion burner designs traditionally requires costly experimental campaigns in which each operating condition is physically reproduced on an instrumented rig and imaged via OH* chemiluminescence, rendering iterative design exploration prohibitively slow. This work develops deep generative surrogate models that map a 15-dimensional vector of continuous operating parameters (thermal power, swirl numbers, momentum ratios, mixture fractions, etc.) directly to a $512\times64$ heat-release flame image, trained on 1,609 experimental samples from the OXYTEC dual-swirl non-premixed oxy-combustion database. Two complementary generative paradigms are investigated, as they offer opposite strengths for surrogate modelling: a custom spatially-anchored conditional least-squares GAN (cLSGAN-L1-CBN), whose single-pass generation enables near-instantaneous inference, and a custom physics-guided denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM), whose iterative sampling favours the smooth, continuous heat-release gradients of fluid flows. Both are validated through ablation on a stratified held-out test set. The optimized cLSGAN reaches RMSE 0.022 and FID 20.68 and generates an image in about 20 ms on a mobile workstation GPU, though extended training leaves its texture realism unchanged; the DDPM improves steadily over long training horizons, reaching RMSE 0.019 and FID 20.29 at 40,000 epochs, at the cost of roughly thirty seconds per image on the same hardware --- three orders of magnitude slower than the adversarial surrogate. A nearest-neighbour analysis traces the persistent worst-case errors to sparsely sampled regions of the parameter space, and a parameter-sensitivity study confirms smooth, physically consistent responses to perturbed inputs. These results demonstrate that carefully guided generative models can act as reliable visual surrogates for combustion experiments.



### Sage-Med: Scaling Medical Multimodal Intelligence
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34575v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34575v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:39:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:39:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Sage-Med Team,  :, Ming Hu, Cheng Tang, Wei Li, Rongbin Li, Feilong Tang, Junru Hu, Hongze Zhu, Qiujuan Yan, Huanze Tang, Shaocong Wei, Siyuan Yan, Min Cen, Zhongxing Xu, Zhonghua Wang, Lie Ju, Zhen Yu, Wenjie Li, Shiyan Su, Jing Hao, Jingyong Liang, Diping Song, Fang Yan, Chenglong Ma, Junzhi Ning, Haochen Xue, Zanting Ye, Chenrun Wang, Jinjie Wei, Yi Xin, Jianghan Shen, Siqi Luo, Jiyao Liu, Huidong Xie, Jun Zhao, Rulin Zhou, Longquan Jiang, Ziyan Huang, Ying Chen, Hanxiao Zhang, Junyang Wu, Mouxiao Bian, Jiashi Lin, Chenyu Wang, Qi Qin, Jie Huang, Yiling Zhou, Jiawei Chen, Kefan Wang, Jingjing Hua, Yaoyu Liu, Huihui Xu, Tianbin Li, Jin Ye, Ziyang Chen, Shujian Gao, Zilin Lu, Jiangtao Yan, Changkai Ji, Wenhao Tang, Xinyi Zeng, Yuxing Chen, Hongwei Yang, Rongzhao Zhang, Jinru Ding, Yingcheng Wu, Zhenfei Yin, Jinzhe Li, Lu Lu, Yun Zhong, Zhishan Lin, Bingli Wang, Xiaohan Ren, Jianmin Qian, Penghao Zhao, Zhongbo Tian, Zhenbang Zhou, Peiling Dai, Mengjing Zhao, Jingyu Zheng, Pinan Liu, Shiwei Li, Victoria Mar, Cristina Vico-Alonso, Qiang Wu, Xianming Liu, Lingtong Huang, Dili Song, Minghui Zhang, Wenlong Zhang, Qipeng Guo, Yuqiang Li, Nanqing Dong, Yun Gu, Qi Duan, Hongliang Ren, Hongming Shan, Yin Xie, Xiang An, Benyou Wang, Yueming Su, Yefeng Zheng, Zongyuan Ge, Chun-Mei Feng, Jie Xu, Yu Qiao, Junjun He, Yirong Chen
- **Comment**: 101 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Establishing effective scaling laws for medical multimodal large language models (MLLMs) remains a persistent and fundamental challenge. Our systematic evaluation shows that existing medical MLLMs tend to underperform frontier general-purpose MLLMs across a range of medical tasks, while scaling model size primarily improves text capabilities rather than multimodal perception, and enabling reasoning modes fails to close this visual gap. To investigate how medical multimodal intelligence scales in practice, we introduce Sage-Med-Preview, a 35B-parameter medical multimodal model with 3B activated parameters, built by combining stage-specific curation over 322.7 million candidate records with a progressive training curriculum spanning medical pretraining, staged supervised fine-tuning, and adaptive reinforcement learning. Across this curriculum, progressive supervised refinement and adaptive reinforcement learning yield consistent stage-wise gains, substantially advancing both specialized visual understanding and medical language capabilities. Evaluated against 77 competing models across 238 clinical tasks spanning 61 public and 13 private multi-center benchmarks in more than 40 modalities, Sage-Med-Preview achieves an average score of 58.26% on the 31 public multimodal benchmarks. It outperforms the state-of-the-art open-source medical model Hulu-Med-235B-A22B at 50.99% and the general scientific model Intern-S2-Preview at 52.24%, and approaches the advanced proprietary model GPT-6.2-Sol across multiple tasks such as comprehensive multimodal understanding, report generation, and visual grounding. Although substantial challenges remain in complex medical reasoning and multi-step clinical workflows, this report provides empirical evidence, actionable data and training recipes, and a rigorous benchmark toward scalable clinical multimodal intelligence.



### ChemoMAE: Self-Supervised Spectral Representation Learning Reveals Chemical Heterogeneity in Historical Wood
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34581v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34581v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:45:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:45:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Ryuji Yamaguchi, Miyuki Matsuo
- **Comment**: 51 pages, 18 figures (9 in the main text and 9 in the appendices)
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In historical timber buildings, multiple environmental factors act over centuries on wood whose chemical composition varies across anatomical regions, producing complex patterns of degradation. Understanding how timber members degrade is important for the effective conservation and restoration of historical buildings, yet the mechanisms underlying these patterns remain unclear. To investigate these mechanisms, we used self-supervised learning to establish a common basis for comparing specimens from timber members that differ in wood species and service history. We examined transverse sections of 49 wood specimens from several historical buildings in Japan using near-infrared hyperspectral imaging. ChemoMAE, a self-supervised Transformer, learns pixelwise spectral representations through masked denoising that exploits multicollinearity among spectral variables. Clustering these representations yields spatial maps without predefined chemical-state categories. Because anatomy and degradation can produce spatially continuous regions, we formulated Local Label Agreement (LLA) to quantify agreement among labels assigned to neighboring pixels. Using spectra alone, ChemoMAE yielded higher mean LLA than baseline methods on validation specimens. We then trained ChemoMAE anew and fitted cluster centers across all specimens. Spatial structures consistent with wood anatomy and the expected effects of surface exposure emerged in the resulting maps. By cross-referencing regional spectra and spatial distributions across specimens in light of wood anatomy, service histories, and prior studies, we proposed models of internal and surface degradation as mechanistic hypotheses. This exploratory AI for Science framework gives researchers a basis for linking latent patterns learned through self-supervision with domain knowledge when investigating how material states form.



### ADClaw: A Programmable Visual Agent for Industrial Anomaly Detection and Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34591v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34591v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:51:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:51:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Xinyi Wu, Rongxin Gao, YuHui Lin, Yonglin Hong, Chu Li, Wei Li, Dingzhao Li, Shijia Zhao, Chaopeng Li, Haixin Sun
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Industrial anomaly detection (IAD), the task of identifying and localizing deviations from normal product conditions, is essential to automated quality inspection in manufacturing. Many vision-language models for IAD rely on large domain-specific datasets for fine-tuning and make predictions in a single pass, leaving no opportunity to re-examine ambiguous regions. Although tool-using agents can revisit these regions, predefined tool interfaces limit how they can combine intermediate results in a follow-up check. When such checks are insufficient, models may miss subtle defects or misidentify the defect type. To address this limitation, we introduce ADClaw, an inspection agent that uses executable code to test anomaly hypotheses without task-specific fine-tuning. For each hypothesis, a Structured Normality Prior identifies the applicable rules and retrieves normal visual references for the inspection target. Guided by this prior, the agent writes and executes code in a persistent Inspection Workbench to combine masks, anomaly maps, and measurements from earlier steps into a new test of the suspected defect. After each check, Hypothesis-Guided Evidence Reasoning assesses whether the evidence supports the suspected defect and its location, using gaps or inconsistencies in that evidence to guide the next code action. ADClaw achieves competitive results in detection and localization across 2D and 3D industrial anomaly detection benchmarks and in anomaly understanding on MMAD.



### $μ$MPC: Microsecond-Scale Nonlinear and World Model Control from Edge to GPU via Koopman-Linearized Precompilation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34597v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV, cs.LG, eess.SY, math.OC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34597v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:56:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:56:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Devesh Nath, Glen Chou
- **Comment**: 9 pages, 4 figures. Project Website: https://www.deveshnath.com/projects/micrompc/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We present $μ$MPC, a fast model predictive control (MPC) framework that reduces online computation using learned Koopman-linearized dynamics. By precomputing finite-horizon control-to-output maps, $μ$MPC eliminates lifted-state trajectories, yielding an action-only Koopman-Compiled QP (KC-QP) with $Hn_u$ decision variables, independent of lifted dimension. A fixed, precomputed primal operator enables efficient primal-dual iterations using only matrix-vector products and projections, accommodating changing goals and constraints without refactorization. This efficiency also enables control-aware dynamics learning through differentiable KC-QPs in the training loop and batched DAgger-style data aggregation. $μ$MPC achieves solve times as low as $23μ$s on a desktop CPU and 24 ms onboard a 168 MHz Crazyflie microcontroller. It scales to 4096D quadruped control in under 5 ms, visual planning with 2048D world models in 0.4 ms, and collision-aware planning for 200 quadrotors at over 30 Hz on the GPU. Across these benchmarks, $μ$MPC achieves up to $108\times$ faster constrained MPC and $1907\times$ faster visuomotor planning than competing methods.



### SEAM: A Self-Evolving and Adaptive Probing Framework for Automatic MLLM Hallucination Evaluation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34598v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34598v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:57:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:57:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Shiyu Zhang, Zengyi Wo, Yuming Liao, Ran He, Caifeng Shan, Yanbo Fan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The severe susceptibility of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to hallucinations poses a critical threat to their reliability, making a rigorous evaluation framework indispensable. However, existing evaluation pipelines largely rely on predefined test items, suffering from high construction costs, rapid obsolescence as MLLMs evolve, and ineffectiveness in probing model-specific weaknesses. To address these limitations, we propose SEAM, a self-evolving and adaptive probing framework for automatic MLLM hallucination evaluation. Specifically, SEAM introduces a composable Hallucination Probe Construction Library comprising ten hallucination-probing skills, three elicitation wrappers, and five question formats. Building on this library, SEAM establishes a dual-loop framework. In the inner loop (adaptive probing), skill-guided visual analysis, question composition, and hierarchical quality verification are coordinated with verifier and target-response feedback to refine test items and uncover model-specific hallucinations. In the outer loop (skill evolution), a skill updater distills historical probing trajectories into localized, reusable rule updates, which undergo held-out validation and are frozen before testing. Driven by these innovative structural designs, SEAM establishes an automated and self-evolving evaluation paradigm. Experiments across four target MLLMs and ten hallucination types demonstrate that SEAM substantially outperforms representative static benchmarks, achieving an 89.2\% relative improvement in mean valid hallucination discovery rate across 12 matched comparisons.



### You Only Look Multiple Times: YoloMT, a Real-Time Streaming Model on a T4
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34601v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34601v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:57:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:57:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Nessim Ben Abbes, Duc Han Le, Sabri Mtibaa, Van-Tam Nguyen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We introduce a new model for real-time streaming captioning on a single GPU. YoloMT is a 449.8M-parameter streaming captioner that provides Pareto-frontier performance among generative vision models in its class. Distilled from a 27B teacher by SFT and GRPO, it leads every model under 1B parameters on OVO-Bench, MVBench, StreamingBench and Video-MME, answered from its captions, and beats LFM2.5-VL-1.6B and SmolVLM2-2.2B. YoloMT is designed to read more frames cheaply and make them informative, encoding each frame once and caching it as 16 tokens. This allows the model to incorporate and infer from past frames without additional vision passes, making it efficient and effective. We also provide a detailed recipe for training YoloMT, with its stages and data, as well as ablations that demonstrate the contribution of each component.



### VisualMontage: Manipulating the Beliefs of Vision-Language Agents with Authentic Image Sequences
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34603v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.MA, cs.SI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34603v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:57:55+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:57:55+00:00
- **Authors**: Jinwei Hu, Lai Wei, Zhuoyun Li, Youcheng Sun, Yi Dong, Xiaowei Huang
- **Comment**: Under Review
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: As agents based on vision-language models (VLMs) increasingly interpret social-media content, errors in their analysis may lend credibility to rumors and amplify their spread. In this paper, we study the problem of visual cognitive collusion, in which coordinated posts combine authentic images and locally faithful captions to induce VLM agents to infer and accept attacker-chosen target claims through their own reasoning. To enable systematic evaluation of this cognitive threat, we introduce a new benchmark comprising 544 target cases and 33.2K candidate images across eight application domains. We further propose VisualMontage, a multi-agent framework for simulating such attacks through narrative planning, image selection and ordering, and caption assignment under iterative review. We evaluate this threat through extensive experiments across 12 vision-language models, examining modality effects, production-team components, and downstream defenses. Using identical attack feeds across models without victim-specific optimization, we demonstrate cross-model transferability, achieving mean attack success rates of 61.93% for proprietary models and 68.71% for open-weight models. Moreover, 92.65% of generated feeds pass independent model-based validity auditing. Even after downstream review, residual attack success rates range from 35.3% to 76.7% across the evaluated configurations. Our additional controlled comparisons further reveal that captions can improve model-rated posting naturalness while reducing attack success. Together, these findings reveal a novel cognitive threat in social-media interpretation and motivate safeguards that assess how authentic posts jointly shape agents' reasoning and conclusions.



### Blind spatially varying PSF estimation for fluorescence microscopy
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34607v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **physics.optics**, cs.CE, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34607v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 15:59:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 15:59:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Minh Hai Nguyen, Florian Sarron, Paul Escande, Sylvain Cantaloube, Thomas Mangeat, Pierre Weiss
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Optical aberrations degrade fluorescence microscopy images and measuring the underlying point spread function (PSF) often requires dedicated acquisitions or isolated emitters. Here we introduce DeepBlur, a neural network that estimates spatially varying two-dimensional PSFs directly from single images of biological structures. Trained entirely on physical simulations spanning aberrations, illumination and noise, it adapts to diverse imaging conditions without requiring user-supplied optical parameters. Using a deformable mirror, we acquire 1,290 images and assess blind PSF estimates against independent references. DeepBlur estimates agree closely with these references and improve deconvolution of experimentally aberrated images. Local PSF estimation also enables correction of spatially varying defocus and improves random-illumination super-resolution reconstruction in biological tissue. Available through DeepInv, a napari plugin and a web demonstration, DeepBlur can be readily integrated into computational microscopy workflows.



### DuCo: Exploiting Dual Cross-Layer Consistency for Sparse Geometric Attention

- **DOI**: None

- **Authors**: Liangyu Xiang, Junyu Gao, Xiaofeng Wang, Changsheng Xu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Dense global attention is crucial for capturing cross-view geometry in visual geometry foundation models, yet its quadratic complexity limits efficient inference on long image sequences. Existing sparse attention methods primarily select entries of the global attention matrix based on attention probabilities and construct masks independently across layers, overlooking value redundancy and cross-layer information reuse. By analyzing attention distributions and clustering value representations across layers, we reveal two forms of \emph{cross-layer consistency}, characterized by highly similar attention probability distributions and localized changes in value redundancy structures. Building on these observations, we propose \textbf{DuCo}, a training-free sparse attention framework that exploits historical attention information and value redundancy for efficient geometric inference. Specifically, DuCo leverages historical attention information to estimate current-layer dense attention probabilities and introduces value clustering to exploit redundancy, with clusters efficiently refined through local updates across layers. Both factors are integrated through probability-weighted value distortion to jointly optimize four cluster representation states under an attention budget for sparse mask construction. Experiments across three visual geometry foundation models and five benchmarks demonstrate favorable accuracy--efficiency trade-offs.



### Commit2Drive: Perception-Grounded Language Decisions as Commitments for Trajectory Planning in Vision-Language-Action Driving Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34614v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34614v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 16:01:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:01:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Zikai Zhang, Hubert P. Shum, Toby Breckon
- **Comment**: 28 pages, 9 figures, 19 tables. Includes supplementary material. Code will be released soon
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models introduce language reasoning into end-to-end autonomous driving. They output a language decision together with a trajectory, which makes the planner more interpretable. However, in existing methods the language decision is either an independent output or an index that selects among similar candidate trajectories, and its annotations are not verified against the trajectory. As a result, the language decision and the trajectory are often inconsistent in closed-loop driving. To address this, we propose Commit2Drive, a framework that imposes the language decision as a consistency constraint on trajectory planning. The decision is a commitment, a maneuver class with a continuous parameter that one deterministic rule reads from any trajectory, and it is grounded in facts reported by the perception of the model itself. A Maneuver Bank maps the commitment to a code with a kinematic meaning, and consistency losses train the planner to execute every admissible decision of a scene, so that the trajectory follows the decision. An automatic data pipeline supplies the supervision. Decisions are labeled from the future motion of the expert, counterfactual executions train the decision and the trajectory together, and rule-based functions with a Vision-Language Model (VLM) generate, review and refine the language annotations. The code is a lookup that involves no hidden state of the language model, so Commit2Drive is extensible to rule-based decisions, which the same planner executes without retraining. Commit2Drive uses a 0.5B language model and runs closed-loop inference online on a single consumer graphics card. On Bench2Drive, Commit2Drive reaches a Driving Score (DS) of 78.50 and a Success Rate (SR) of 55.91% with a lightweight model, which is competitive with the state of the art, and greatly reduces the language-action inconsistency. Code will be released soon.



### Beyond Pose Control: Anatomically Consistent Ultrasound Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34622v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34622v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 16:08:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:08:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Yue Zhou, Fangxu Han, Tony Danjun Wang, Nassir Navab, Yuan Bi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Camera-controlled video generation anticipates how a scene appears from new viewpoints by relying on a prior learned implicitly from large-scale data. Ultrasound poses the same problem, with the probe pose as the viewpoint, but under opposite conditions: anatomy offers an explicit prior that every sonographer relies on, whereas tracked scans are too scarce to learn it implicitly. As a result, pose control alone is not enough, and pose-conditioned ultrasound generators produce implausible anatomy in unscanned regions and inconsistent anatomy on revisits. We go beyond pose control and give the generator the two kinds of knowledge that make up a sonographer's mental model. An anatomical prior, built from a population of subjects, guides generation where nothing has been scanned, and is refined online by every generated chunk towards the scanned subject. A 3D latent memory, designed around the cross-sectional physics of ultrasound, compounds the generated slices into a volume of the patient and reslices it at new poses, keeping revisited anatomy consistent. The fine-grained memory enters the generator at the input, while the coarse anatomy prior guides the layout across transformer blocks. Experiments on thyroid, kidney, and lumbar spine show improved visual fidelity, pose following, and anatomical consistency over pose-conditioned and memory-augmented baselines.



### Carrying Feature Ablations Across Sparse Autoencoder Seeds
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34637v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34637v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 16:20:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:20:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Karl Li
- **Comment**: 44 pages, 4 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A vision encoder turns an image into one vector per patch, refined layer by layer. A sparse autoencoder (SAE) writes each vector of one layer as a sum of learned directions, most with a coefficient of zero. Researchers call each direction a feature and edit the encoder by removing one feature. An SAE trained from another random seed learns different features. Earlier work matches the two SAEs' features one to one by how closely their directions agree. Matched features can point alike yet respond to different image parts, so removing them may change the encoder differently. We remove a feature and, separately, its match, then compare the changes in the encoder's output on the same held out images. When the second change nearly reproduces the first, we say the removal carries across seeds. A setting fixes a DINOv2 or SigLIP layer and its SAE feature count. At our tolerance, the removal carries for 12% to 70% of matched pairs across three settings. The share is 0% in two controls: an SAE whose directions stay random and, post hoc, matches shuffled among trained features. Post hoc, predicting which removals carry is more accurate with layer contributions than with directions. Some incorrect predictions remain. One persists on independent images. So pairs can be screened at the layer. Measuring the output detects errors this screen misses.



### PRAM: Activating Latent Multimodal Knowledge through Partwise Reasoning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34640v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34640v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 16:21:29+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:21:29+00:00
- **Authors**: Haidong Chen, Ying Guo, Shangze Li, Zhongkuan Mao, Linrui Xu, Canran Xiao, Fei Shen, Tat-Seng Chua
- **Comment**: 36 pages, 8 figures. Project page: https://luo1yan.github.io/PRAM/ ; Code: https://github.com/Luo1Yan/PRAM ; Features: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Rowance/PRAM-features
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Emergent cross-modal classification and retrieval enable transfer between modalities without direct paired supervision. However, existing methods often prioritize sample-level alignment, overlooking local discriminative information and partwise statistical relations. To this end, we propose partwise reasoning across modalities (PRAM), which activates latent multimodal knowledge in frozen encoders by composing partwise statistical relations through a shared pivot modality. Specifically, we first present a multi-granularity covariance regularization module that estimates covariances from global and local vectors in two collections of modality-pivot pairs, stabilizing local estimates through adaptive blending with their cross-part means. Second, we devise a pivot-based spectral relational reasoning module that infers global and partwise statistical relations between target modalities through regularized covariance composition and extracts paired projection directions and their correlation strengths via whitening and spectral decomposition. Finally, we introduce a multi-granularity score fusion module that standardizes and fuses matching scores from global and local relations, incorporating task-specific information on pivot associations and class semantics for class prediction and candidate ranking. Extensive experiments across ten datasets demonstrate that PRAM achieves state-of-the-art performance in 16 classification settings and 19 retrieval settings, while exhibiting highly competitive performance in three sensing scenarios.



### Motion Beyond Pixels: Learning Future Visual Representations for Object-Centric Dynamics
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34644v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34644v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 16:24:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:24:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Chengzhi Zhao, Juelin Li, Zhennan Wang, Sheng Wang, Zhuoman Liu, Yuhao Lu, Jiongxu Chen, Haitang Feng, Yanjie Li, Lin Shao
- **Comment**: Project page: https://xyz1206.github.io/MotionRep/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Many embodied world models predict future states in latent spaces optimized for RGB reconstruction, which do not explicitly prioritize object-level semantics and geometry relevant to motion generation. We present MotionRep, a visual representation world model that jointly generates future DINO representations and 3D object flow, coupling semantic and geometric information with object-centric dynamics. To make high-dimensional DINO features amenable to generative modeling, we introduce DINO-VAE, which combines temporal and channel compression with latent regularization to produce compact latents while preserving semantic and geometric information. Conditioned on an initial observation, object query points, and a language instruction, a unified diffusion transformer predicts future visual representations and 3D object flow through joint attention. MotionRep employs asymmetric modality-specific noise schedules to keep the visual stream ahead of 3D object flow during joint denoising, allowing progressively refined future visual representations to guide flow generation. Experiments on simulated and real-world manipulation benchmarks demonstrate that MotionRep outperforms pixel-based baselines in 3D object flow accuracy and generalization to unseen scenes, while requiring only three denoising steps for inference. These results support predictive visual representations as an effective future-state space for object-centric dynamics modeling.



### A Hard Background Pairing Transfers across Classifiers and Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34645v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34645v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 16:24:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:24:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Karl Li
- **Comment**: 41 pages, 5 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: An image classifier names the object in an image, and its accuracy is the share of images named correctly. A classifier that names a car on a road may miss the same car pasted onto clouds. Composite benchmarks measure such failures by pasting objects cut from photographs onto backgrounds from other photographs. A pairing puts every object label on a different background label, such as truck on sea. The pairing on which some classifiers do worst, their hardest pairing, makes a strict test, but may be hard only for them or only on the photographs used to choose it. We show that the hardest pairing of some classifiers stays hard for others and on new photographs. We split 40 ImageNet classifiers into halves balanced by reported ImageNet accuracy. One half picks its hardest pairing of 8 object and 8 background labels from Common Objects in Context (COCO). The other half, held out of the choice, compares that pairing with random pairings of the same objects and backgrounds. For the 14 held-out classifiers with little net accuracy change after pasting objects back onto their own backgrounds, mean accuracy on composites from new photographs falls from 50.3% over random pairings to 43.7% on the hardest pairing, 6.6 percentage points lower. A benchmark using these composites can compare this pool's held-out classifiers on the fixed and random pairings.



### Atunda: Building an Afrogenic Dance Movement Dataset for AI-Driven Classification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34649v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CY, cs.DB
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34649v1)

- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:25:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Diego Carrascosa, Tristan Pank, Qudus Onikeku, Mas'ud Onikeku, Jeremy A Frusco, Osubi Craig, Angelos Barmpoutis
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: This paper introduces Atunda, the first systematically collected video dataset of Afrogenic dance moves, designed for training and evaluating deep learning models for movement classification. The first phase of the dataset includes nearly 400 videos covering 13 distinct dance moves from African and African Diaspora performative culture, making it the first AI-ready resource of its kind. Using this dataset, we train and evaluate various configurations of temporal convolutional neural networks, achieving a classification accuracy exceeding 95%. The baseline results provide a benchmark for future studies in movement classification, especially in the understudied area of Afrogenic performative culture and tradition. This work lays the foundation for developing in the future digital metrics of similarity, automated attribution systems, and tools that preserve and promote the cultural heritage of Afrogenic dances.



### Counting Beyond Frames: Codec Motion Tells Frozen Diffusion VLMs How Many
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34657v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.34657v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-25 16:30:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-25 16:30:09+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhuoqin Li, Jiayu Ding, Ge Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
